# Mectov OS

[![CI Boot Test](https://github.com/MAliffadlan/Mectov_OS/actions/workflows/build-boot-test.yml/badge.svg)](https://github.com/MAliffadlan/Mectov_OS/actions/workflows/build-boot-test.yml)

A monolithic x86 kernel written from scratch in C and Assembly — no external libraries, no libc, no POSIX. Every byte runs directly on hardware: GRUB Multiboot boot, protected mode with paging, a graphical desktop with a floating-window manager, a real Unix process model, and an SMP scheduler that runs user tasks on all four CPU cores.

## Overview

Mectov OS is a from-scratch i386 operating system with:

- **Quake III Arena, playable end to end (v38.111–v38.157)** — `./quake.sh` builds the `MECTOV_Q3=1` ISO, stages **your own** pak0 onto the ext2 volume and boots it with KVM; type `q3arena q3dm1` in the Terminal and you are in id's own engine core, running id's own `qagame` bytecode in id's own VM, drawing the map through this kernel's software rasterizer into a window on this desktop — WASD, live mouse look, and a status bar drawn from your pak's own `gfx/2d` pictures out of the module's own `playerState`. The player's own session (4 Okt): ~1070 frames of q3dm1 at a stable 20 fps, 26 frame-time windows all at `slack=0ms`, `look_s` 25–68/s, zero panics. Rendering is this port's own **320×240** rasterizer — a standing rule, never touched.
- **Ring 0 / Ring 3 isolation** — user applications run in separate address spaces with COW fork, demand-paged `mmap()`, and per-process page directories — on **PAE 3-level paging with NX (W^X)** since v38.49: user heap/stack/mmap pages are non-executable, and an instruction-fetch fault on data kills the task with SIGSEGV (`nxtest` proves it in CI).
- **True SMP** — per-CPU runqueues, work-stealing migration, and a LAPIC timer per core; all four QEMU cores execute tasks (verified live by SysInfo's per-core load bars).
- **A Unix process model** — `fork` / `exec` / `waitpid`, POSIX signals (`sigaction`, `sa_mask`, `SA_RESTART`), process groups, sessions and a controlling terminal with SIGTTIN/SIGTTOU, plus System V shared memory, pipes, and fds.
- **Ownership, permissions & real threads (v38.23–v38.24, v38.27)** — every file carries a POSIX uid/gid + 9-bit mode enforced at the syscall layer (`chmod`/`chown`/`ls -l`, root bypass, ext2-persisted); every task has a private `%gs` TLS descriptor with `clone()` + a pthread-style runtime, so thread-local variables are genuinely per-thread; futex-based `mct_mutex_t`/`mct_cond_t` (pthread-style) are proven on 4 cores by `conddemo`; and **POSIX resource limits (v38.28)** — `RLIMIT_NPROC`/`RLIMIT_AS`/`RLIMIT_NOFILE` per task with soft/hard values, POSIX `getrlimit`/`setrlimit` privilege rules, and enforcement at fork/clone/thread-create, fd allocation, and heap/mmap growth, so a fork bomb or an fd/memory leak can no longer take the whole system down.
- **A real shell (msh)** — env vars with `$VAR` expansion, aliases, tab completion, scripts, job control, and a growing POSIX toolkit (`uname`, `whoami`, `hostname`, `env`, `seq`, `head`, `grep`, `cp`, `mv`, `df`, ...).
- **A graphical desktop** — double-buffered window manager (resize, Aero snap, rounded corners), taskbar with system tray and clickable window buttons (minimize/restore, hover-highlighted, v38.39), a Start menu with search-as-you-type filtering (v38.40), draggable persistent icons with gradient tiles, soft shadows and label chips (v38.46), and a graphical login screen.
- **Eager FPU/SSE context switching (v38.41)** — every task carries a 512-byte `fxsave` image the scheduler swaps on every context switch, so DOOM's x87 and any Ring 3 float/SSE code can be preempted against each other safely (proven by `fputest`: two forked processes accumulating in live x87/SSE registers).
- **Runtime mounts & POSIX sockets (v38.42–v38.43)** — a real mount table with `mount`/`umount` (syscalls + shell builtins, root-only), and an fd-integrated socket API (`socket`/`bind`/`listen`/`accept`/`connect`/`sendto`/`recvfrom`) where accept spawns per-connection slots POSIX-style and read/write/poll/select work on socket fds uniformly.
- **Real ACPI poweroff (v38.45)** — the FADT is parsed for the PM1a/b control blocks and the DSDT `\_S5` package, so `shutdown` writes the firmware's own sleeping type + SLP_EN; QEMU exits by itself (`poweroff_test` asserts it).
- **On-disk persistence** — an ATA-backed VFS, a fully writable ext2 partition (verified with host `fsck.ext2`), a FAT32 volume at `/fat32` (drive 3, LFN read/write), an **AHCI/SATA driver (v38.50)** that registers SATA disks as drives 4+ and an **xHCI/USB 3.0 driver (v38.56)** that registers SuperSpeed sticks as drives 8+ on the same sector API — ext2/FAT32 and runtime `mount()` work on SATA/USB volumes unchanged — and a **virtio-blk driver (v38.78)** for KVM speed (transitional legacy PCI, poll-only) that registers disks as drives 12+ on that same API.
- **Networking** — RTL8139 driver with IRQ-driven RX, 16-connection TCP with a listener/backlog accept model, UDP, ARP/ICMP/DNS, and a browser that reaches the real internet through a host gateway proxy.
- **Audio** — Sound Blaster 16 driver with WAV playback and PC-speaker tones.
- **Developer tooling** — in-kernel GDB stub (attach over TCP, breakpoints, single-step), ELF32 + custom `.mct` loaders, and a shared-library runtime that keeps app binaries under 2 KB.

---

## Highlights

**Quake III Arena, end to end (v38.111–v38.157)** — the game is not a screenshot on this page: it is playable.
- **What actually runs.** `q3arena` links id's own engine core (`third_party/q3a`) into this kernel and drives it from a dedicated task; the official `qagame.qvm` — real Quake III Arena bytecode built from id's source by id's own `lcc`/`q3asm` — is executed by id's own VM loader and interpreter, and the level is drawn by this kernel's own software rasterizer (TinyGL) into a normal WM window on the desktop. No id data is redistributed: the map, textures, shaders and status-bar art come from **your** pak0.
- **The whole path is one script.** `./quake.sh [map]` builds the `MECTOV_Q3=1` ISO, stages your pak's map closure (plus `--with-viewmodel`, the machinegun) onto `/ext2/baseq3`, boots QEMU with KVM when it is available, and gets the Terminal open. Then `q3arena q3dm1` in the Terminal (add `nolimit` for no frame cap); WASD moves, the mouse looks, ESC returns to the desktop. `scripts/q3a_data.py --list` prints the staging plan and `--verify` reports any shader that resolved to no file, so a partial extraction says so instead of looking finished.
- **Proven by the player's own session, not a test rig** (4 Okt, `serial_debug.log`, ~1070 frames of q3dm1 ≈ 53 s): zero panics, zero watchdog resets, `fps=20` steady, all 26 `[Q3HIT]` frame-time windows at `slack=0ms` (v38.156 read ~22 ms in every one), mouse packets and turns both flowing (`pkts_s` 75–344, `look_s` 25–68/s). The reports of v38.149–v38.156 ("patah-patah", "16 fps", mouse lag) are closed by the ACPI/SMP fix that actually brings the APs online and the asynchronous serial ring — see the v38.157 row for the measurements.
- **Stated limits, not implied by a screenshot.** Rendering is this port's own 320×240 rasterizer (the standing rule; untouched by every release from v38.99 on). The status bar is id's 2D `CG_DrawStatusBar` — no player head model, no 3D ammo/armor models, no team background. The sound bridge was retired in v38.142, so the game runs **silent**. About 13–16 ms/frame is still spent in the present/composite path; that is the next lever and it is written down in the v38.157 row with its numbers.

**FPU context switching, runtime mounts, POSIX sockets & ACPI S5 (v38.41–v38.45)**
- **Eager fxsave/fxrstor (v38.41)**: `task_t` grew a 16-byte-aligned 512-byte fxsave image; `schedule()` swaps it with IF=0 under `task_lock` (SMP-safe, no per-CPU owner tracking). Fresh tasks are seeded from a canonical `fninit` template, `fork()` inherits the parent's live state, `exec()` resets it (POSIX). CR4.OSFXSR/OSXMMEXCPT are enabled per-CPU (BSP + every AP); #NM is a logged safety net and #XM delivers `SIGFPE` to Ring 3. `apps/fputest.c` + `scripts/fputest.py` prove two forked processes accumulating in live x87-stack and SSE registers across preemption both stay exact — the exact scenario the old "single FPU owner" design could not survive.
- **Runtime mount table (v38.42)**: `SYS_MOUNT`/`SYS_UMOUNT` (root-only) + `mount`/`umount` shell builtins over a mount-point table; the boot `/ext2` and `/fat32` mounts are its first two entries, `umount` drops the subtree and restores a plain directory, `mount` re-inits the backend and re-populates from the real disk. `scripts/mount_test.py` does a full umount → refuse-non-mount → remount → fat32demo-PASSES roundtrip.
- **POSIX socket API (v38.43)**: `socket`/`bind`/`listen`/`accept`/`connect`/`sendto`/`recvfrom` (syscalls 108–114) return ordinary fds — `read`/`write`/`close`/`poll`/`select` are socket-aware. The listener model is POSIX-shaped: a LISTEN slot never mutates; each inbound SYN spawns a fresh child slot (free slots = backlog) and `accept()` hands completed children out one by one. `socktest.py` verifies a guest→host client echo (10.0.2.2) AND a host→guest server accept (hostfwd) end to end with PING/PONG.
- **Capacity bumps (v38.44)**: TCP connections 8→16, global fd table 128→256 (socket-heavy servers), windows 16→32. `MAX_TASKS` stays 64 (GDT TLS coupling). `MAX_NODES` was 256 at v38.44 and is **2048** today — the on-disk node table was widened twice since (256→1024 at layout v4/v38.111, 1024→2048 at layout v5/v38.141, 1 MB on disk); both caps are documented in code (`src/sys/task.c`, `src/include/vfs.h`).
- **ACPI S5 (v38.45)**: `acpi_init` now records the FADT in addition to the MADT (both XSDT and RSDT paths scan every entry), parses the DSDT `\_S5` package for the real SLP_TYP values, and `shutdown` writes PM1a/PM1b with SLP_EN before falling back to the legacy hardcoded ports. `poweroff_test.py` asserts the QEMU process exits by itself.

**Scheduling & SMP (v36.7–v36.8)**
- Per-CPU runqueues: each core picks with priority + aging, steals work from idle peers (migration), and parks in a pinned Ring 0 idle task.
- LAPIC timer per Application Processor, PIT-calibrated once on the BSP (concurrent calibration corrupted the shared PIT and caused timer storms).
- Every runqueue mutation under one `task_lock`, cli-first — the timer IRQ can never self-deadlock in `schedule()`.
- Per-CPU load sampling exposed to Ring 3: SysInfo renders four live utilization bars; the serial log prints `[LOAD] c0=.. c1=..` every 3 s.
- `smpstress` forks 8 children (CPU burn + sleep + pipes + self-signals) in two parallel waves across all four cores — all 16 exit and are reaped.

**Process model & signals (v36.1–v36.6, v38.5–v38.7)**
- COW `fork()` with deterministic syscall-frame copying (the KVM fork-GPF fix), in-place `exec()` via live-frame patching, blocking `waitpid()` with WNOHANG.
- POSIX signals with `SIGACTION`/`SIGPROCMASK`, handler sigframes on the user stack, `SA_RESTART` re-parking interrupted sleeps — and unresolvable Ring 3 faults deliver `SIGSEGV` to user handlers instead of killing the task.
- Process groups + sessions + controlling terminal: `setpgid`, `setsid`, `tcsetpgrp`; background readers get stopped by SIGTTIN; Ctrl+C/Ctrl+Z target the foreground group.
- Real pipelines and redirection: `run A | run B`, `run A > file`, `run A < file` through dup2 + a per-task fd table.
- Job control: Ctrl+Z stops, `jobs` lists, `bg` resumes, `fg` waits, `kill %1` terminates.
- Demand paging for heap and stack: a shared lazy zero page + COW backs untouched pages, and an unmapped guard page catches stack overflow cleanly.

**Shell & toolkit (v36.9, v37.x)**
- POSIX-style builtins across three toolkit rounds: `uname`, `whoami`, `hostname`, `env`, `seq`, `head`, `wc`, `type`, `cat -n`, `yes`, `printf`, `sort`, `uniq`, `tee`, `find` — all pipe-aware.
- Shell scripting: `for VAR in list`, `while true/false`, `$VAR` expansion, `break`, 4-level nesting; env vars + aliases + tab completion.
- `passwd` stores the login password in `/etc/passwd` as `<salt_hex>:<sha256>` since v38.52 (single source, no hardcoded default, no plaintext on disk); a single `OS_VERSION` constant keeps the help banner, `mfetch`, `uname` and login footer in sync.

**Memory & kernel hardening (v38.3–v38.8)**
- Frame-bitmap physical allocator sized from the multiboot memory map (RAM above 128 MB usable; identity map to 512 MB), per-process address spaces, COW with frame refcounting, demand-paged `mmap()` (`0x40000000..0x80000000`), System V shared memory.
- File-backed `mmap()` (`SYS_MMAP_FILE` + `SYS_MSYNC`): pages fault in from disk on demand, dirty pages write back on msync/munmap, `fork()` shares them MAP_SHARED-style.
- 16 KB kernel stacks with a 4 KB guard page below each; vector 8 is a hardware task gate so an overflow prints `[PANIC] DOUBLE FAULT` instead of triple-faulting into a silent reboot.
- Lock audit: per-structure irqsave spinlocks replaced global `cli()` — syscalls are preemptible and SMP parallelizes; heap gets magic + canary + redzone, clean panic on double-free, OOM returns NULL.
- `dmesg` — a 64 KB in-memory kernel log ring (crash lines included) exposed via `dmesg` and `/proc/dmesg`.

**Filesystems & storage (v37.0, v38.12, v38.15–v38.16, v38.23, v38.25)**
- Persistent VFS with auto-save; a fully writable ext2 partition (host-verified `fsck.ext2` clean); a **FAT32 volume at `/fat32` (drive 3) with long file names (LFN) read AND written**, interoperable with host `mkfs.fat`/mtools both ways.
- **Bus-mastering IDE DMA (v38.26)**: the PIIX3 IDE controller's BMIDE engine moves the data itself through a Physical Region Descriptor table — the CPU programs the PRDT + command and waits, no port I/O in the data path at all. Under KVM a 160 KB uncached read dropped **~42 ms → ~1.3 ms (~31×)**, and ~510× vs the original per-sector PIO (~680 ms); `scripts/bigread_test.py` now asserts DMA was engaged and no transfer failed. (v38.25 added multi-sector PIO batches + `rep insw`/`rep outsw` as the no-DMA fallback.)
- POSIX file positioning: `SYS_LSEEK` (`SEEK_SET/CUR/END`), `SYS_FSTAT`, honored `O_APPEND`; fd reads advance a real offset.
- **Ownership & permissions (v38.23)**: every VFS node carries a POSIX uid/gid + 9-bit mode, enforced at open/read/write/create/delete/exec in the syscall layer — root (uid 0) bypasses, `chmod` needs ownership, `chown` is root-only, and chmod while a descriptor is open takes effect on the *next* read/write. `SYS_CHMOD`/`SYS_CHOWN` (+ shell `chmod`/`chown` builtins and `ls -l`), persisted to ext2 inodes so permissions survive the image. FAT32//proc//dev keep no permission concept.
- Virtual `/proc` generated live from kernel state: `/proc/tasks`, `/proc/meminfo`, `/proc/cpuinfo`, `/proc/uptime`, `/proc/version`, `/proc/dmesg`.

**Network (v35.5, v38.11, v38.43)**
- 16-slot multi-connection TCP with seq/ack, retransmit/timeout sweeps, a full FIN handshake, and a listener/backlog `accept()` model (the listener never becomes the connection — each inbound SYN gets its own slot); UDP bind/send/recv (fd-integrated datagram sockets, single global binding); DNS via the QEMU gateway.
- **DHCP client (RFC 2131)** configures IP/netmask/gateway/DNS at runtime over UDP broadcast, with static fallback (`ipconfig` prints the result); RTL8139 IRQ-driven RX (spinlock-guarded against the SMP TX race).

**GUI & input (v37.x–v38.x)**
- Double-buffered WM: dirty-region tracking, Aero snap, 8-directional resize, traffic-light controls, scroll-wheel events (IntelliMouse), 32-window capacity (v38.44), shared `ps2_drain()` arbitration so keyboard and mouse never steal each other's bytes.
- Instrument-console identity: a Windows-style lock screen → password login, live RTC clock. The whole chrome is **full black** (v38.37) — taskbar with neutral bevels (no warm/orange tint), a plain centered **"START"** label instead of the amber logo square, and **window chrome** (titlebars + borders) pure black with off-white title text and window-control glyphs (no amber hairline around focused windows); amber survives only as selection feedback inside the Start menu/calendar popups.
- **Login gate (v38.30–v38.33)**: full-bleed scaled wallpaper (never cropped), digital clock + full date (day, date, month, year) pinned bottom-left, SPACE-only password entry, and a 4-second idle fallback back to the clock view. The lock screen is deliberately bare — no branding, no hint, no version footer (v38.33). While the password panel is open (v38.31) the wallpaper **blurs** and every caption **disappears**, so the panel is the only thing in focus. Every switch between the two screens **cross-fades** (v38.32): the blur eases in/out, the captions fade, and the panel fades — one lerp pass animates all of it, in both directions.
- **Lock screen feature (v38.34)**: the login gate is no longer only a boot/logout screen — a **Lock** item in the Start menu, the **Ctrl+Alt+L** shortcut, and the `lock` shell builtin all run the gate **without resetting the session**: every window and task stays alive behind the lock (the old text-mode `lock` stub printed "System Locked" and verified nothing; it's now a flag the kernel main loop polls, `pending_lock`). Unlocking with the password restores the desktop via `full_redraw()` — verified pixel-identical, terminal window included. **Idle auto-lock (v38.51)**: `locktimeout <seconds>` arms a countdown that locks the same way once keyboard+mouse have been quiet that long (`0` disables); the countdown restarts on any input and is re-anchored after unlock so the desktop never re-locks immediately.
- **Kernel CSPRNG + hardened passwords (v38.52)**: a ChaCha8 DRBG (`src/sys/entropy.c`) is seeded at boot and fed continuously from the timer IRQ + every keyboard/mouse byte; `/dev/random` and TCP ISNs now draw from it, a new `SYS_GETRANDOM` syscall (117) exposes it to Ring 3, and `passwd` writes a salted SHA-256 hash (`salt:hash`) instead of plaintext — legacy plaintext files still verify until the next change.
- **Ring 3 scanout takeover — the display-server foundation (v38.53)**: `SYS_FB_MAP` (118) maps the VBE linear framebuffer read/write into a Ring 3 task as `PAGE_DEV` PTEs (never COW'd on fork, never refcounted or freed by the frame allocator, not inherited by children), authorized logind-style — uid 0 or the controlling terminal's foreground process group; while the holder lives, **the kernel desktop stops presenting frames entirely** and keystrokes pump to it through the same queue `SYS_GET_KEY` pops (`SYS_FB_RELEASE` (119), exit or exec hands the pixels back and the desktop repaints). The clone/free page-table walkers skip `PAGE_DEV` entries — closing a latent unbounded-refcount OOB on MMIO addresses fork would have hit. `apps/fbmap.mct` animates straight to the scanout, then chases the mouse until any key releases; `scripts/fbmap_test.py` proves both the clean-release roundtrip AND a Ctrl+C kill mid-takeover with the desktop restored.
- Smooth compositing: delta-copy VRAM presentation (~10× less MMIO traffic), truthful FPS HUD, tick-scaled 60 FPS cap — <1 ms/frame on KVM.

**App runtime & tooling (v38.3, v38.13, v38.24, v38.27)**
- `.mct` binary format + standard ELF32 loader + shared-library runtime (app binaries ~2 KB); the first **Rust** Ring 3 app (`apps/rusthello.rs`, `no_std`, same MCT1 format) proves the loader is language-agnostic.
- **Real per-thread TLS + `clone()` (v38.24)**: every task slot owns a private FS/GS GDT descriptor (DPL=3) whose base is the thread's control block, saved/restored with the context-switch frame in all three interrupt stubs — so `%gs:0` is a genuine per-thread pointer that survives preemption, signals, fork and CPU migration. `SYS_CLONE` (child stack + TLS base) creates threads sharing the caller's address space; `SYS_TLS_SET` installs/removes the current thread's TLS; `apps/lib/thread.h` gives apps a tiny pthread-style runtime (`mct_tls_init`/`mct_thread_create`/`mct_thread_join`), and `threaddemo` proves isolation — 4 cloned workers each bump a private counter in their own TCB, every one landing on exactly `ITERS`.
- **Futex mutexes + condition variables (v38.27)**: `apps/lib/sync.h` gives apps pthread-style `mct_mutex_t`/`mct_cond_t` on top of `SYS_FUTEX_WAIT`/`SYS_FUTEX_WAKE` — the mutex fast path is one `lock cmpxchgl`, the slow path parks the thread in the kernel (`TASK_STATE_BLOCKED`, burns no CPU, works across cores); the condvar keeps a sequence counter so a signal racing the sleep is never lost, and the kernel's `futex_wait` re-checks the value under its lock. `apps/conddemo.mct` proves both on 4 cores: 4 threads bump a shared counter to exactly 20,000 under the mutex, then 2 producers + 2 consumers move 3,000 items through a bounded buffer over the condvar with no loss, no duplicates, and zero `[PANIC]`. Making it reliable exposed a real scheduler wake-path bug (see the v38.27 changelog).
- The 3,384-line shell monolith split into a dispatcher + 72 per-command modules; in-kernel GDB stub (COM2, breakpoints, single-step, DWARF symbols).

---

## Architecture

```
+--------------------------------------------------------------------+
|                       GRUB Multiboot (VBE)                         |
+--------------------------------------------------------------------+
|  boot.asm  -->  kernel.c  (kernel_main entry point)                |
+--------------------------------------------------------------------+
|  GDT (Ring 0+3)  |  IDT (ISR+IRQ)  |  TSS  |  Syscall (int 0x80)   |
+--------------------------------------------------------------------+
|  PIT Timer (1kHz, BSP) |  LAPIC Timer (per AP)  |  PS/2  |  Serial |
+--------------------------------------------------------------------+
|  Memory Manager  |  VMM (per-process page dirs, COW, mmap, shm)    |
+--------------------------------------------------------------------+
|  ACPI (RSDT/XSDT/MADT)  |  SMP (APIC/IOAPIC, 4 cores)              |
+--------------------------------------------------------------------+
|  Per-CPU Runqueues  |  Semaphores & Futexes  |  VFS + ATA PIO + AHCI + xHCI USB + virtio-blk |
+--------------------------------------------------------------------+
|  VGA/VESA Driver   |  Window Manager  |  RTL8139 NIC (IRQ-driven)  |
+--------------------------------------------------------------------+
|  Network Stack (Ethernet/ARP/IPv4/ICMP/UDP/TCP/DNS)                |
+--------------------------------------------------------------------+
|  MCT + ELF32 Loaders |  Ring 3 User Tasks |  GDB Stub (COM2)       |
+--------------------------------------------------------------------+
|  Desktop |  Taskbar  |  Login Screen  |  fd layer  |  UNIX pipes   |
+--------------------------------------------------------------------+
```

### Target Platform

| Component | Detail |
|---|---|
| CPU | i386 (32-bit x86), monolithic kernel |
| Privilege | Ring 0 (kernel) + Ring 3 (user) |
| Scheduler | Preemptive, per-CPU runqueues, priority + aging, work stealing |
| Display | VESA VBE linear framebuffer, 1024×768, 32-bit color |
| Storage | ATA PIO (IDE) — drive 0 VFS, drive 1 ext2, drive 3 FAT32 (drive 2 CD-ROM) · AHCI SATA 4+ · xHCI USB 3.0 8+ · virtio-blk 12+ |
| Audio | Sound Blaster 16 + PC speaker |
| Network | RTL8139 (virtual, via QEMU) |
| Serial | COM1/COM2 UART 16550A (38400 baud, 8N1) |

## Project Structure

```
boot.asm               Multiboot header + 32-bit bootstrap
kernel.c               kernel_main: init order, main loop, input dispatch
linker.ld / Makefile   Kernel link script + parallel build (target-isolated)
run.sh                 QEMU launcher (KVM, 4 cores, gateway proxy, serial log)

src/
  sys/                 Kernel core
    task.c             Scheduler: per-CPU runqueues, fork/exec/waitpid/signals
    schedule, sleep/wake, per-CPU load sampling
    vmm.c              Frame allocator, page dirs, COW, mmap, shm frames
    loader.c           MCT + ELF32 loaders, loader_build_image/task_exec
    syscall.c/.gui/.vfs/.net/.ipc/.proc   Modular syscall handlers
    shell.c            Kernel shell (commands, env vars, aliases, jobs)
    smp.c apic.c acpi.c  AP boot (INIT-SIPI-SIPI), APIC/IOAPIC, MADT
    ext2.c vfs.c fd.c  ext2 write layer, VFS, file descriptors, pipes
    sync.c ipc.c shm.c clipboard.c security.c  Sync primitives, IPC, shm
    gdt.c idt.c timer.c interrupt_entry.asm  CPU setup, IRQ dispatch, 1 kHz tick
  drivers/
    vga.c font8x16.c   Framebuffer compositor, primitives, font
    keyboard.c mouse.c serial.c rtc.c timer.c speaker.c
    ata.c ahci.c xhci.c pci.c net.c rtl8139.c sb16.c dma.c gdb_stub.c
  gui/
    wm.c desktop.c taskbar.c login.c   Window manager + desktop shell
  include/             Public headers (syscall.h, task.h, vmm.h, ...)

apps/                  Ring 3 user applications
  terminal.c nano.c notepad.c explorer.c sysinfo.c taskmgr.c browser.c fbmap.c
  clock.c calc.c gcalc.c snake.c flappy.c pci.c mplayer.c volume.c
  hello.c elfdemo.c syncdemo.elf udptest.elf    (ELF32 demos)
  forkdemo.c execdemo.c shmdemo.c mmapdemo.c mmapfiledemo.c lseekfiledemo.c
  sigdemo.c bgread.c smpstress.c pipegen.c piperead.c tcpserver.c looper.c
  rusthello.rs               (FIRST RING 3 APP IN RUST — freestanding no_std,
                             built by scripts/build_rust_mct.py, same MCT1
                             format the C apps use; rustc ≥ stable via rustup,
                             target i686-unknown-uefi, entry resolved at link)
  lib/libc.c libc.h    Homegrown dynamic shared library (libc.mct)

scripts/               build_mct.py, build_elf.py, inject_vfs.py, gateway.py,
                       boot_test.py, usb_test.py, ahci_test.py, fbmap_test.py, debug.py (serial log reader)
docs/architecture/     scheduler.md, memory.md, syscalls.md, smp_and_apic.md
.github/workflows/     build-boot-test.yml (CI boot test)
doom/                  Embedded DOOM engine (GPLv2)
```

## Core Subsystems

### Kernel & Memory

| Subsystem | Highlights |
|---|---|
| Virtual Memory (`vmm.c`) | Frame-bitmap allocator (32 768 pages), per-process page dirs, region allocator, COW with refcounts |
| Demand paging | `mmap()` reserves zero frames; the PF handler lazily maps+zeroes one page per fault |
| Shared memory | System V segments, `PAGE_SHARED` skips COW, leak-proof attach bitmap |
| Syscall layer | Modular handlers, every user pointer validated (`validate_user_ptr`) |
| GDT/IDT/TSS | Ring 0/3 segments, interrupt-gate IRQs, trap-gate `int 0x80` (preemptible syscalls) |
| Debugging | In-kernel GDB stub (RSP on COM2): F12 break, breakpoints, single-step, DWARF symbols |

### Scheduling & SMP (`task.c`, `smp.c`, `apic.c`)

- Per-CPU runqueues with priority (IDLE < LOW < NORMAL < HIGH) + aging; work-stealing migration between cores.
- APs tick via their own LAPIC timer (PIT-calibrated once on the BSP before AP wake — prevents timer storms).
- One `task_lock`, cli-first everywhere; signal delivery on the syscall-return path is locked too.
- Task states: FREE / RUNNING / READY / SLEEP / BLOCKED / STOPPED / ZOMBIE; hlt-park for zero-CPU waits.
- Per-CPU load sampling → `SYS_GET_SYSINFO` (`cpu_count`, `cpu_load[4]`) → SysInfo live bars.
- 16 KB kernel + 64 KB user stacks per task; per-CPU TSS with kernel-stack switching.

### Process Model (`syscall_proc.c`, `loader.c`)

| Feature | Notes |
|---|---|
| `fork()` | COW address-space clone, kernel-stack frame copy from `TSS.esp0 - sizeof(registers_t)` (deterministic), fd/handler/mmap/shm inheritance |
| `exec()` | Live syscall-frame patching; caught handlers reset to default, pending signals cleared (POSIX) |
| `waitpid()` | Blocks in TASK_STATE_BLOCKED, reaps zombie, WNOHANG; 15 s orphan-zombie safety net |
| Signals | sigaction/sa_mask/sigprocmask/SA_RESTART; sigframes on user stack; default action = exit `128+sig` |
| Job control | pgrp/session per task; SIGTTIN/SIGTTOU on background tty access; fg/bg/jobs/kill %n |
| Shell | env vars + `$VAR`, aliases, tab completion, scripts, `ps`/`kill`/`uptime`/`memstat`, `fetch`; POSIX toolkit `uname`/`whoami`/`hostname`/`env`/`seq`/`head` |

### Filesystems & Storage

- **VFS** — 16-slot node table over ATA PIO, auto-save, growth guard (files move to the end of disk instead of overwriting neighbors).
- **ext2** — fully writable secondary partition: block/inode bitmap allocators, file grow/truncate (direct + singly-indirect), directory entry create/remove/rename with slack-splitting, superblock sync; allocators refuse metadata blocks; host `fsck.ext2` clean.
- **FAT32** — third drive (secondary slave, `index=3`), mounted at `/fat32`: BPB parsing, FAT cluster-chain traversal, **long file names (LFN) read + written** (reverse-order chain, checksum-verified; SFN fallback), cluster allocation/free on write/create/delete, whole-tree rebuild at mount; volumes created by host `mkfs.fat`/mtools work both ways.
- **FD layer** — 32 fds/task, 256 global, typed (`FILE`/`PIPE_READ`/`PIPE_WRITE`/`DEV`), refcounted, closed on task teardown.

### Network

- RTL8139 driver (bus mastering, IRQ 11 → INT 43), spinlock-serialized TX/RX (SMP race fix).
- Full in-kernel stack: Ethernet, ARP, IPv4, ICMP, UDP, DNS (via QEMU gateway `10.0.2.3`).
- Multi-connection TCP: 8 slots, per-conn seq/ack + 16 KB RX buffer, retransmit (5×6 s) + connect timeout (10 s), FIN handshake.
- UDP bind/send/recv syscalls; `fetch [domain]`, `ping`, `host` shell commands; host `gateway.py` proxy for real-internet HTTP.

### GUI & Input

- Double-buffered WM: dirty-region tracking, z-order, rounded corners, traffic-light buttons, Aero snap, 8-directional resize (min 220×150), minimized-window ghost prevention.
- Desktop: baked wallpaper, squircle icons, draggable + persistent positions, double-click launch.
- Taskbar: per-app icon buttons, WiFi indicator, CAPS + HDD LEDs, WIB clock; system tray.
- Input: IntelliMouse 4-byte protocol + scroll wheel (event type 4), shared `ps2_drain()` 8042 arbitration, modifier-key tracking (Alt+Tab).
- Rendering: triple-buffer delta compositing (~4 ms/frame), enforced clip rectangle on all primitives.

### Audio

- SB16 native driver, 8/16-bit mono/stereo WAV streaming, tone synthesis, volume control (scroll-adjust), mapped to QEMU's PulseAudio backend.

### App Runtime

- `.mct` binary format (16-byte header, fixed-base mapping) + standard ELF32 ET_EXEC loader (auto-detected by magic).
- Shared-library system (`libc.mct`): dynamic symbol resolution via export-table pointer; app binaries ~2 KB.
- `SYS_SET_STDOUT_IPC` lets apps stream output to queues; pipelines/redirection via `task_fork_exec` + dup2 rewiring.

---

## Syscall API Reference

All syscalls are invoked via `int 0x80` (trap gate). Registers: `EAX` = number, `EBX`/`ECX`/`EDX`/`ESI`/`EDI` = arguments.

### Core (1–10)
| # | Name | Description |
|---|------|-------------|
| 1 | SYS_PRINT | Print string. EBX=str_ptr, ECX=color |
| 2 | SYS_OPEN | Open/create VFS file. EBX=filename → fd |
| 3 | SYS_READ | Read file. EBX=fd, ECX=buf, EDX=size |
| 4 | SYS_WRITE | Write file/pipe/serial. EBX=fd, ECX=buf, EDX=size |
| 5 | SYS_CLOSE | Close fd. EBX=fd |
| 6–7 | SYS_MALLOC / SYS_FREE | Identity-mapped heap allocate/free |
| 8 | SYS_GET_TICKS | PIT tick count |
| 9 | SYS_YIELD | Yield CPU (`sti; hlt; cli`) |
| 10 | SYS_EXIT | Terminate current task |

### GUI (11–17)
| # | Name | Description |
|---|------|-------------|
| 11 | SYS_DRAW_RECT | EBX=win_id, ECX=x, EDX=y, ESI=(w<<16)\|h, EDI=color |
| 12 | SYS_DRAW_TEXT | EBX=win_id, ECX=x, EDX=y, ESI=str, EDI=color |
| 13 | SYS_GET_KEY | Non-blocking keyboard char |
| 14 | SYS_GET_MOUSE | Mouse state → EAX=x, EBX=y, ECX=buttons |
| 15 | SYS_CREATE_WINDOW | EBX=x, ECX=y, EDX=w, ESI=h, EDI=title → win_id |
| 16 | SYS_GET_EVENT | Window event (paint/key/mouse/scroll/close) |
| 17 | SYS_UPDATE_WINDOW | Commit draw commands |

### Threads & Processes (18–22, 46–51)
| # | Name | Description |
|---|------|-------------|
| 18 | SYS_THREAD_CREATE | EBX=entry, ECX=priority, EDX=page_dir → TID |
| 19 | SYS_SLEEP | Sleep EBX ticks |
| 20–22 | SYS_GET_PID / SET_PRIORITY / GET_PRIORITY | Task identity + priority |
| 46 | SYS_GET_TASKS | List tasks. EBX=array, ECX=max |
| 47 | SYS_GET_WINDOWS | List windows. EBX=array, ECX=max |
| 48 | SYS_KILL_TASK | Force kill EBX=tid |
| 49 | SYS_GET_LAUNCH_ARG | Launch argument string |
| 50 | SYS_CREATE_FILE | Create empty VFS file |
| 51 | SYS_LOAD_LIBRARY | Dynamically load shared library → base |

### IPC (23–28)
| # | Name | Description |
|---|------|-------------|
| 23–26 | SYS_IPC_CREATE / SEND / RECV / DESTROY | Named 64-byte message queues |
| 27–28 | SYS_IPC_TRY_SEND / TRY_RECV | Non-blocking variants |

### Memory & VM (29–31, 78–83, 93–94)
| # | Name | Description |
|---|------|-------------|
| 29–31 | SYS_VMM_MAP / ALLOC / FREE | Low-level page mapping |
| 78 | SYS_SHMGET | Create/attach segment. EBX=key, ECX=size → id |
| 79–81 | SYS_SHMAT / SHMDT / SHMCTL | Attach / detach / IPC_RMID |
| 82 | SYS_MMAP | Reserve VA range (lazy zero-fault). EBX=size → base |
| 83 | SYS_MUNMAP | Free faulted frames of a range |
| 93 | SYS_MMAP_FILE | Map an open VFS file fd; pages fault in lazily **from disk**; dirty pages write back on msync/munmap. EBX=fd, ECX=flags |
| 94 | SYS_MSYNC | Flush dirty pages of a file-backed mapping back to the file |
| 95 | SYS_LSEEK | Reposition fd read/write offset. EBX=fd, ECX=offset, EDX=whence(SEEK_SET/CUR/END) → new offset |
| 96 | SYS_FSTAT | File metadata by fd. EBX=fd, ECX=stat_t* {size,type,node_idx,parent,data_sector,name} |

### I/O Multiplexing & POSIX misc (97–101)
| # | Name | Description |
|---|------|-------------|
| 97 | SYS_POLL | POSIX poll(). EBX=pollfd_t*, ECX=nfds, EDX=timeout_ms (<0 = forever) → ready count |
| 98 | SYS_SELECT | POSIX select(). EBX=nfds, ECX=readfds*, EDX=writefds*, ESI=exceptfds*, EDI=timeout_ms (uint32_t bitmaps) → ready count |
| 99 | SYS_GETCWD | Absolute path of the working directory. EBX=buf, ECX=size → 0 |
| 100 | SYS_CHDIR | Change working directory. EBX=path (relative or absolute) → 0 |
| 101 | SYS_CLOCK_GETTIME | Monotonic uptime. EBX=CLOCK_MONOTONIC, ECX=timespec_t* {tv_sec,tv_nsec} → 0 |

### UNIX Compatibility & Hardware (32–38)
| # | Name | Description |
|---|------|-------------|
| 32 | SYS_PIPE | Create pipe pair. EBX=pipefd[2] |
| 33 | SYS_GET_TIME | RTC time. EBX=rtc_time_t* |
| 34 | SYS_PLAY_SOUND | Tone. EBX=freq, ECX=ms |
| 35 | SYS_GET_SYSINFO | Hardware + stats incl. per-core load |
| 36–38 | SYS_GET_PCI_INFO / LIST_DIR / STAT_FILE | PCI, directory, file info |

### Network (39–43, 67–70)
| # | Name | Description |
|---|------|-------------|
| 39 | SYS_DNS_RESOLVE | Resolve domain |
| 40 | SYS_TCP_CONNECT | EBX=ip, ECX=port → conn id 0–7 |
| 41–42 | SYS_TCP_SEND / RECV | EDX=conn_id |
| 43 | SYS_NET_STATUS | Packed network state |
| 67–69 | SYS_UDP_BIND / SEND / RECV | Datagram sockets |
| 70 | SYS_TCP_CLOSE | Graceful FIN shutdown. EBX=conn_id |
| 85 | SYS_TCP_LISTEN | Listen on a port (TCP server) |

### Synchronization (61–66)
| # | Name | Description |
|---|------|-------------|
| 61–64 | SYS_SEM_CREATE / WAIT / POST / DESTROY | Counting semaphores, FIFO waiters |
| 65 | SYS_FUTEX_WAIT | Sleep while *addr==expected. EBX=addr, ECX=expected |
| 66 | SYS_FUTEX_WAKE | EBX=addr, ECX=max_waiters → woken |

### Process Model & Signals (71–77, 84, 86–92)
| # | Name | Description |
|---|------|-------------|
| 71 | SYS_FORK | COW clone → child tid / 0 / -1 |
| 72 | SYS_WAITPID | EBX=pid, ECX=status, EDX=WNOHANG |
| 73 | SYS_KILL | Send signal. EBX=pid, ECX=sig |
| 74 | SYS_SIGNAL | Install handler (0 default, 1 ignore) |
| 75 | SYS_SIGRETURN | Restore pre-handler frame |
| 76–77 | SYS_GETPPID / SYS_EXEC | Parent id / replace image |
| 84 | SYS_DUP2 | Redirect an fd (pipelines/redirection) |
| 86 | SYS_SIGACTION | Full sigaction: handler, sa_mask, flags |
| 87 | SYS_SIGPROCMASK | Block/unblock signals |
| 88–92 | SYS_SETPGID / GETPGRP / SETSID / TCSETPGRP / TCGETPGRP | Process groups, sessions, controlling terminal |
| 102–103 | SYS_CHMOD / SYS_CHOWN | Change permission bits (owner/root) / transfer ownership (root only) |
| 104–105 | SYS_CLONE / SYS_TLS_SET | Create thread (child stack + TLS base; child sees 0) / install current thread's TLS (`%gs` → TCB) |

| Sync Demo | .elf | Semaphore + futex workers |
| UDP Test | .elf | UDP datagram round-trip |

| lseek File Demo | .mct | SYS_LSEEK/SYS_FSTAT/O_APPEND on real files |
| Poll/Select Demo | .mct | POSIX poll()/select() over fds+pipes, getcwd/chdir, clock_gettime |
| FAT32 Demo | .mct | FAT32 drive 3: read mtools files, create/write/append/delete in `/fat32` |
| Sig Demo | .mct | sigaction/mask/SA_RESTART semantics |
| BG Read | .mct | Background-group reader stopped by SIGTTIN |
| SMP Stress | .mct | 8 children × 2 waves across 4 cores |
| Pipe Gen/Read | .mct | Pipeline producer/consumer |
| TCP Server | .mct | Echo server (host-connectable) |
| Looper | .mct | Infinite loop (Ctrl+C testing) |
| Fuzz Syscall | .mct | ~2000 random syscalls with hostile args — kernel must never panic |
| IO Bench | .mct | Page-cache micro benchmark: cold disk read vs cached RAM read, repeated write timing |

## Build and Run

### Requirements
`gcc (-m32)` · `nasm` · `make` · `qemu-system-i386` · `python3` + `Pillow` · `grub-mkrescue` + `mtools` · `mkfs.ext2` (for the ext2 drive)

### Commands
```bash
make clean && make        # build kernel + apps + ISO assets
./run.sh                  # KVM boot: 4 cores, gateway proxy, serial -> serial_debug.log
```

`run.sh` boots `mectov.iso` with `-smp 4`, the RTL8139 NIC, SB16 audio, and three drives: `disk.img` (MECTOVFS, index 0), `ext2.img` (index 1) and `fat32.img` (a 16 MB FAT32 volume at index 3 — index 2 is the CD-ROM; created with `mkfs.fat` if missing), streaming the serial log to `serial_debug.log` (also copied to `log.txt`). The Web Gateway Proxy (`scripts/gateway.py`) gives the guest real internet access.

The whole toolchain builds optimized: the kernel and embedded DOOM compile at `-O2 -march=i686 -fno-pie -fno-pic` (no GOT/PIC indirection — the kernel is linked at a fixed 1 MB and never relocates), and every Ring 3 app, `libc.mct` and ELF demo builds at `-O2`.

### Building User Applications
```bash
make apps/hello.mct       # builds any apps/<name>.c -> apps/<name>.mct (baked into the VFS)
```
App binaries are produced by `scripts/build_mct.py` (freestanding, entry `_start`, linked at `0x08000000`) and injected into the VFS by the Makefile. Standard ELF32 binaries work too:
```bash
python3 scripts/build_elf.py apps/elfdemo.c elfdemo.elf
```

### Debugging with GDB
```bash
# Terminal 1 — boot with COM2 as a GDB server
qemu-system-i386 -cdrom mectov.iso -m 128 -smp 4 \
  -serial file:serial.log -serial tcp:127.0.0.1:2345,server=on,wait=off \
  -drive file=disk.img,format=raw,index=0,media=disk

# Terminal 2 — attach (press F12 inside the guest to break in)
# (symbols live in myos.bin.debug since v38.81 — myos.bin itself is stripped)
gdb myos.bin.debug -ex "target remote :2345" -ex "c"
```

### Game data

This repository ships **no id Software data** — no `pak0.pk3`, no maps, no textures, no sounds. `.gitignore` refuses `*.pk3` and `assets/q3/` outright, and every suite in `make check` runs against the arena that `scripts/build_test_bsp.py` generates. You can point `q3arena` at a real map with **your own** copy of the game data:

1. Get the free **Quake III Arena demo** (id released it for free; it is not in this repo). The archive.org item `Q3A-Demo` carries `Quake 3 Arena Demo.zip` — 97,722,669 bytes, md5 `46cc890aa685b5fe083190f6d8ac38db`, sha1 `143788db882b347deb2e955f5a1c8365df759fc5` — and the file needed here is its `demoq3/pak0.pk3` (46,853,694 bytes):
   <https://archive.org/download/Q3A-Demo/Quake%203%20Arena%20Demo.zip>
   (the same item's torrent is far faster than the HTTP mirror). The demo ships four maps **and the weapon models**, so `--with-viewmodel` works on it; a retail `pak0.pk3` from the full game works identically if you own it.
2. Drop it at `assets/q3/pak0.pk3` (git-ignored).
3. Stage one map's closure — the `.bsp`, the shader scripts that name its textures, the images those scripts name, any skybox they use — as a loose-file tree, **plus the machinegun view model** (v38.124: without `--with-viewmodel` the port draws its procedural fallback gun instead of id's own `.md3`):
   ```bash
   python3 scripts/q3a_data.py --pak assets/q3/pak0.pk3 --map q3dm1 --out build/q3data --verify --with-viewmodel
   ```
   `--list` prints the plan without extracting anything; `--verify` re-reads the staged `.bsp` and reports any shader name that resolved to no file, so a partial extraction says so instead of looking finished. `--with-viewmodel` adds `models/weapons2/machinegun/*` — the body, its `_barrel` and `_flash` parts and the two images they name, ~48 KB, which the demo pak0 ships. `--with-models` / `--with-sounds` widen the closure much further.
4. `run.sh` and `check.py` mirror `build/q3data` onto the ext2 volume as `/baseq3/...` through `scripts/seed_ext2.sh`, sizing that volume at 512 MB with 4 KB blocks once the payload passes 8 MB (the kernel's ext2 driver walks 32 block groups, and 4 KB blocks put 512 MB at four of them). Boot with `MECTOV_Q3=1 ./run.sh` and type `q3arena q3dm1` in the Terminal — or just run `./quake.sh`, which does steps 3 and 4 for you (build + stage + KVM boot) and opens the Terminal.

Once the pak is staged, playing is: **`./quake.sh`** → `q3arena q3dm1` in the Terminal (WASD + mouse, `nolimit` to remove the frame cap, ESC back to the desktop).

Licensing: Quake III Arena's data is © id Software. Nothing here redistributes it, no suite that CI runs reads it, and the copyright of anything you stage stays with id.

## Testing

The project is tested on QEMU under **both KVM (4-core)** and TCG:

Run the whole battery locally with **`make check`** (builds the kernel + ISO, recreates fresh `disk.img`/`ext2.img`/`fat32.img` exactly like CI, runs every suite in CI order, and prints a concise pass/fail table in `.check/summary.txt`). **`make check-quick`** runs a fast high-signal subset (~6–8 min); `make check CHECK_ARGS=--keep-images` keeps the current disk images and `make check CHECK_ARGS=--only=<suite>` runs one suite. The fork/fputest KVM regressions are opt-in via `make check CHECK_ARGS=--kvm` — some hosts' KVM cannot run the `qemu32` machine even with `/dev/kvm` present (the guest stalls at AP wake); CI runs them on runners where KVM works.

| Suite | What it verifies |
|---|---|
| `scripts/boot_test.py` | Boot → login → `BOOTED KERNEL LOOP` smoke window |
| `scripts/fork_test.py` | COW fork + waitpid + signals (TCG) |
| `scripts/jobcontrol_test.py` | `sleep 2 &`, `jobs`, `bg`, `fg`, `kill %1` (TCG) |
| `scripts/fat32_test.py` | FAT32 drive 3: mtools-created files, nested dir, create/write/read-back, O_APPEND, delete (TCG) |
| `scripts/wfail_test.py` | Medium write-failure propagation (v38.159, audit F1): `apps/wfiledemo.c` writes 512 bytes to `/fat32` and prints what the syscalls returned; host `mtools` proves what actually reached the medium. `--mode control` demands the full success path (write + read-back + bytes on disk); `--mode inject` wraps the image in QEMU `blkdebug` (`write_aio` -> EIO) and demands the refusal be reported to the writer — no false success, nothing on the medium (TCG) |
| `scripts/pollselect_test.py` | poll/select over files+pipes (incl. timeout, EOF/HUP, POLLNVAL), getcwd/chdir round-trip, clock_gettime monotonic (TCG) |
| `scripts/fuzz_test.py` | Ring 3 syscall fuzzer: ~2000 random+hostile syscalls (garbage ptrs/sizes/fds), targeted overflow/sigreturn/kill probes, zero `[PANIC]` (TCG) |
| `scripts/iocache_test.py` | Whole-file page cache: cold disk read vs cached reads (min-of-N, must beat 3x), same-size rewrite save-skip, zero `[PANIC]` (TCG) |
| `scripts/app_smoke_test.py` | Demo apps, one boot: `smpstress` (8 children across 4-core SMP: CPU burn + sleep + self-signals → `ALL PASS`), `sigdemo` (sigprocmask/sa_mask/SA_NODEFER/SA_RESTART), `crashme` (deliberate `ud2` → kernel logs `[EXCEPTION] int_no=6` and survives) (TCG) |
| `scripts/doom_test.py` | Windowed DOOM: Terminal → `doom` → a WM window opens, the game loop enters, and `[DOOM] tick` lines prove frames are being produced and composited by the WM; OS stays alive (TCG; `--kvm` for speed) |
| `scripts/login_redesign_test.py` | Redesigned login gate: wallpaper full-bleed + amber clock at bottom-left, non-SPACE key ignored, SPACE opens the password panel through the fade/blur transition (waits for it to settle), wallpaper blurs + clock/footer hidden while typing, panel stays up, 4 s idle reverts through the closing fade (retries until the clock is back), password still logs in (TCG) |
| `scripts/mmapfile_test.py` | File-backed mmap: lazy page fault-in from disk, msync write-back, munmap, exit 0 (TCG) |
| `scripts/rusthello_test.py` | First Rust Ring 3 app (`rusthello.mct`): loads, runs, prints all markers, exits 0 (TCG) |
| `scripts/lseekfile_test.py` | POSIX lseek/fstat/O_APPEND on real files: all assertions pass, exit 0 (TCG) |
| `scripts/panic_test.py` | Multi-core panic self-test: boots with `panic_self_test panic=reboot` + `-no-reboot`, the kernel panics deliberately and must dump registers for **all 4 cores** (NMI-IPI snapshot), then reset so QEMU exits cleanly (TCG) |
| `scripts/perm_test.py` | Ownership/permission enforcement from Ring 3: create/write own file, chmod 0400/0000 (open-time + per-call read/write denials), chmod restore, chown root-only, root-owned 0644 read-allowed/write+delete-denied (TCG) |
| `scripts/thread_test.py` | TLS + `clone()`: main thread installs TLS, 4 cloned workers each with a private `%gs` TCB bump per-thread counters; every counter must land on exactly `ITERS` (isolation proof), joins via waitpid (TCG) |
| `scripts/cond_test.py` | Futex mutex + condvar: 4 threads bump a shared counter to exactly 20,000 under the mutex, then 2 producers + 2 consumers move 3,000 items through a bounded buffer over a condvar — every item consumed exactly once, zero `[PANIC]`. v38.160: `--repeat N` (N demo rounds in one boot — any failed round fails the suite) and `--smp N`, per-round serial offsets and terminal re-focus, a full QEMU-monitor post-mortem on a stall, and a stall-signature assertion (a `[WATCH] parked-frame` line — a task resumed into dead code — fails the run; `[WATCH] create-skip` is reported as survived evidence). That shape is what pinned F0's scheduler bug, and the fix is verified with `--repeat 60` on 4 vCPUs (60/60, dead-cur every round, one create-skip survived). CI's cond step is unpinned (`MCTOV_SMP=4`) again. |
| `scripts/rlimit_test.py` | Resource limits: getrlimit defaults sane, fork refused under NPROC cur=1 (per-uid count), fork OK after raising cur, non-root may never raise the hard limit, NOFILE stops fd allocation at the soft limit (close frees a slot), AS stops mmap at the soft limit (2 MB under 1 MB refused, 256 KB allowed, raise re-allows) — zero `[PANIC]` (TCG) |
| `scripts/ulimit_test.py` | Shell `ulimit` builtin: `-a` lists all resources with sane defaults, `-n 8` lowers NOFILE and reads back 8/32, raising soft above the hard limit is refused for a non-root caller, `-u 4` lowers NPROC, raising cur back to the hard limit is allowed, and rlimittest still passes afterwards (limits are inherited by child processes) — zero `[PANIC]` (TCG) |
| `scripts/bigread_test.py` | IDE DMA + multi-sector disk reads: asserts the BMIDE controller engaged and zero DMA transfers failed, then a 160 KB uncached file read 3× with byte-pattern verification on every read; `--kvm` for real timing (TCG in CI) |
| `scripts/lock_test.py` | Lock screen feature: logs in, launches Terminal, locks via the `lock` builtin, unlocks and pixel-compares the terminal window pre/post (session preserved, not reset), then locks again via Ctrl+Alt+L (TCG) |
| `scripts/autolock_test.py` | Idle auto-lock: `locktimeout 20` arms the timer, a quiet desktop locks on its own, unlock restores the session without re-locking, `locktimeout 0` disables (a longer quiet window passes with no lock) (TCG) |
| `scripts/hardening_test.py` | Kernel hardening: `passwd mectov123 hunter2` then the NEW password unlocks while the OLD one is rejected (a plaintext file would accept both); `apps/hardening_test.mct` proves from Ring 3 that `SYS_GETRANDOM` returns fresh non-zero bytes, two `/dev/random` reads differ, and `/etc/passwd` is `salt:hash` with no plaintext — zero `[PANIC]` (TCG) |
| `scripts/ahci_test.py` | AHCI/SATA driver (ich9-ahci + drive 4): boots with `ich9-ahci` + FAT32 SATA disk, asserts `AHCI` `drive 4` + `[AHCI] ready`, `mount /sata fat32 4` (BPB via AHCI), `cp` + host `mtools` byte-compare (TCG) |
| `scripts/usb_test.py` | xHCI/USB 3.0 driver (qemu-xhci + drive 8): boots with `qemu-xhci` + FAT32 USB stick on SuperSpeed bus, asserts `USB3 -> drive 8` + `[XHCI] ready`, `mount /usb fat32 8` (BPB via xHCI), `cp` + host `mtools` byte-compare (TCG) |
| `scripts/virtio_test.py` | VirtIO-Blk driver (virtio-blk-pci + drive 12): boots with transitional `disable-modern=on` + FAT32 disk, asserts `[VIRTIO] ready` + `drive 12`, `mount /vblk fat32 12` (BPB via virtqueue), `cp` + host `mtools` byte-compare, plus a cross-volume write landing on `fat32.img` (per-volume auto-select proof) (TCG) |
| `scripts/virtiogpu_test.py` | VirtIO-GPU driver (virtio-gpu-pci, **modern** virtio-pci — this device has no legacy interface): attaches its own GPU device, requires the four capability-found regions + the negotiated 64-bit features (`VIRTIO_F_VERSION_1`) in the boot log, recomputes the driver's pattern FNV-1a, byte-compares a `screendump` **of the device's own scanout** against that pattern (all 16384 pixels of a 128x128 test resource: guest memory → resource → scanout → pixels), runs `gpustat` and requires its serial line to repeat the live checksum; zero `[PANIC]` (TCG). `--gl` switches to `virtio-gpu-gl-pci` (virgl: capset + the 3D readback round trip, which is the only way to read a GL console back — QEMU answers `no surface` to a screendump of it) |
| `scripts/tls_selftest.py` | TLS 1.3 engine, offline: Terminal → `run /apps/tlsselftest.mct`, which checks the Ring 3 TLS library against RFC 4231/5869/8439/7748 and FIPS-197/NIST-GCM vectors, OpenSSL-made RSA/ECDSA signature fixtures and real DER certificates, then asserts the X.509 rule both accepts the chain it was handed and refuses the near-misses (wrong host, wrong CA, expired, tampered signature, missing issuer). Needs no network: the suite asserts the guest's own tally (`checks 53, failures 0`) and that no `[FAIL]` line appeared, so a stack that answers two assertions and dies cannot pass. v38.162 (TCG) |
| `scripts/tls_handshake_test.py` | TLS 1.3 engine, **live handshake on the host**: compiles the shipped engine (`apps/lib/tls/`) with only `src/include/syscall.h` swapped for a libc shim, drives a real OpenSSL TLS 1.3 server behind a throwaway trust anchor generated per run, and asserts that **every secret the engine derives** — both handshake traffic secrets and both application traffic secrets — is byte-identical to the server's own keylog, that the encrypted request and the encrypted reply survive the round trip, and that a chain the store does not contain is refused with `TLS_ERR_VERIFY` rather than a protocol error and leaks no application data. Needs no guest and no ISO, runs in seconds, and is the only suite that can see a wrong key schedule: a broken one still *completes* a handshake and only fails when the peer refuses to decrypt. v38.163 |
| `scripts/browser_https_test.py` | HTTPS in the Mini Browser: the guest fetches `https://10.0.2.2:8443/` from a host server holding a throwaway chain the image deliberately does **not** trust — the server must log `first_byte=0x16` and a parsed `[SRV] sni=`, and the browser must then print `[BROWSER] tls-fail ... not trusted` and render nothing (the positive path is `tls_handshake_test.py`'s job, offline and far more precisely). The same run's plain-HTTP controls then prove response framing: a Content-Length reply must stop at the promised length and a chunked reply must be de-chunked before render, both asserted on the rendered body length rather than on how the page looks — zero `[PANIC]`. v38.163 (TCG) |
| `scripts/fbmap_test.py` | Ring 3 scanout takeover: `SYS_FB_MAP` grants the framebuffer to a Ring 3 task, the kernel desktop suppresses its rendering, a typed key routes through the pump and releases cleanly (desktop restored), then a second takeover is killed with Ctrl+C mid-flight and ownership drops via task_cleanup with the desktop restored — zero `[PANIC]` (TCG) |
| KVM regressions | fork, exec, pipe, redir, mmap, shm, sig, ctrlc, stop, job, bgread, tcp, smpstress (4 cores) |

CI (`.github/workflows/build-boot-test.yml`) runs every suite above on each push: boot, login (redesigned gate), lock screen, idle auto-lock, kernel hardening (CSPRNG + salted passwd), Ring 3 scanout takeover, fork, jobcontrol, KVM fork, FAT32 (including its write-failure propagation via `blkdebug` EIO), poll/select, fuzz, page cache, app smoke tests, windowed DOOM, mmap, Rust, lseek, panic, permissions, TLS/clone, futex/condvar, resource limits, the `ulimit` shell builtin, VirtIO-Blk, VirtIO-GPU (modern virtio-pci), multi-sector PIO, the offline TLS 1.3 engine self-test, the live-handshake TLS 1.3 integration (host), and the HTTPS Mini-Browser refusal/framing suite.

---

## Version History

| Version | Highlights |
|---|---|
| v38.163 | **"HTTPS nyala di Mini Browser — dan yang dibutuhkan engine-nya bukan kode baru, tapi tes yang bisa benar-benar MENYELESAIKAN handshake; gate baru itu lalu menemukan sembilan bug nyata yang tidak bisa dilihat dua suite lama."** **(1) Yang benar-benar ditambahkan.** `apps/browser.c` menerima `https://`: `req_tls` (apa yang diminta URL) dan `using_tls` (transpor request yang sedang jalan) dipisah, skema URL yang menentukan port default (443 vs 80, port eksplisit selalu menang), SNI dikirim dari host di URL, dan handshake jadi **state 4** yang dipanggil **satu `tls_step()` per poll** dari loop yang sudah ada — jadi jendelanya tetap repaint dan app-nya tidak pernah masuk `tls_handshake()` yang memblokir. Framing balasan ditentukan header, berurutan: `Transfer-Encoding: chunked` (di-de-chunk di tempat sebelum body diukur), lalu `Content-Length` (baca berhenti di panjang yang dijanjikan, bukan menunggu peer menutup), lalu close-delimited; balasan tanpa `close_notify` diterima kalau sudah ada byte yang masuk (`[TLS] peer closed without close_notify`), sambungan yang berakhir tanpa apa-apa itu gagal. Chain yang ditolak **tidak menggambar apa pun**, dan verdict-nya dari `TLS_ERR_VERIFY` — itu sebabnya browser bilang "not trusted" untuk CA tak dikenal dan pesan lain untuk protocol error; "ditolak" dan "ditolak dengan alasan yang benar" itu dua klaim berbeda. **(2) Blind spot yang ditutup, dan bentuknya.** `tlsselftest.mct` menguji tiap primitif dengan vektor tetap, dan guest tidak punya server yang chain-nya dipercaya image — jadi record layer, key schedule dan framing daftar sertifikat **tidak pernah punya tes yang menyelesaikan handshake** sepanjang satu rilis. Itu blind spot berbentuk khusus: key schedule yang salah **tidak menghentikan** handshake, tapi membuat handshake yang dari luar kelihatan sehat dan baru ketahuan saat peer menolak mendekripsi. `scripts/tls_handshake_test.py` menutupnya di host dalam hitungan detik: compile `apps/lib/tls/tls13.c` **yang dikirim** dengan hanya dua baris `#include` ditulis ulang (syscall.h kernel → shim libc, trust store image → trust store hasil `scripts/gen_tls_roots.py` dari CA sekali pakai), lalu drive server OpenSSL TLS 1.3 sungguhan lewat `scripts/tls_host_client.c`. **(3) Sembilan defect, dan kenapa masing-masing bisa lolos.** (a) `Derive-Secret(Secret, "derived", "")` memakai konteks panjang-nol padahal RFC berarti `Transcript-Hash("")` — SHA-256 dari string kosong, dan RFC 8448 mematok nilainya (`6f2615a1…`); ini meracuni **handshake secret**, jadi **kedua** handshake traffic secret salah, tapi semua record sampai ServerHello masih plaintext sehingga suite di atas tetap melihat ClientHello sah, ServerHello yang bisa di-parse dan transcript yang cocok. (b) Additional data AEAD tidak diisi, di seal **dan** open — RFC 8446 §5.2 menyebutnya header record; record keluar tetap tampak well-formed, hanya tag peer yang gagal, dan hanya setelah record jadi terenkripsi. (c) `split_certificates()` tidak pernah memakan panjang 2 byte extensions tiap `CertificateEntry`, jadi cek panjang akhir gagal di **setiap** chain nyata. (d) Daftar sertifikat sampai ke verifier masih membawa framing wire-nya, jadi sertifikat pertama di-parse mulai dari panjang 3 byte-nya sendiri — (c) dan (d) tersembunyi di belakang (a), yang gagal lebih dulu. (e) MGF1 menambahkan counter **satu** byte padahal RFC 8017 minta empat oktet, jadi setiap tanda tangan RSA-PSS gagal — self-test tidak pernah memverifikasi tanda tangan PSS sama sekali, cuma memeriksa bahwa SHA-384 "ada untuk RSA-PSS". (f) Master secret diturunkan dari handshake **traffic** secret server, bukan dari handshake secret itu sendiri (yang tidak disimpan struct-nya). (g) Application secret diturunkan **setelah** Finished milik kita sendiri sudah masuk transcript. (h) `tls_step()` balik lebih awal begitu handshake selesai, jadi `tls_read()` tidak pernah menyentuh socket lagi — setiap byte pasca-handshake (balasan, ticket, `close_notify`) diam tak terbaca selama umur koneksi, dan client melaporkan "belum ada apa-apa" selamanya. (i) `tls_read()` membuang plaintext yang sudah ter-buffer ketika pump-nya berhenti karena error atau close di panggilan yang sama — dan begitulah persis balasan `Content-Length` datang (peer menjawab lalu menutup dalam satu burst), jadi balasan lengkap yang sudah terdekripsi dibuang. (f), (g) punya bentuk yang sama: handshake-nya **selesai** — Finished dicek dengan kunci handshake dan lolos — satu-satunya gejala adalah peer menolak mendekripsi request pertama, jauh setelah semuanya kelihatan sehat; dan (i) cuma muncul kalau server menutup tepat setelah menjawab. **(4) Dua bug di gate-nya sendiri, dicatat karena keduanya kesalahan yang sama.** `ctx.wrap_socket(far, server_side=True)` menjalankan **seluruh** handshake sebelum balik, dan dipanggil sebelum thread relay perekam byte ada — ClientHello tidak pernah sampai ke TLS stack dan pemanggilnya memblokir sampai guest menyerah; suite melaporkannya sebagai "ClientHello tidak bisa di-parse", padahal ClientHello-nya sempurna. `do_handshake_on_connect=False` itu load-bearing. Dan helper browsing mengambil offset log serial **setelah** URL disubmit, jadi penolakan TLS (yang cuma perlu sampai Certificate server, dan karena itu cepat) mendarat **sebelum** jendela yang diperiksa — penolakan yang benar dilaporkan sebagai gagal menolak. **(5) Ketahanan gate-nya diuji, bukan diasumsikan.** Sebelum ditutup, dua dari sembilan bug itu dimasukkan lagi satu per satu secara sengaja: mengembalikan (f) membuat gate FAIL di `c ap sec`/`s ap sec` (kedua secret app tidak cocok keylog) plus request tidak pernah sampai; mengembalikan (e) membuat handshake mati di `-3`. Gate yang tidak bisa gagal tidak membuktikan apa-apa. **(6) Verifikasi.** `python3 scripts/tls_handshake_test.py` — handshake selesai, server bilang `TLSv1.3 TLS_CHACHA20_POLY1305_SHA256`, **4/4 secret cocok keylog**, request terdekripsi server dan balasan 99 byte terdekripsi engine (sha256 sama), dan chain tak dikenal ditolak `TLS_ERR_VERIFY` tanpa satu byte pun data aplikasi. `python3 scripts/browser_https_test.py` — 5 `[OK]`, RC=0 (ClientHello sampai + SNI ter-parse + browser menolak tanpa render; kontrol `Content-Length` 79 byte dan chunked 195 byte wire → 83 byte body). Regresi lewat `check.py`: **7 suite, 0 failed** — boot 13s, app_smoke 262s, browser 31s, browser_web 30s, browser_https 80s, tls 33s (guest, `checks 53, failures 0`), tls_host 4s. `make iso` bersih **0 warning**. Dua suite baru terdaftar di `check.py` dan dua step CI baru ditambahkan. `docs/architecture/tls.md` dapat bagian live-handshake plus koreksi lama: dokumennya masih bilang validity dicek dengan `sys_get_ticks()` ("OS tidak punya wall clock") padahal engine membaca RTC lewat `SYS_GET_TIME`. Yang **belum** dan disebut apa adanya: TLS 1.2, resumption/PSK (jadi tiap fetch https bayar handshake penuh), 0-RTT, client certificate, HelloRetryRequest, AIA/OCSP, HTTP/2, keep-alive, cookie, dan proxy `CONNECT`. `OS_VERSION` 38.163; resolusi 320×240 dan `third_party/q3a/` tidak disentuh. |
| v38.162 | **"TLS 1.3 ditulis dari RFC-nya, lalu diuji lebih dulu sebelum ada yang boleh bergantung padanya" — engine TLS 1.3 Ring 3 lengkap ada di `apps/lib/tls/`, dijalankan sebagai gate offline di guest, dan yang menemukannya delapan bug kripto + empat bug X.509 nyata, bukan "compiled, jadi jalan".** **(1) Isinya.** Satu library Ring 3 tanpa syscall baru dan tanpa satu baris pun perubahan kernel: SHA-256/384/512, HMAC, HKDF (extract/expand/expand_label), ChaCha20, Poly1305 26-limb, AEAD_CHACHA20_POLY1305, AES-128-GCM (GHASH via gf_mul), X25519 (tangga Montgomery), RSA PKCS#1 v1.5 **dan** PSS, ECDSA P-256/P-384 (aritmetika Jacobian + Montgomery CIOS), DER + X.509 (tbs, SPKI, sig-alg, basicConstraints/keyUsage, validity, SAN), record layer + handshake state machine + key schedule, `tls_roots[]` hasil generate. Entropi dari `SYS_GETRANDOM` (117) — **gagal getrandom = handshake dibatalkan**, tidak ada RNG cadangan yang lemah; record keluar dipecah supaya muat di klamp 1400 byte `net_tcp_send`; port 443 tidak di-redirect, jadi tidak ada yang perlu diubah di `src/drivers/net.c`. **(2) Kenapa gate-nya offline, dan kenapa itu justru intinya.** `apps/tlsselftest.mct` menautkan source library-nya langsung (bukan libc) dan tidak menyentuh jaringan sama sekali: vektor RFC 4231/5869/8439 dan RFC 7748, FIPS-197 dan NIST GCM, fixture tanda tangan RSA/ECDSA yang dibuat OpenSSL, sertifikat DER sungguhan, plus aturan path building X.509 yang harus **menerima** chain yang diberikan **dan menolak** yang nyaris benar — host salah, CA salah, kedaluwarsa, tanda tangan diutak-atik, issuer hilang. Kegagalan yang menarik di stack TLS bukan "tidak bisa connect", tapi "connect, dan yang salah diterima", dan itu tidak bisa dites lawan server hidup yang berubah di bawah kaki. `scripts/tls_selftest.py` menjalankannya di guest lewat `check.py` (suite `tls`, 35 detik) dan meng-assert **angka dari guest-nya sendiri** — `checks 53, failures 0` plus tidak ada baris `[FAIL]` — jadi stack yang menjawab dua assertion lalu mati tidak bisa lolos. **(3) Delapan bug primitif, bukan tebakan.** Eksponen RSA dibaca dengan dua urutan bit berbeda sehingga `0x010001` menyusut jadi eksponen **1** (semua verifikasi positif gagal, negatif "lulus" karena menolak segalanya — pola yang menyesatkan); keluar dari bentuk Montgomery dilakukan dengan mengalikan citra Montgomery dari 1 alih-alih 1, jadi tidak membatalkan apa pun; `jac_to_affine_x` mengalikan satu R kebanyakan; `jac_add` bagian Y3 memakai `r` yang **belum** digandakan padahal X3 sudah, sehingga titik yang dihasilkan tidak ada di kurva; `fsub` meninggalkan `n` bukan nol untuk hasil nol, jadi penjumlahan titik dengan negasinya tidak pernah jadi titik di tak hingga; trailer AEAD_CHACHA20_POLY1305 menghitung **bit** padahal RFC menghitung **byte**; ChaCha20 memuat key dan nonce big-endian; dan konstanta modulus + `b` P-384 salah ketik — dua kali, dan yang kedua itu justru "referensi Python" gue sendiri yang salah, itu sebabnya konstanta sekarang **digenerate**, bukan diketik. **(4) Empat bug di lapisan X.509.** Perbandingan OID menguji panjang *body* lawan panjang *TLV* penuh; kursor di-reset ke 0 di antara notBefore dan notAfter (jadi notBefore terbaca dua kali); satu offset dipakai ulang untuk SEQUENCE dalam sehingga `RSAPublicKey` di-parse 11 byte dari awal; dan modulus masih membawa byte tanda DER-nya (257 byte untuk kunci 2048-bit). **(5) Perubahan pendukung yang benar-benar dibutuhkan.** `scripts/build_mct.py` akhirnya bisa N source (argumen terakhir = output, bentuk dua-argumen tetap byte-identical — diverifikasi dengan `cmp` pada `hello`/`gcalc`/`explorer`), rule Makefile + `objcopy` + pendaftaran VFS untuk `tlsselftest.mct`, entri `check.py` dan step CI, `scripts/gen_tls_fixtures.py` (sekaligus memperbaiki bug PEM→DER yang membuat fixture pertama bukan DER sama sekali), `scripts/gen_tls_roots.py` + `tls_roots.h` (121 root publik), dan `scripts/tls_selftest_host.sh` yang menjalankan **file yang sama** di host dengan hanya menukar `sys_print`. **(6) Verifikasi.** Host: 53 checks / 0 failed. Guest: `checks 53, failures 0`, `TLS selftest OK`, tanpa `[PANIC]`. Regresi di tree ini: `make iso` bersih **0 warning**, dan `python3 scripts/check.py --only=boot --only=app_smoke --only=tls` = **3 passed, 0 failed** (boot 13s, app_smoke 260s, tls 35s). Yang **belum**: HTTPS di `apps/browser.c`, Content-Length/chunked, dan wiring `tls_roots[]` ke browser — itu v38.163, dan belum diklaim jalan. `OS_VERSION` 38.162; resolusi 320×240 dan `third_party/q3a/` tidak disentuh. |
| v38.161 | **"utang audit F2–F11 ditutup sekali jalan — dan satu temuan ternyata salah" — sembilan temuan sisa laporan audit `docs/audit/architecture-review-2026-10-04.md` dibereskan, dan F11 dikoreksi, bukan diperbaiki, karena `task_reap_zombies()` memang jalan.** **(1) Dokumen yang salah, diperbaiki.** `scheduler.md` bilang `MAX_TASKS = 32` padahal kernel jalan dengan 64 (`MAX_CPUS` 16); batas VFS node dikutip tiga cara (64 di `memory.md`, 256 di baris README v38.44, 2048 di `src/include/vfs.h` — layout v5 sejak v38.141), dan bullet "capacity bumps" di README sekarang menyebut angka sekarang plus lokasi kodenya; intro `memory.md` yang masih menjanjikan "two-level x86 paging" padahal migrasi PAE 3-level-nya didokumentasikan di file yang sama; dan `init_mem()` yang dideskripsikan "mengurai mmap Multiboot lalu membulatkan ke atas" padahal kodenya menerima skalar `mem_size` dari header Multiboot dan mem-floor (`mem_size / PAGE_SIZE`) — plus catatan v38.156 bahwa RAM dan *span yang dipetakan* itu dua besaran berbeda, yang dulu jadi bug boot nyata. **(2) Gate yang tidak bisa gagal, dibuat bisa gagal.** Pre-flight seed DOOM di `scripts/doom_test.py` mencetak `[skip]` dan `return` kalau `debugfs` tidak ada atau gagal jalan, jadi satu-satunya suite yang membutuhkannya diam-diam kehilangan daya bedanya (doom1.wad hilang/terpotong dulu adalah confounder nomor 1 untuk "tidak pernah masuk game loop"); sekarang **FAIL exit 2** dengan hint install, dan `debugfs` masuk preflight `TOOLS` di `check.py` supaya tool yang hilang disebut lebih awal. `check.py` juga jadi salinan terakhir probe KVM yang memutuskan pakai `exists()`+`os.access()` — `/dev/kvm` bisa ada dan tetap gagal dibuka (itu sebabnya kbd/heap/cons/gui dimigrasikan di `a969f84`); sekarang node itu **dibuka** untuk diuji. **(3) Dua bug kecil di kernel yang ketemu sambil membereskan nits.** `src/sys/entropy.c` menamai penghitung `CHACHA_ROUNDS` padahal setiap iterasinya satu *double round* (8 → 16 round penuh), jadi kotaknya lebih kuat dari namanya — makro-nya sekarang `CHACHA_DOUBLE_ROUNDS`; dan `get_random_u32()` mengembalikan **0 keras** selama pool belum ter-seed, artinya ASLR dan ISN TCP pertama mendapat konstanta, bukan acak — sekarang retry lalu jatuh ke campuran TSC yang tidak pernah konstan dan tidak pernah nol. `wm_lock_release()` di `src/gui/wm.c` dulu menurunkan depth ke 0 lalu melepas lock tanpa syarat, jadi release tak seimbang bisa diam-diam mencabut critical section milik task lain; depth 0 sekarang mencatat `[WATCH] wm-unbalanced-release` (throttled) dan **tidak** mengubah state. **(4) Klaim "0 warning" akhirnya benar.** Build bersih masih mencetak 36 baris `ld: ... RWX permissions` (satu per payload Ring 3) plus dua warning rustc: catatan RWX itu memang disengaja (W^X hanya mengikat heap/stack/mmap user, image `.mct` yang dimuat tetap executable karena tak ada dynamic linker) dan linker 64-bit sudah lewat `--no-warn-rwx-segments` sejak dulu — sekarang linker app 32-bit ikut; step rustc berhenti mewarisi `MAKEFLAGS` (rustc tidak bisa memakai jobserver make, yang bikin catatan "Bad file descriptor" di setiap build inkremental) dan berhenti menyebut "mmx" yang sebenarnya bukan feature yang dikenal. **(5) F11 dikoreksi, bukan dikerjakan.** Audit menyebut `task_reap_zombies()` dead code; ternyata grep-nya cuma di `src/` sementara pemanggilnya di `kernel.c` (dua jalur tick 1 Hz BSP, baris 650 dan 1005). Reaper-nya jalan, jadi `zombie_reap_ms` dan knob `/proc/sys/zombie_reap_ms` memang hidup — dan itu sekaligus menjelaskan detail dump F0 yang dulu "tidak dijelaskan apa pun": slot FREE yang masih membawa `zombie_since` itu reaper yang membebaskan zombie setelah timeout 15 s karena induknya tidak pernah memanggil `waitpid()`. Supaya bisa dilihat, reaper sekarang mencetak `[WATCH] free-zombie` (watch kind 8, throttled) saat itu terjadi. **(6) Tabel README.** Tiga baris versi punya `|` mentah di dalam code span (v38.128 `map|clampmap|animMap` dan `tcMod scroll|scale`, v38.50 `PCD|PWT`, v38.41 `OSFXSR|OSXMMEXCPT`) sehingga tabelnya pecah; sudah di-escape. Angka "12 baris" di laporan audit itu artefak scan yang menghitung `\|` yang sudah benar sebagai pemisah — rescan yang jujur hasilnya 3. Verifikasi: `make iso` bersih tanpa warning, `make check-quick` 12/12, `py_compile` untuk script yang diubah. `OS_VERSION` 38.161; resolusi 320×240 dan `third_party/q3a/` tidak disentuh. |
| v38.160 | **"race condvar lintas-core v38.92 ternyata dua bug — keduanya ditutup; yang kedua bug scheduler: slot dipakai ulang saat frame mati masih dianggap 'current' di satu core" — temuan F0 laporan audit ditutup dengan root cause yang bisa dibuktikan, bukan ditebak, dan pin CI 1-core akhirnya dilepas.** **(1) Bug pertama, di aplikasi.** Loop consumer `apps/conddemo.c` membawa `mct_mutex_unlock(&c_mu)` **kedua** sejak v38.92 — commit yang sama yang mencatat "pre-existing cross-core race" dan mem-pin `cond_test` ke `MCTOV_SMP=1`. Di 2+ core, unlock pertama (`lock=0` + wake) memberi kesempatan core lain masuk critical section, lalu store kedua melepas lock lagi SAAT peer itu ada di dalam — dua thread di section yang sama, item hilang/dobel, persis signature yang dilaporkan audit (`FAIL consumed count` / `FAIL missing/duplicate items`). Di 1 core store kedua tidak berbahaya (pemiliknya mengakuisisi ulang sebelum peer jalan) — itu sebabnya stres SMP1 selalu hijau. Fix: satu unlock per critical section. **(2) Bug kedua (scheduler): frame mati dicomot-plot ke slot yang didaur ulang.** Stall 4-core tanpa verdict itu bukan wake yang hilang: task yang keluar tetap jadi `current_task[cid]` di core-nya sampai tick berikutnya — `task_exit_with_code` hanya bisa parkir di loop `hlt`, dan perpindahannya terjadi di `schedule()`, jadi jendelanya ≤10 ms pada 100 Hz. Kalau slot direbut di dalam jendela itu (conddemo melakukannya secara struktural: worker mutex terakhir keluar, `join()` balik beberapa mikrodetik kemudian, fase producer/consumer langsung meng-`clone` ke slot yang sama), tick core basi menulis frame mati itu ke slot yang sudah didaur ulang (`tasks[cur].esp = esp`) — menimpa frame segar thread baru, dan pick berikutnya me-iret thread itu ke loop `hlt` milik task mati. Thread baru tidak pernah menjalankan satu instruksi pun, `waitpid` induknya tidak pernah selesai, ronde mati tanpa verdict; ronde 1 tiap boot selalu aman karena belum ada pendahulu di slot mana pun — itu sebabnya semua step CI single-run hijau. Buktinya dari post-mortem v38.160, bukan inferensi: consumer yang macet (`tasks[8]`) ber-status READY di `rq[0]` sementara frame tersimpannya adalah loop parkir exit (`task_exit_with_code+0x71`), `zombie_since=0` (thread itu tidak pernah exit sendiri), induknya BLOCKED `waiting=8`, tabel futex kosong. **(3) Fix berlapis + alat yang bisa dilihat.** `schedule()` tidak lagi menulis apa pun ke current task yang sudah mati (esp, watermark, state, runqueue, citra FPU, akunting tick), dan saat tak ada yang runnable core-nya diparkir ke idle task-nya sendiri alih-alih me-iret frame mati; `rq_enqueue` menolak tid mati; jalur commit menolak `next` yang mati; `task_set_state` menolak menghidupkan kembali FREE/ZOMBIE; `sem_destroy` menyaring waiter mati/idle (satu-satunya penulis READY tanpa guard di `sync.c`); dan semua jalur pengklaiman slot (`clone`/`fork`/`fork_exec`/idle) melompati slot FREE yang masih dinamai `current_task` sebuah core. Diagnostik: baris `[WATCH]` ber-throttle per jenis (`dead-cur`, `create-skip`, `revive-refused`, `dead-in-rq`, `parked-frame`) plus sweep integritas 1 Hz di BSP; `scripts/cond_test.py` **gagal** kalau baris `parked-frame` muncul (task di-iret ke kode mati) dan melaporkan `create-skip` sebagai bukti yang berhasil diselamatkan, bukan kegagalan. **(4) Verifikasi dan unpin.** `--repeat 60` di 4 vCPU (TCG) = **60/60 ronde ALL PASS** — bentuk yang sama sebelumnya stall di ronde 36 — dengan `dead-cur` di tiap ronde dan satu `create-skip tid=8` (jendela clobber memang menyala, sekarang selamat); run kedua `--repeat 30` juga hijau; `make check-quick` 12/12 dan `boot_test.py` rc=0 di tree ini. Post-mortem lengkap saat stall (register semua vCPU, `current_task`, `rq[0..3]`, `tasks[]`, dua kernel stack, tabel futex) tetap tersedia di harness. Step CI akhirnya **dilepas dari pin** (`MCTOV_SMP=4`, timeout 900 s) karena pin 1 core memang tidak bisa melatih bug mana pun di sini; stres multi-ronde tetap perintah lokal yang didokumentasikan (`python3 scripts/cond_test.py --repeat 60`). `OS_VERSION` naik ke 38.160. |

Sekarang ada `ata_status_error()` (dicek hanya setelah command selesai), counter `ata_write_errors`, log `[ATA] write refused by drive <hex> at LBA <hex> (status ERR/DF set)`, dan ketiga jalur tulis (multi-sector PIO, single-sector PIO, DMA) pulang `-1`. Di atasnya `ext2_write_block`/`ext2_sync_super` dan `fat32_write_sectors`/`fat32_write_cluster`/`fat32_set_next` jadi `int`, semua pemanggil internal (bitmap, dirent, salinan FAT, inode/superblock) dicek dan di-rollback, dan `vfs_save()` plus jalur data VFS (ext2, fat32, native) meneruskan kegagalan ke nilai balik syscall — `size` tidak pernah disimpan untuk byte yang tidak mendarat. Jalur release-only (`free_block`, `free_chain`, …) sengaja tetap log-lalu-lanjut: menolak free saat recovery justru bocor. **(2) Yang membuktikan: media yang menolak menulis.** `scripts/wfail_test.py` plus app Ring 3 baru `apps/wfiledemo.c` yang mencetak persis apa yang dikatakan syscall; ground truth-nya `mtools` membaca image, bukan kata guest. `--mode inject` memasang filter `blkdebug` QEMU di image FAT32 dengan `[inject-error] event="write_aio" errno=5` (EIO) — semua tulis ditolak di level block, read normal sehingga guest tetap boot dan mount. Sebelum fix: rc=1, guest mencetak `WFILE write ok` padahal nol byte ada di media (`write ok=True readback ok=False file on media=False`) — bug direproduksi harfiah. Sesudah fix: rc=0, `write ok=False`, `failure markers=['WFILE create FAILED']`, `[OK] inject: the refused write was reported to the writer (no false success, nothing on the medium)`. Kontrol tanpa injeksi tetap hijau: write + read-back ok dan `mtools` membaca balik 512 byte pola persis. Jalankan `make check-wfail`. **(3) Regresi hijau:** `fat32_test.py` (LFN round-trip via mtools), `boot_test.py` (login), `make check64` (M12 BOOT OK + FS self-test + kbd/heap/blk/fs/cons/gui), `make check-quick` 12/12 di run ini — temuan F0 (race condvar lintas-core) tetap **terbuka** dan tidak diklaim tertutup. **(4) Yang belum:** temuan lain laporan audit (F0, F2–F10) belum disentuh. `OS_VERSION` 38.159; resolusi render 320×240 tidak disentuh; `third_party/q3a/` tidak disentuh. |
