The fix (`q3ref_viewmodel_cycles` already had the gun's own cycles; `emit = total - prep - (vert - vm_vert)`) is the kind of thing the instrument exists to find, and the same class of bug — one pass's cycles hiding in another pass's meter — is exactly why the gun's own fill was split out in v38.124. **(c) What the numbers say, on this host, windowed q3dm1, at the deterministic park the earlier rows establish (`pos=(1060,1431) yaw=-45`, `drawn=3`: a wall filling the frame).** The world's whole draw is **1.0 ms/frame**, of which prep is 0.14 and the raster 0.87 — and `w_vert_us` equals `w_fill_us` to the quantization, i.e. the per-vertex transform is not measurable at that view: **the world's cost there is all fill**, 76,032 shaded fragments for 76,800 screen pixels (101%) at ~20 cycles/fragment. **(d) The 101% is the important number: the overdraw this release was expected to attack is already gone.** v38.121's nearest-first sort leaves the world shading each pixel essentially once, so a better sort key (nearest-vertex depth instead of face-centre distance) has nothing left to win — and THAT is why no reordering, occlusion-culling or coarse-z change shipped here. The one fixed cost that did show up is the view model: 243 triangles into a 144x112 box, 19,081 shaded fragments, **1.6-2.5 ms/frame — ~139 cycles/fragment, ~7x the world's per-fragment cost, at 8.7 pixels per scanline against the wall's ~105.** Its cost is per-triangle and per-scanline setup, not pixels; the same is true of q3dm1's 4,838 patch quads, which are small by construction. That is the lever the next release has, and it is a raster-layout change, not a knob. **(e) And the phase meter's own blind spot is now named.** The reconciliation clause ("charge whatever no phase claimed to `other`") reports its share as `recon=` in the `glsplit` line: **1.4-1.6 ms/frame at the park** — small — but walk windows read `other_ms` up to **16 ms/frame with `idle_ms=0`**, i.e. the walking frame genuinely spends most of its non-GL time somewhere no meter names. That is the next instrument target, stated as a number rather than a theory. Nothing here is claimed as an fps win beyond the HUD trim in (1): the panel was ~7,000 blended pixels per frame on a 320x240 picture (its 124x56 block over 76,800 pixels), and it is a true no-op now (`q3ref_draw_perf_overlay()` returns on the first line unless F3 set `ov_detail`), while the readout that replaced it is ~60 pixels drawn once per frame after the upscale. **(7) One cosmetic guard, and the regression runs.** With F3 on, the panel's own top row and the corner readout would have overlapped — the panel's right-aligned value ends at buffer x 304, which at scale 2 lands exactly under the readout — so the readout is suppressed while the panel is up (the panel shows the same windowed fps in its own first row); a scan of the F3 frame confirms the readout's box (content x 588-636, y 4-20) stays empty while the panel draws. The suites never press F3, so what they exercised is the default path: **q3arena PASS** (1m51s on the first build, re-run 1m45s after the guard) and **q3heavy PASS** (1m29s), re-run at the very end on the tree that carries the instrument and the corrected `emit` formula — **q3arena PASS 96s, q3heavy PASS 80s, both on a 0-warning ISO** (the two dev lines this release adds are only printed every hundredth frame and no suite parses them, which is what that re-run checks), ISO builds 0 warnings, `third_party/q3a/` untouched, and the shipping image is built at `Q3ARENA_SCALE=2` — the user's own configuration, which is the image the pixel evidence above comes from. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.126.
| v38.125 | **"pas lagi nembak kenapa ada item item dah" + "semua texture masih belum di terapin dari quake 3 arena demo": one muzzle flash drawn opaque and one resolver bug — 77 of q3dm1's 94 shaders were already loading, and the 17 checkerboards were two parser mistakes, not missing data (Q3 phase 20).** **(1) The flash was the user's own screenshot, verbatim: a black disc with an orange star over the scene.** id's shader for it — `models/weapons2/machinegun/f_machinegun` in `scripts/models.shader` — is `sort additive ; cull disable ; { map models/weapons2/machinegun/f_machinegun.tga ; blendfunc GL_ONE GL_ONE }`: a star drawn on a BLACK background, meant to be ADDED to whatever is behind it. v38.124 submitted those 24 triangles through the world's opaque path, so the black background painted over the scene — `dst = src` is exactly a black disc with an orange star on it, and nothing about that shape was a placement or scale bug. The flash pass now runs inside `glEnable(GL_BLEND)` + `glBlendFunc(GL_ONE, GL_ONE)` with culling off, restoring both after. This fork can only do additive: `third_party/tinygl/src/misc.c`'s `TGL_BLEND_FUNC` implements `GL_ONE`/`GL_ONE` and the `ONE_MINUS_*` cases, and a `GL_SRC_ALPHA` request falls through to `GL_ONE` — so additive is not merely id's instruction, it is the only blend this rasterizer can express, and it happens to be the one the file asks for. **(2) The evidence is the box's pixels, not the call.** `scripts/q3viewmodel_test.py` now requires, on every sampled frame, that the model's screen box holds **zero near-black pixels** (a black-backed star drawn opaque guarantees them), that the firing samples are bright (mean channel ≥ 200), and that the loader's additive state agrees with the flash's own triangle count — measured `add=1` with 0 near-black pixels (`bright=250`) on a firing sample against `add=0`, `bright=157` on an idle one. **(3) "Semua texture masih belum di terapin" was one bug seen through 17 windows, and its first cause was a doubled extension.** The demo pak ships `textures/skies/killsky_1.jpg`; the script spells `map textures/skies/killsky_1.tga`; the resolver took the script's spelling as a NAME and appended its own extension, so it asked the volume for **`killsky_1.tga.jpg`** and got the placeholder. Every `map` operand that already carried an extension went down that path — sky, lava, the rust trim, the trans skins, the torches. The resolver now probes the script's spelling **verbatim first** (id's own `.tga` is real for most of the map), then the extension-stripped name with **`.jpg` before `.tga`** (which is what the demo actually holds), then the shader name itself; one shared `q3w_strip_image_ext` serves both this and the exported md3 path, so the two can no longer drift. An empty extension now MEANS "exactly as written", which is what makes the verbatim probe expressible at all. **(4) The second cause was the engine's own operand.** Four shaders (the pentagram glow among them) open with `map $lightmap`; `$`-operands are supplied by the renderer and are never images, and the old walk took the first `map` line it saw and stopped — so those four could never resolve. The walk now skips any operand beginning with `$` and keeps reading later stages, and it takes the first real image from `map`, `clampmap`, **or the first FRAME of an `animMap`** (skipping the fps token). It also reads a `blendFunc` written *before* its `map` line, which the demo's own scripts do. **(5) The same walk gave the world the flash's two flags, because the flash and the torches are the same file format.** `q3bsp_shader_decl_t` now carries `additive` and `cullNone` per shader, and `additive` is claimed **only** by a `blendFunc GL_ONE GL_ONE` sitting at the same stage DEPTH that produced the image — the reason is in the data: `pentagram_light1_1K`'s stage 3 IS a `GL_ONE GL_ONE` glow layer over an opaque stage 2, and a parser that read the flag from anywhere in the definition would have made the whole wall additive. `cull none`/`cull disable` anywhere in the definition sets `cullNone`. The renderer reads both into the shader slot at load — the flags survive even when no image resolves — the backface test becomes `!cullNone && backface`, and additive faces are pulled out of the opaque list and drawn **after** it with blending on and `glDepthMask(GL_FALSE)` while the depth test stays on. The tex lines carry `flags=add`, `culloff`, or both; the driver prints `[Q3ARENA] draw flags: additive=N culloff=N` and `addfaces=` in its render totals. **(6) What is still a placeholder, and why that is the honest answer.** `gothic_door/km_arena1archfinalc_mid` genuinely is not in the demo pak (its `finald_mid` sibling is the only variant shipped), and the tool shaders (caulk/clip/weapclip/hint/donotenter/nodraw, all `surfaceparm nodraw`) own **zero** faces — there is nothing there to texture. Nothing else falls back. **(7) What the user will see after a rebuild:** the sky's 16 surfaces resolve to `killsky_1/2.jpg` (clouds, not the purple checkerboard), the lava's 8+8 resolve to `lavahell.jpg`, the torch flames come out additive the way id drew them, and the muzzle flash no longer covers the view. Still absent, stated plainly rather than implied: multi-stage blending (`tcMod scroll/scale/turb`, so sky and lava are static), skybox `env/*` (this sky is `skyparms - 384 -`, no six images to load), and md3 map models (torches, lion head) are still not drawn — all of it out of this release's scope by the user's own choice. **(8) Why two CI suites said "no placeholder" while the real map was full of them, and the fix for that.** q3arena's and q3retail's gates ran against a synthetic script that never spells an extension after `map` and never puts `$lightmap` first, so both passed on a fixture that could not reach either bug. `build_test_bsp.py` now carries four probe shaders behind a `with_probes` switch, one per pattern (`probe_dollar` = `$lightmap` first stage, `probe_anim` = animMap-only, `probe_extension` = script spells `.tga` while the pak ships `.jpg`, `probe_flame` = `GL_ONE GL_ONE` + `cull none`); `build_q3pak.py` defines and stages them — with `probe_ext_target.jpg` present and `probe_ext_target.tga` **deliberately absent**, because staging the spelled file would let the resolver pass by accident, exactly as the demo's real `.jpg` does; `q3retail_test.py` asserts each probe resolves to its intended file and that `probe_flame` reports `add,culloff`. The first q3arena run of this release **failed**, and the reason is the design working: `seed_ext2.sh` seeds the arena WITHOUT probe definitions, so four names had no shader and the suite's no-placeholder gate fired — hence `with_probes=False` by default, which keeps that suite's five-shader arena byte-identical to v38.124 while it now also asserts the negative (`additive=0 culloff=0 addfaces=0`, and `addfaces=` present in the totals line). **(8b) The new probes then failed twice more, once for the harness and once for the engine — both worth recording, because the first hid the second.** The harness one is v38.124's `debugfs` lesson landing on a different file: `seed_ext2.sh` writes the generated arena into the volume itself, `debugfs`'s `write` refuses a name that already exists **while still exiting 0**, and `q3retail_test.py`'s overlay — whose comment claimed "the staged files win" — therefore kept the seeded copy. Invisible for eight releases because both copies were the same five-shader arena; the probe shaders live only in the pak's `.bsp`, so the loaded mesh read `shaders=5` where the pak has 9, the probes had no names to attach to, and the suite failed on `probe_dollar produced no texture line`. The overlay now `rm`s before it writes (the same fix `q3viewmodel_test.py` needed in v38.124), and the mesh check compares the loaded shader count against `len(build_test_bsp.SHADERS)` instead of asserting `> 0`, so a stale copy can never pass silently again. The engine one is the kind only a fixture finds: **`decl_is` folds the TOKEN to lower case and compares the literal verbatim, and two of the new literals were spelled in the file's own mixed case — `"GL_ONE"` and `"animMap"`.** Neither could ever match. The flame's `blendFunc` pair never set `additive` (the census read `additive=0` while `cull none` in the same definition worked, which is the tell), and a shader whose image is named by an `animMap` — how id writes animated fire and sky frames, and there are 9 such names in q3dm1 — resolved to nothing. `decl_is` now carries the warning on itself (the literal must be lower case; an upper-case letter in one is a bug, not a style choice), and the probe run reads `probe_anim -> probe_anim0.tga` with the census at `additive=2 culloff=1` — and with 914 definitions instead of 903, because the `animMap` stages that used to capture nothing now name a file. **(9) The probes' third failure was in the game clock, and it was in neither of the files this release set out to touch.** Chasing why a scheduled jump could read `vel_z=-239` turned up a clause v38.116 left at the end of every rendered frame — `if (now > a_srv_last_ms + 250u) a_srv_last_ms = now;` — meant to drop the drift after a stall. **Moving the REFERENCE is not the same as dropping the DRIFT:** the next frame's catch-up then measured the time since the END of the previous frame (a few microseconds) rather than since its start, so `dt` collapsed to ~0 and the game clock crawled. On this TCG host that read **0.3 ticks per rendered frame at 333 ms/frame** — `t` advancing 300 ms per 20 frames, `srv_ticks=6` per window, an auto-walk that covered 219 units in 165 frames — i.e. the module simulated about a tenth of real time, and v38.119's open verdict that "this VM's TSC and PIT disagree on rate" was this clause, not the clock. The clause is gone, and the spiral it guarded against cannot happen anyway: the tick block caps the accumulator at 125 ms — 2 ticks per frame — so a five-second stall still advances the world by exactly 100 ms. Nothing changes on a host whose frames are under 250 ms, which is why every real session (40+ fps) has always read a healthy `srv_ticks`; the same run now reads `srv_ticks=40 srv_ms=2000` per window, `t=2000` per 20 frames, and the same walk covers 880 units and stops against the level's own wall. **(9b) The scheduled jump now waits for the ground**, because the frame grid says when the driver *wants* to jump while Pmove says whether it *can*: with the clock fixed the two agree again (`jump hit frame=41 vel_z=190 ground=1023`, exactly where v38.122 saw it), but on a starved clock the intent used to be consumed by a tick in mid-air, where PM_CheckJump refuses — and the driver still called that "jump hit" while the suite read its negative velocity as a failed jump. Waiting for `groundEntityNum` is what a player does with the key and makes the one-shot evidence honest at any frame rate; a real key press is untouched (the scheduler's hold is released by the tick, and SPACE still writes `a_up` itself). **(10) Evidence.** All five suites PASS on the shipped ISO: `q3viewmodel` — `parts=3 surf=3 tris=28 chain=1`, firing frame 200 `flash=1 drawn=28`, and "the flash is composited additively, id's way: 1 firing sample(s) each report add=1 with 0 near-black pixels in the box (bright=250) against 2 idle sample(s) with add=0 (bright=157)"; `q3retail` — the staged mesh reads `shaders=9`, all three resolution probes land (`probe_dollar_real.tga`, `probe_anim0.tga`, `probe_ext_target.jpg`), `probe_flame` reports `flags=add,culloff` with the census at `additive=2 culloff=1`, and the staged map renders textured through its script; `q3jump` — `jump hit frame=41 vel_z=190 ground=1023`, landed at frame 100, 40 shots (`ammo_mg 100 -> 60`), the real 267-triangle view model on screen; `q3arena` — 148 frames (913 surfaces, 1826 triangles) in 69.7 s of TCG wall clock, the census empty (`additive=0 culloff=0`) and the additive pass empty (`addfaces=0`) on a map with no shader script, the walk covering 880 units into the level's own wall; `q3vis` — the PVS culling suite, unchanged and green; `q3heavy` — the exclusive-fullscreen suite, which needed a fix of its own (12). **(11) And the answer to the complaint, on the real map.** The demo pak staged into the volume (136 files) and `q3arena q3dm1` typed by hand: **86 of q3dm1's 94 shaders now resolve through id's own scripts, and the 8 that still fall back own ZERO faces** — `noshader` plus the six tool textures (`common/caulk`, `clip`, `weapclip`, `hint`, `donotenter`, `nodraw`, all `surfaceparm nodraw`) plus `gothic_door/km_arena1archfinalc_mid`, the one texture the demo pak genuinely does not ship (only its `finald_mid` sibling). So nothing the player can SEE is a checkerboard any more. The ones the user pointed at: `skies/tim_hell -> skies/killsky_1.jpg` (the sky that was a purple checkerboard), `liquids/lavahell_750` and `lavahellflat_400 -> liquids/lavahell.jpg` (the lava), `sfx/flame1_hell`, `sfx/flame1side` and `sfx/flame2 -> sfx/flame1.jpg` **with `flags=add,culloff`** (id's fire, additive and unculled), `gothic_trim/pitted_rust2_trans -> pitted_rust2.jpg`, `skin/surface8_trans -> surface8.jpg`, `skin/tongue_trans -> tongue.jpg`, `gothic_light/pentagram_light1_1K -> pentagram_light1.jpg` — and the census over the whole map reads `additive=3 culloff=8`, with the three flames the ONLY additive entries: the pentagram's stage-3 `GL_ONE GL_ONE` glow did **not** take the opaque base image with it, which is the stage-depth guard doing its job on real data rather than on the fixture. **(12) One suite failed for a reason that was not this release, and under it was a real ordering bug worth fixing.** `q3heavy_test.py` (v38.119's exclusive-fullscreen suite) came back with `296 distinct colours on screen while fullscreen owned it, against 4 in the frame the renderer produced`. A per-block colour map of the screendump plus a pre-game desktop reference showed the screen was NOT the desktop: it was the game, and the ~290 colours were the **view model** — v38.124's real 267-triangle machinegun with its own 256x256 texture, drawn *after* the ZBuffer histogram this suite compares against (the histogram is taken at the end of the 3D pass; the overlay and the gun are both painted after it). So the bound was calibrated at v38.123, when the screen's only extra colours were the overlay's, and it had been wrong since v38.124 — the suite just had not been re-run against it. The replacement is a better test than the number it retires: the suite now screendumps the DESKTOP before it launches the game and measures how much of the fullscreen screen is still that reference — `100%` at the instant fullscreen is entered, `0.0%` once the game presents, `87.8%` again after ESC returns the window — so "the desktop is gone" and "the desktop is back" become the same measurement read in opposite directions, and a gradient desktop can no longer be confused with a textured gun. **(12b) The ordering bug the investigation turned up is real and is fixed.** `vga_fullscreen_target()` used to claim the screen *before* handing back the buffer — damage everything, set `needs_redraw` — which is a standing invitation for the kernel main loop's swap to land while the game has not written a pixel of the frame yet. Handing over a buffer is not presenting it, and here that is not pedantry: `swap_buffers()` clears the damage rect when it runs, so a swap that lands mid-write presents a torn frame whose not-yet-written rows are also no longer marked dirty — nothing repaints them until the next frame's claim. The claim moved to a new `vga_fullscreen_present()`, called once the upscale is done, and the driver no longer invalidates the game window while fullscreen owns the screen (`wm_invalidate` schedules a composition since v38.118 — right for a window, wrong for a screen the window is not on; ESC re-invalidates it on the frame that hands the screen back). Every fullscreen assertion still passes, and the windowed paths are byte-identical. **(13) Evidence, one more time, with the two new checks:** q3heavy PASS — desktop reference 351 colours, fullscreen 296 with `0.0%` of it identical to that reference, the pin exact (`eye_z=114, drawn=2, tris=4`) across every sampled frame, windowed 564 colours at `87.8%` desktop after ESC, clean shutdown. ISO warning-free, `third_party/q3a/` untouched. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.125. |
| v38.124 | **"Senjata apaan ini? ini mah bukan aset dari quake 3 arena demo, yang bener dong": the corner gun is id's own view model now — 267 triangles, its own 256x256 texture, its own tag-chained muzzle flash — and the asset was on this machine the whole time (Q3 phase 19).** v38.123's row claimed "this machine has no retail pak0.pk3"; that was true of what is STAGED (`build/q3data` holds mapobjects, no `models/`), not of the machine: `assets/q3/pak0.pk3` — 46,853,694 bytes, 1387 files, dated 1999-11-29 — is the genuine Quake III Arena **demo** pak0, and the demo ships the weapon models at `models/weapons2/machinegun/`. It stays out of the repo by rule (`assets/q3/` is in `.gitignore` and the file is untracked), so no id bytes can reach a commit; what ships is a loader. **(1) The format, read by byte offset rather than by casting a struct.** `third_party/tinygl/q3viewmodel.c` follows the layout cited from `third_party/q3a/code/qcommon/qfiles.h:113-209` (MD3_IDENT `('3'<<24)+('P'<<16)+('D'<<8)+'I'`, version 15, header 108 B, frame 56 B, tag 112 B, surface header 108 B, shader 68 B, triangle 12 B, st 8 B, xyzNormal 8 B, `MD3_XYZ_SCALE 1.0/64`) with the one property that is the whole difficulty: **every `ofs*` inside a surface is relative to the start of THAT surface**, so reading them as file offsets yields plausible garbage instead of an error. Frame 0 is the bind pose, and the only frame drawn: a view model is animated by the player's own anims, which is a different piece of work. **(2) Three parts, chained by id's own tags, with a self-check that cannot be faked.** `machinegun.md3` (188 verts / 171 tris, 1 surface `w_machinegun_body`) carries `tag_weapon` at the origin and `tag_barrel` at (9.84,−0.31,0.17); `machinegun_barrel.md3` (129 verts / 72 tris) hangs off that and carries `tag_flash` at (15.72,0.02,0.00); `machinegun_flash.md3` (19 verts / 24 tris) is the muzzle star, placed at that sum — and the body's OWN `tag_flash` independently claims (25.56,−0.30,0.17) against the chain's 25.56, so the loader prints `chain=1` only when the two agree within 1 unit and otherwise drops the barrel and the flash rather than drawing them in the wrong place. **(3) What is here, and what deliberately is not.** Body + barrel + flash is 267 triangles and one image (`machinegun.jpg`, 256×256 baseline JPEG; the flash's `f_machinegun.jpg` is 128×128), and the flash is submitted only while the live weaponstate says WEAPON_FIRING. The HANDS are not here, and the reason is in the file: `machinegun_hand.md3` carries 16 animation frames and three tags but **zero surfaces** (verified against the pak), so the hands retail shows in first person come from the player model (`models/players/<model>/upper.md3` + its `.skin` + `animation.cfg`, `TORSO_ATTACK` 130..136 at 15 fps) — a much larger piece of work, stated here rather than implied by a screenshot. **(4) No scale factor appears anywhere, and that is id's own geometry.** The port already renders a 90-degree horizontal FOV at 4:3, which IS id's default, so one model unit is one world unit as in retail; the pass clears depth instead of using id's depth-range trick (the equivalent this rasterizer has, and the same call id's own `glopClear` uses), writes the model→camera axis mapping out explicitly so the reported screen box is computed with the mapping the draw used, and lights the gun full-bright so it reads the same in a dark corridor and a lit room. **(5) Two bugs, both found by pixels.** The gun first came out as a WHITE SILHOUETTE: `q3w_draw()` ends with `glDisable(GL_TEXTURE_2D)` (q3world_render.c:1370), so a pass drawn after the world rasterizes in the current colour — the view model's pass now enables texturing itself and restores the mode after, and `distinct=3` was the tell (3 colours is a fill, not a texture; the same box reads 295 with id's image on it). And the flash's shader name in the .md3 is the literal file name `models/weapons2/machinegun/f_machinegun.TGA` — **capital TGA**, the one extension in the set a case-sensitive lookup misses — so `q3w_resolve_image()` strips a known extension before the shader-image lookup; that function plus `q3w_upload_image()` and `q3w_release_image()` are now exported from q3world_render.c, and the view model owns its texture ids and frees them on unload. **(6) The fallback is a rule, and the shape of its line was itself a bug.** No .md3 on the volume keeps the v38.123 procedural gun and says so loudly — but the first version of that line printed only `parts=0 rc=N`, which matches no parser, so q3jump's "honest fallback" branch could never fire and CI's legitimate fallback would have looked like a suite failure; the loader now emits **the same field shape either way** (zeros, `rc=`, and the reason), so `parts` decides which branch a suite takes instead of whether the line parses at all. **(7) The suite builds its own model, in id's format, because the demo pak is not redistributable.** `scripts/build_q3pak.py` writes three synthetic .md3 parts (12 + 12 + 4 = 28 tris, with tags chosen so the chain yields muzzle 15.0 AND the body's own `tag_flash` agrees) plus two real JPEGs; `scripts/q3a_data.py --with-viewmodel` stages them through the REAL staging path (10 files, ~48 KB); `scripts/q3viewmodel_test.py` overlays them on the volume and boots the OS. Two of its own bugs are worth recording because both looked like code faults: `debugfs write` refuses a name that already exists, so a stale demo-pack model left on the volume made the first run assert 267 triangles against 28, and the flash image had been written as a TGA under a `.jpg` name, which the loader correctly refused — `parts=2` was the report, not a loader bug. **(8) `flash` was one column doing two jobs, and no single number could answer both.** The line prints every 100th frame while a burst holds BUTTON_ATTACK for 20, so it needs a STICKY `latched` flag for "a tick ran id's own fire path" — but reading that flag as the drawing state made `drawn` look wrong on every non-firing sample: id draws the flash only on the live `ps->weaponstate == WEAPON_FIRING` frame, so `drawn` is `tris` on a firing frame and `tris - flash_tris` on any other (the loader now reports the flash's own count as `flash_tris`), and the two fields are separate, one per question. The suite runs `fire=5` rather than 3 for the same reason: bursts open on a 40-frame cadence and a burst is 20 frames, so 3 bursts ([40,60) [79,99) [118,138)) never overlap a sample at all and the flash's triangles were only ever checked as ABSENT; the fifth opens at frame 196, frame 200 is a sample, and the firing branch is now measured live. **(9) The harness bug, recorded because it hid three assertions from its cause.** `sendkey =` is not a key name in QEMU's table (`equal` is), and the monitor socket is fire-and-forget, so the terminal received `q3arena mectovtest fire3`; the driver's argument walk aborted on that word (by design — a half-understood argument may never half-configure a run), the run silently used the DEFAULT map name with no fire bursts at all, and the suite failed on `drawn` three steps later. The suite now asserts the `args` line FIRST, because a map name is no evidence here: `mectovtest` is also the driver's compiled-in default. **(10) Evidence.** On this machine with the demo pak staged (10 files out of the 46 MB pak), the loader reads `[Q3VIEWMODEL] load base=models/weapons2/machinegun/machinegun parts=3 surf=3 tris=267 tex=256x256 muzzle=255,-2,1 chain=1 barrel=0 flash=0 flash_tris=24`, and every sampled frame reads `model=3 surf=3 tris=267 drawn=243 tex=256x256 box=176,129,319,239 distinct=296` — 243 is 267 minus the flash's 24, because that suite drives no weapon input, and the box is id's receiver, barrel and raked grip in the bottom-right corner of the 320x240 buffer (the screendump confirms the shape; only the hands are absent, per point 3). Suites at ship time, all PASS: q3viewmodel (new — the synthetic model end to end, incl. the firing sample), q3jump (the md3 branch, loader `tris=28 flash_tris=4`), q3arena (167 frames, 3939 surfaces / 7878 triangles submitted in 67.4 s of wall clock, and the real 267-triangle model on the volume), q3vis, q3retail; ISO warning-free outside the pre-existing `ld: warning: ... LOAD segment with RWX permissions`, `third_party/q3a/` untouched. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.124. |

| v38.122 | **Lompat dan nembak: the two usercmd channels the driver never filled — `upmove` and `buttons` — now carry real input, and everything downstream is id's own code (Q3 phase 17).** SPACE jumps (`upmove` 127), C ducks (`upmove` -127), LMB/CTRL fires (`buttons` & BUTTON_ATTACK); `q3vm_fill_usercmd` writes both fields, and from there the port does nothing itself: PM_CheckJump gives `velocity[2] = JUMP_VELOCITY` (270) while grounded and gates the next jump on PMF_JUMP_HELD until the intent is released, and PM_Weapon takes one `ammo[WP_MACHINEGUN]` per shot and raises EV_FIRE_WEAPON, which the server turns into FireWeapon/Bullet_Fire. The headless `q3vm` path is untouched (its command stays the full-throttle walk; upmove/buttons stay 0, so that suite's log shape cannot shift). **The knobs `jump=<n> fire=<n>` schedule the same inputs from the command line** so CI can drive the chain without injecting keys — fixed frame grid, host-speed independent. Writing that exposed a parser defect worth recording because of how it hid: `=` is not in the token walk's word alphabet, and the walk treats anything else as a FATAL, so `q3arena mectovtest jump=4` silently discarded its entire argument — yet `mectovtest` still loaded, because that name is the driver's static DEFAULT (`q3vm_bsp_name`), which made the broken parse look like a working one. The walk now stops at `=` instead of dying, and a word of any other shape followed by `=` is still fatal. **Two determinism fixes the first suite run forced:** (1) the scheduled jump held its intent for one RENDERED frame — but under v38.116 catch-up most rendered frames run zero game ticks, so the intent usually expired unseen; the intent is now held until one game TICK consumes it (`a_srv_ticks_total` moves) and released after, which PMF_JUMP_HELD makes safe, and `a_jumps` counts CONFIRMED jumps rather than raises; (2) after the last fire burst `a_btn` was never cleared — the `if (a_fire_left)` guard meant the player fired forever; the final burst now hands the button back. **The evidence lines are built for the races they could hit:** the every-100th-frame `play` line samples weapon/ammo/health/ground/vel_z straight out of the playerState the module registered at trap_LocateGameData, but it cannot catch a ~23-frame airtime reliably — so the driver also prints a one-shot `jump hit` line at the instant a tick consumed the intent, when velocity[2] is exactly JUMP_VELOCITY and ground is ENTITYNUM_NONE; only PM_CheckJump can produce that pair. The new suite `scripts/q3jump_test.py` (`make check-q3jump`) asserts on the fixture arena: the knobs reached the driver (args line reports `jump=4 fire=3`), `jump hit` reads vel_z>100 with ground=1023, a later `play` line has ground back OFF ENTITYNUM_NONE (the fixture floor is a world brush: ENTITYNUM_WORLD 1022 — the first draft asserted ground==0 and was wrong), `ammo_mg` ends below the spawn 100 with `shots>=1`, health stays sane, and ESC shuts down clean. Running it sharpened two more tools: the `q3vm` suite's "spawned at the map's own point" assertion now allows the couple of units id's own ClientBegin moves the player (it runs a deliberate 100 msec of Pmove at spawn — "run a client frame to drop exactly to the floor" — and with the suite's forward throttle that is ±2 units; the bound is 8, still rejecting the port's old 0,0), and `check-q3jump` passes `--keep-images` through so CI does not pay the 10-minute reseed per suite. Measured on the shipped ISO: `jump hit frame=42 vel_z=190 ground=1023 jumps=1`, `play frame=200 weapon=2 ammo_mg=71 jumps=4 shots=60` (spawn 100 → 71 through PM_Weapon), health 120→108 (id's own spawn +25 and falling damage), ESC quit clean. Suites at ship time: q3jump PASS 61s, q3arena PASS 77s, q3retail PASS 81s, q3vis PASS 89s, q3heavy PASS 63s (one TCG run died waiting for the exclusive-present marker after a killed-by-timeout run left the harness state dirty; PASS on re-run, and the kernel's own serial log shows nothing wrong — recorded as a harness flake, not a code fault), q3vm PASS 33s, q3play PASS 95s. ISO warning-free outside the pre-existing RWX note, `third_party/q3a/` untouched. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.122. |
| v38.121 | **"40 fps and not smooth" was two problems, and the renderer's share of it is fixed by drawing the world in the order id's engine does: front to back (Q3 phase 16).** The user's v38.120 walk on q3dm1 read 37-50 fps with `gl_ms` swinging 164-814 ms per window while `glcyc` held fill at ~97% of the raster cycles and `tris` flat at ~9200/window — the signature of OVERDRAW, not triangle count: faces were emitted in BSP index order, so far walls filled first, near walls filled over them, and every occluded pixel paid the full textured path (texel sample + lightmap mix + blend) before its z-test rejected it. Because the overdraw follows the camera's view, the cost swings view to view — which is exactly why walking felt unstable while standing still (one view, warmed) read 56. **(1) The fix: a front-to-back draw list.** `q3w_draw` runs its existing culls (PVS/backface/frustum, unchanged) once, stores each survivor's index plus its squared camera distance (no sqrt), heapsorts ascending — nearest first — and emits the fans in that order, so the early z-test turns occluded pixels into ~15-cycle rejections instead of ~80-cycle fills. In-place heapsort: O(n log n) worst case, no recursion, no 64-bit divide (this link has no `__udivdi3`); the pool is `kmalloc`'d once per mesh size — the first A/B version ran the cull twice to size the buffer and gave back most of what the sort saved, which is why the single pass matters. Pixels are unchanged (same z-winner per pixel for opaque faces), and the suites' histograms confirm it. `nosort` on the command line restores the v38.120 index order for A/B on one ISO; a new `f2b` diag line reports sort state and pool size, and the args line carries `sort=`. **(2) The A/B that decided it, on the deterministic walk where the user's complaint lives:** sort ON median 66 fps (the limiter's ceiling) with **min 51**, gl 7.4 ms/f; `nosort` median also 66 but **min 28** — the dips ARE the stutter, and the sort removes them. The pinned stand-still view is the honest exception: 56 fps unsorted vs 48 sorted, because a parked camera looking down a corridor has almost no overdraw to reclaim and pays the sort (~2-3 ms/f at this view) instead. Default stays ON: the complaint is walking. **(3) A second bug found on the way, unfixed and documented:** relaunching `q3arena` after ESC-quit fails with id's own `Hunk initialization failed. File system load stack not zero` — the quit path leaves the engine FS stack dirty, so a second launch in the same session cannot boot. One game per boot until that gets its own release. **(4) A self-inflicted harness incident, recorded as the rule it proves:** a `check.py`-run bench recreated `ext2.img` UNDER the user's live VM (their `run.sh` holds the images without `-snapshot`), blanking the staged game data mid-session; reseeded from `build/q3data` (126 files verified) and the bench harnesses now boot with `-snapshot` — the same guard `boot_test.py` always had. Suites at ship time: q3arena PASS 86s, q3vis PASS 93s, q3retail PASS 94s, q3heavy PASS 69s; ISO warning-free outside the pre-existing RWX note, `third_party/q3a/` untouched. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.121. |
| v38.120 | **The perf HUD stopped covering half the game: the user played v38.119 and the readout they were squinting at was a solid 236x140 slab pinning the view's top-right corner — so the overlay was redesigned for the player, not the log (panel ~124x56, half the previous area, right where a frame rate readout belongs without hiding the game).** Four changes, all in `q3ref_draw_perf_overlay` plus the driver's cost comment: **(1) a hand-drawn 4x8 mini font** (26 glyphs: the labels the HUD prints — fps/vm/gl/blit/wm/other — plus digits and space) replaces the 8x16 kernel font, halving every line to 8 px; each glyph was rendered and eyeballed before commit, because the repo's two existing fonts disagree on bit conventions and neither was verifiable from its reference (the first attempt reused TinyGL's `font8x8_basic` and drew mirrored, garbage glyphs — caught by simulating the renderer's plot loop in Python against the header's own constants, not by trusting a comment). **(2) The backing panel is smoked glass now: a 25% alpha blend toward black** (`dst = (3*dst + 0)>>2` per channel, rounding-safe via `0x00FCFCFC`) instead of a solid `0x101018` fill, so the world stays readable through it; the corner nearest the view is chamfered 8 px so the block tapers instead of ending in a slab edge. Blending toward black keeps the suite's pixel evidence sound: text and panel pixels are always *darker* than the scene under them, the histogram's bright/warm buckets can only ever be added to, and the panel colour (0x000000) is a colour any dark scene already produces. **(3) Values are right-aligned to one column** (no more ragged gaps after 1-digit values), fps gets the top line, the bar is 5 px instead of 10 and spans the panel — one 5 px cell per 2 ms of the 48 ms a 20-frame window shows at 43-47 fps, so a heavy frame no longer reads as "half the bar is missing". Bar and text stay solid (only the panel blends): the bar IS the datum, and a blended bar would darken with the scene behind it. **(4) The cost story in the driver comment was updated to match.** What did not change: the overlay still paints every frame after the histogram is read, the snapshot still happens after it, all six numbers are the same window costs the serial perf line carries, and the render resolution stays 320x240 — the HUD is cheaper pixels, not fewer. **A real bug the first build had, caught by pixels and not by the suite:** the font table ships high-aligned nibbles (0x60 = two pixels in the row's LEFT half) while the first renderer mask read the low nibble (0x8>>col), so every glyph drew zero pixels — panel, bar and underline all rendered, text never did, and the q3heavy colour bound still passed because blended panel pixels are scene colours and the bar is an arena texture colour; what caught it was reading the screendump's HUD region as ASCII art against the glyph table. The mask is 0x80>>col and the labels verify pixel-for-pixel in the fullscreen dump (`fps`, `vm`, `gl`, `blit`, `wm`, `other` plus right-aligned values, colour-coded). Verified by building both kernels clean and running the q3heavy and q3arena suites (the former bounds the screendump palette at frame-plus-overlay and exercises the fullscreen present where the HUD rides the exclusive path; the latter covers the windowed walk and the perf-line accounting), both passing. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.120. |
| v38.119 | **The release that went looking for a triangle-amplification bug the renderer did not have, and came back with an honest clock instead (Q3 phase 15).** The plan was to kill a 17-19x "triangle amplification": the user's session log paired `[Q3ARENA] frame=... tris=3929` with `glcyc tris=63402`, and a corridor frame on the bench read `tris=1229` against `glcyc tris=23367`, which looked like TinyGL's own clipper splitting every large face into a forest of pieces that each paid full raster setup. **(1) The world-space clip was built, then measured, then removed.** It was a real implementation — Sutherland-Hodgman in world space against the six planes `frustum_planes` already builds, inward slack, per-attribute lerp, a `noclip` knob so one ISO could A/B both emit paths, and a host-side equivalence test that confirmed the clipped polygon's coverage matched the inside-frustum region of the source quad (0 mismatches over 40401 samples). Then the A/B said no: at one pinned pose on one ISO, `noclip` ran **57 fps with gl 13.4 ms/f where the clip ran 47 fps with gl 17.5 ms/f** — the polygon pass costs more than the splits it avoids — for just 8% fewer raster triangles (17540 against 16120 per 20-frame window), and it changed the parked view's pixels (`warm` 6662 -> 6651, `distinct` 163 -> 160). **(2) The amplification was a units error in the measurement, and this is the release's real finding.** `glcyc tris` is drained with the TSC cycle counters, which are reset once per 20-frame sample window, so it is a 20-FRAME total; the frame line's `tris` is ONE frame's fan count. Dividing the first by the second invents a factor of exactly the window size. Measured per frame, the same heavy q3dm1 view submits ~930 fan triangles and the raster sees ~880 — a *sub*-unity ratio, because back-facing and zero-area triangles are dropped. There was never anything to remove, and the clip is gone; the fan emit is byte-for-byte what v38.118 shipped. **(3) The metering was blind, and is now a real clock.** Every `*_ms` field was the difference of two `get_ticks()` samples, so a 0.4 ms phase read as 0 unless it happened to straddle a 10 ms tick — which is why `wm_ms=0 blit_ms=0 draw_ms=0` appeared in window after window while the compositor was demonstrably working, and why the one question that mattered (where does the 25 ms frame go?) had no answer in the log. `timer_get_us()` (tick milliseconds plus the latched PIT count) now clocks `vm_ms`, `gl_ms` and its three sub-phases, `other_ms` and `idle_ms`, and the WM's window/composite charges. Porting it exposed two accounting bugs worth recording because both produced *plausible* numbers: the GL sub-phases were computed by re-reading the clock after the phase instead of subtracting the timestamps captured around it (so `begin+draw+end` measured from the phase start to *now*, inflating a 10 ms world draw to a reported `gl_ms=600` in a 360 ms window), and the idle accumulator mixed a millisecond timestamp with a microsecond one (58 seconds of "idle" per frame). **The honest breakdown of a heavy pinned q3dm1 view** (`q3arena q3dm1 @318,2253,76,-45,0`, KVM, quiet host, 320x240 into a 642x502 window): **43-47 fps**, `gl_ms` **15-16 ms/frame** of which the world draw is ~all of it (clear 0.3, present snapshot 0.0), `vm_ms` 0.3-0.4, `other_ms` 4-7 with `idle_ms` 0. Inside `other`, the parts are small — the perf overlay 0.4 ms, the frame snapshot 0.15, the counter readback ~0, the histogram 0.1 — so the rest is the frame's un-named work plus whatever the vCPU lost; the TSC counters account for only ~20.6 Mcycles of raster fill per frame (~7 ms at this clock) against a 17.6 ms wall GL phase, so either this VM's TSC and PIT disagree on rate or the vCPU is descheduled inside the phase, and the bench cannot yet separate those two. The compositor's own pass is 1.4 ms/frame. **(4) Trimming the dev log was tried and did not move the needle:** `ps`, `glcyc` and the new `glsplit` sub-fields are now printed every fifth window instead of every window (the sampled `frame`/`pixels`/`perf` lines every suite reads are unchanged), which cuts ~1000 bytes of UART text per window to ~620 — and `other_ms` did not budge, so the 87 ms of serial time per window is real but is not what `other` was made of. **(5) Fullscreen, as asked, with its cost measured rather than assumed.** `q3arena <map> fullscreen` hands the present path to the driver (`vga_fullscreen_enter`): the WM stops compositing the desktop (the kernel main loop swaps the back buffer instead of running `full_redraw`) and the finished 320x240 frame is upscaled straight into it. The render is pixel-identical to the window (`warm` 6662, `distinct` 163 both ways) because the render resolution is untouched — the renderer's own histogram cannot even see the present path, which is why the new suite reads QEMU screendumps instead. **It is slower than the window in a present-bound heavy view: 30-36 fps against 43-47**, and for a plain reason worth writing down: fullscreen writes 786k output pixels (nearest-neighbour upscale) and then swaps the whole 1024x768 screen, while the windowed path upscales 307k, composites 322k and delta-swaps 322k. A bigger image costs present time in proportion, and that is exactly why the window stays the default. ESC returns to the window (the game keeps running, same scene, desktop back) and only a second ESC quits. **(6) A pinned camera, and the argument bug the first version had.** `q3arena [<map>] [fullscreen] [@x,y,z,yaw[,pitch]]` pins the eye and view angles exactly while the module keeps simulating, so a heavy view can be replayed frame for frame; the new suite asserts the pin is exact rather than assuming it. Writing the token walk exposed a real defect: whitespace was never consumed as a separator, so the space after the map name hit the "unknown character" arm and **every multi-token command silently ran the generated arena** — `q3dm1 <anything>` had never worked, and the q3dm1 benches up to that point had only ever passed one token. **(7) New suite:** `scripts/q3heavy_test.py` (`make check-q3heavy`) on the generated arena with fullscreen plus a pinned spawn pose, asserting the argument reached the driver, the exclusive present path was taken, the screendump holds only the game's own palette (10 colours against the frame's 4) while the desktop is gone, the window's 316 colours return after ESC, the pin reproduces identical geometry (`eye_z=114, drawn=2, tris=4`) on every sampled frame, and ESC twice quits cleanly with the kernel alive. Suites at ship time: q3arena PASS 83s, q3vis PASS 93s, q3retail PASS 84s, virtiogpu PASS 27s, q3heavy PASS, ISO warning-free outside the pre-existing `ld: warning: ... LOAD segment with RWX permissions`, `third_party/q3a/` untouched. Two harness facts: `check-virtiogpu` rebuilds a **no-Q3-engine** ISO, so a bench run right after it silently tests a kernel that cannot start `q3arena` (hit again this release), and `/dev/kvm` went from `user:mectov:rw-` to **permission denied** mid-release, so the new suite was validated under TCG, where it passes. **Honest verdict on the user's 25 fps:** their own log is not an amplification, it is a much heavier view plus a wall-clock GL phase that is roughly 2-3x the cycles actually executed. Their parked frames rasterized ~3170 triangles per frame against the pinned heavy fixture's ~880, and per raster triangle their machine was about twice as fast as this bench — so the cost is fill and overdraw in the view they were standing in, and the remaining lever is the raster fill itself. That is where the next release has to start. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.119. |
| v38.118 | **The camera now moves on every rendered frame: server/render clock decoupling (v38.116) left the *pose* still chained to the 20 Hz tick, and the user's own session (median 44 fps, every sampled `srv_ticks=7-8` per 20-frame window) measured exactly what that costs at 44+ fps — 2-3 frames of frozen pose, then a ~16-unit jump, i.e. the "patah-patah saat gerak, di semua map" that survives a fast renderer because it is not a render cost at all (Q3 phase 14).** The fix is the deal Q3's own client makes, minus prediction: the driver keeps the last two tick-end eye poses (origin + yaw/pitch), records the fresh one when the catch-up consumed a tick, and draws from `prev + (cur-prev) * alpha` with `alpha = a_srv_acc / 50` — the wall-clock phase the tick accumulator already holds. Pmove, `commandTime`, and G_RunFrame's tick grid are untouched; the price is the flat ~50 ms of pose latency a VM-local driver accepts (no client-side prediction to hide it, exactly like every Q3 client that ever trusted its snapshots). Yaw lerps through the ±180 wrap, the pitch term is plain, a pose jump > 512 units (teleport/respawn) snaps instead of smearing one frame, and eye height is added at draw time so the poses store the module's origin verbatim. Forward vector comes from the module's own `AngleVectors` through a temp angle vector — no freestanding trig. **Telemetry is honest by construction:** the camera consumes `a_srv_ticks`, so the window count moved to `a_srv_ticks_win` (the glsplit line reports the window, not what the camera ate), and the frame line carries `cam_alpha=` so interpolation is observable instead of assumed. Verified on the deterministic `-vnc` KVM bench of `q3arena q3dm1`: 190 sampled `cam_alpha` values spread 0-89 across six decades (before: every sampled frame drew one of exactly two poses), the walk ends at the same park (`pos_end=(1060,1431)`), game time tracks wall time (~970 ms/s), fps median **53** with gl 13.9 ms/f and other 5.7 (the mover is still non-GL variance, now the *only* remaining mover of frame cost). The fixture suites needed one assertion re-grounded, and the reason is the fix working, not failing: with the camera now interpolating, the arena walk legitimately ends nose-to-wall (16 units off a wall face, staring right at it) where a 64-unit checkerboard fills the frame with 2-3 colours + crosshair = **4 distinct** — a correct close-up the old per-frame `distinct >= 8` gate (calibrated on the pre-interpolation walk) read as a flat fill; the gate is removed because its two other gates still catch what it was built for (a flat fill scores `live < 1000` in the classified-pixel gate, a missing texture never passes the run-wide `distinct >= 10`). **The stutter's second half was not the renderer at all — it was the compositor never being scheduled.** The user's post-interpolation session still read as stutter, and its serial log held the tell: `blit_ms=0 wm_ms=0` in nearly every sampled window while the game rendered 10-25 ms per frame. The chain was broken at one link: `wm_invalidate()` (which the game calls every frame) only set the window's `buffer_dirty`; the kernel main loop composites only when `needs_redraw` is set, and nothing in the WM set it for buffer repaints — so the desktop was presented at mouse-move/HUD-cadence (~5-10 Hz) over a game rendering at 53 fps. A slideshow of a 53 fps game is indistinguishable from a 20 fps game, which is why the complaint ("patah-patah saat gerak, di semua map") survived two renderer releases untouched. The fix is one pulse: `wm_invalidate_unlocked` now sets `needs_redraw`, turning buffer dirt into a scheduled composition (the main loop's 60 Hz frame cap still applies). Measured: the same KVM bench moves from median 53 to **66 fps** (the limiter's own ceiling, gl 4.0 ms/f, idle present again because the main loop hlt-waits between scheduled compositions), and all four suites stay green. Suites at ship time: q3arena PASS 77s (plus the 2× stability re-run earlier in this release), q3retail PASS 69s, q3vis PASS 82s, virtiogpu PASS 26s, ISO warning-free outside the pre-existing `ld: warning: ... LOAD segment with RWX permissions`, `third_party/q3a/` untouched. A harness fact worth keeping: suites and the bench both boot their own ISO, and `check-virtiogpu`'s rebuild is a no-Q3-engine ISO — running the bench right after it silently benches a kernel that cannot start `q3arena` (measured: typed commands echo 3× with zero Q3ARENA serial output); the bench must always rebuild `MECTOV_Q3=1` first. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.118. |
| v38.117 | **Backface culling ships — using the file's own normals, not a winding rule — and the fixture pixel assertions it "broke" turned out to have been counting a z-fight artefact all along (Q3 phase 13).** The plan came from the user's own q3dm1 session (12-24 fps, every window GL-bound, the worst view fill-bound at 39.8 of 92.4 Mcycles/frame), and its first premise died on a loader read: the tessellated patches were **already** frustum-tested quad by quad (each Bezier quad is its own `q3bsp_face_t` with center/radius), so per-quad splitting was done and the real remaining lever was the back half of every wall — filled by the rasterizer and then thrown away by the depth test, with no winding-independent way to reject it earlier. **(1) The cull reads the file, not the winding.** The two `GL_CW`/`GL_CCW` experiments of v38.116 failed because screen winding is the wrong source of truth: q3dm1's planar faces carry per-vertex normals from q3map that can disagree with the triangle order, and the fixture's planar and patch windings disagree with each other. So the loader now copies the file's own `drawVert_t.normal` per vertex (`q3bsp_vert_t.normal`; Bezier-combined on patch quads, exactly like `st`/`lm`), `bsp_face_finish` reduces it to a per-face **front normal** (`frontNormal`, the mean — never touching shading, `normal` keeps the sun's job), and the renderer culls with id's own `R_CullDotTri` form: draw only when `dot(frontNormal, center-cam) < eps`, eps = 4 world units so edge-on walls are drawn rather than guessed away, and a face centered on the eye always draws. Ground truth came from a serial dump of the parked view: the three faces producing the frame's pixels read df = -23, -15, -74 (all "toward the eye", all drawn) while the winding normal on the same faces pointed the other way. **Verified by pixels, not by the new counter:** the deterministic `-vnc` walk compared scene by scene at exact game time — 31 scenes, zero mismatches — and the parked histogram is byte-identical to v38.116 (`warm=8062 bright=69 distinct=21`), with `back=1927/6780` faces never transformed (gl 5.6 -> 1.9 ms/f measured mid-release; the shipped ISO's KVM bench still reads median 66 fps, gl 3.1 ms/f at the park). **(2) The fixture suites failed on honesty, and the fix was to make the assertions honest.** With culling live, q3arena/q3retail failed "walls never appeared", but the A/B said walls were never the evidence: cull off lifted `warm` from 0 to **26 px** — and a dump of the drawn faces plus an offline projection showed the interior walls were drawn correctly in both cases; the 26 "warm" pixels were the wall tops (normal +Z, light 0.89) z-fighting the ceiling base at z=256, invisible from the eye, and the step bucket was counting the same class of artifact. The pixel line therefore gains a **`wall` bucket** — the wall texture's actual shaded colour (dark orange-brown, r>g>b: the fixture's sandstone at its 0.4 baked sun is (67,54,38), a staged q3dm1 wall under lightmap shading lands in the same test) — `stepgreen` is re-ground to the real step palette (the old g>80 threshold only the artifact cleared), and both suites assert `wall >= 200` px instead of a z-fight's 26. **(3) Kept and rejected, with reasons:** `-O1` stays (v38.116 measured `-O2` slower on the same view), the `misc.c` glFrontFace flag fix stays (the cull here is a dot product and never needed it, but the fork's op was genuinely broken), the frame line's `drawn + culled == numFaces` contract is preserved by counting `back=` separately, and the TCG-vs-KVM ACL note (`/dev/kvm` granted per-session by logind) is a harness fact, not a regression: correctness suites are TCG-native and all four PASS. Regression at ship time: q3arena PASS 102s, q3vis PASS 102s, q3retail PASS 222s, virtiogpu PASS 26s, ISO warning-free outside the pre-existing `ld: warning: ... LOAD segment with RWX permissions`, `third_party/q3a/` untouched. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.117. |
| v38.116 | **`q3arena q3dm1` runs at 66 fps: the user's "can it do 60?" was answered by measuring what actually capped the frame — and the cap was the *clock*, then a frustum test that had been agreeing to cull nothing (Q3 phase 12).** The session that asked ran at 7-20 fps, and the first thing the measurement killed was its own premise: `fps min/median/max = 19/20/21` is a clock signature, not a workload one. `TIMER_HZ` is 100, so every `*_ms` field in the log below 10 ms is a rounding artifact and fps can only come from frames per wall second; the old loop paced the *tick* and the *frame* off the same 50 ms budget (`a_next_ms += 50`), and a v38.115 session log shows what that costs in a view the rasterizer could only put out in 32.6 ms: `gl_ms=32.6 other_ms=7.5 idle_ms=11.2 sum_ms=51.8` -> 19.3 fps, i.e. 11 ms of every frame spent waiting for a clock whose work was already done. **(1) The server clock and the render clock are now separate.** `G_RunFrame`/`G_ClientThink` still run on the 50 ms grid — Pmove physics, `commandTime`, and the module's own demo gate see exactly the tick sequence a 20 Hz server produces — but the ticks are caught up *at the top of each rendered frame* from the wall clock (`a_srv_acc`, clamped at 125 ms so a stall caps at two ticks instead of spiralling into a slow-motion replay), and the render loop's only delay is a 15 ms frame limiter (`RENDER_MIN_MS`, i.e. 66.7 fps) so the desktop keeps its timeslice. Same map, same walk: `sum_ms` 51.8 -> 22.0 ms/frame, 19.3 -> 45 fps — with the rasterizer untouched, because the clock was the cap. **(2) The pixel histogram runs on sampled frames, not every frame.** It read the finished 320x240 ZBuffer once per frame to fill the `pixels cyan/warm/.../distinct` line — measured 7.4 ms of `other` per frame at 20 fps, more than the entire VM step; it now runs on the frames that actually print (`frame % 20 == 0`) and `other_ms` is 4.5 ms/frame. **Two new diagnostics, because the next lever had to be picked from data rather than from the obvious.** `[Q3ARENA] glsplit` splits `gl_ms` into the viewport+clear, the world draw, and the present snapshot, and reports what the two decoupled clocks did over the window (`srv_ticks`, `srv_ms`, `glfps`); then `[Q3ARENA] glcyc` goes under that, using `rdtsc` accumulated inside TinyGL itself — `vert` = every `glVertex3f` (transform + texture mapping + the clip cascade + the raster nested in it), `fill` = the raster call alone, `tris` = the triangles that reached the raster — because a 10 ms kernel clock cannot split a 35 ms GL phase and the freestanding link has no `__udivdi3` (the units are kcycles/frame via shifts only, and one window can exceed 2^32 cycles). **(3) The data picked the lever, and it was not the per-pixel loop.** The split read setup 65% / fill 35%, and the raster counter read **5430 triangles emitted per frame against ~68 that reached the raster** — because v38.113's frustum "quorum" (drop a face only when the PVS *and* the frustum agree) could never drop a face the PVS kept, and the PVS is per-cluster: ~99% of that setup was geometry outside the view cone, transformed and clipped in full so the clipper could throw it away. The bounding-sphere test is gone, replaced by an exact per-face test: a face goes only when ALL of its **own vertices** lie behind ONE of the six planes (2 units of slack). It cannot drop a partially visible face, which is the property the sphere test lacked and the reason the quorum existed (`pvs_kept` and its per-frame copy are gone with it). Measured at the same deterministic view: emitted faces 2415 -> 3, GL cycles 15.77 -> 6.3 Mcycles/frame with the **fill unchanged** (6.06 -> 6.03 Mcycles — the same pixels, the same cost), `gl_ms` 17.4 -> 4.3, `draw_ms` 16.9 -> 3.8, median fps 47 -> 66 (the limiter's own ceiling). **Verified by pixels, not by the count that changed.** The frame histogram is taken from the finished ZBuffer, so the pre-cull and post-cull runs were compared **scene by scene at matched game time**: 68 scenes, zero mismatches, and at the parked view both runs read the identical `cyan=0 warm=8062 stepgreen=0 violet=0 bright=69 patch=0 sky=0 distinct=21`. Suites: q3arena PASS 90s, q3vis PASS 134s, q3retail PASS 148s. **Three honest notes.** (1) Windows during the walk still read 44-56 fps, and those windows are guest work *outside* the GL phase (`other_ms` 7.5-12 ms/frame there, `gl_ms` a couple of ms) — the renderer now spends 96% of its GL time in the fill it cannot avoid, so the next lever is the compositor/overlay, not the rasterizer. (2) `-O2` for the whole TinyGL tree was tried and **rejected on measurement**: same view, same build otherwise, `gl_ms` 17.4 -> 27.1. `-O1` stays, now for a measured reason instead of an inherited one. (3) The fork's `glFrontFace` was storing the raw GL enum (2304/2305) into the 0/1 flag `clip.c` XORs, so every earlier "turn culling on" experiment had measured a silent no-op — a real bug, fixed in `misc.c`; backface culling itself stays OFF because with the flag fixed, planar walls survive `GL_CW` while the tessellated patches survive `GL_CCW`, and two windings that disagree are a milestone, not a tuning knob. **A harness finding worth writing down:** the suites launch apps by clicking the desktop through QEMU's monitor, and that click grabs the pointer — after which the *user's own mouse* steers the game (measured: yaw -45 -> -107 within one run), so two "identical" headless runs are not comparable at all and an fps number taken that way is not reproducible. The bench harness now starts the VM under `-vnc` (no local window, hence no host mouse path) and reproduces exactly: the same walk, the same park at pos=(1060,1431) yaw=-45, the same pixels in every run. **(4) The in-window perf HUD reaches the screen for the first time since v38.113.** Chasing a screendump that showed the parked view but no HUD found the cause in this release's own subject: v38.113 moved the compositor's snapshot into `q3ref_end_frame()`, and the driver paints the overlay into the ZBuffer *after* the histogram has been read — so the snapshot was always HUD-free while the log kept describing an fps counter the user could not see. The snapshot is now its own step (`q3ref_present_frame()`, called once the HUD has landed, and by the older `q3_client.c` path too), which also means one fewer copy than a second snapshot would cost and that `r_in_frame` covers the overlay as well. Verified in the screendumps that motivated it: the panel's `0x101018` now covers 23.4% of the window with 0.9% white text, and a re-run of the frame counter still reads median 64-66 fps. Regression at ship time: q3arena PASS 90s, q3vis PASS 98s, q3retail PASS 124s, ISO warning-free outside the pre-existing `ld: warning: ... LOAD segment with RWX permissions`, `third_party/q3a/` untouched. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.116. |
| v38.115 | **The kernel's first MODERN virtio-pci driver, and the first one that is not a disk: `virtio-gpu`. The device had to be measured before it could be driven, and the measurement contradicted the plan — `virtio-gpu-pci` has no legacy interface at all.** The assumption going in was that the legacy PIO transport `virtio_blk.c` has driven since v38.78 would carry over. `info pci` says otherwise: with `disable-modern=on` a virtio-blk-pci in the same QEMU run still appears as 1AF4:1001 with `BAR0: I/O at ...`, while virtio-gpu-pci stays 1AF4:1050 with only BAR1 (MSI-X) and BAR4 (the region block) and no I/O BAR under any flag. So `src/drivers/virtio_gpu.c` speaks virtio 1.x: walk the device's vendor capabilities for the common-config / notify / ISR / device-config regions, negotiate the 64-bit feature word — accepting `VIRTIO_F_VERSION_1` (bit 32), without which a modern device marks itself FAILED, plus `VIRGL`/`EDID` when offered and nothing else — publish the rings' physical addresses through the common config, enable queue 0, and notify with a memory write at `notify_base + queue_notify_off * notify_off_multiplier`, with the available ring's `NO_INTERRUPT` flag keeping INTx quiet (poll-only, like every other driver here). **Two mistakes were paid for and are now comments in the code.** The capability's `offset` lives at +8, not +5 — `struct virtio_pci_cap` carries an `id` byte and two padding bytes after `bar` — and reading the padding produced a garbage region address plus the honest log line `region unreachable for a 32-bit kernel (bar=0)`; and the PCI config-access cap (type 5, bar 0, offset 0) must be skipped rather than validated as a region. On the real boot path SeaBIOS places the 64-bit prefetchable BAR4 at **0xFE000000**, below 4 GB, so a 32-bit kernel can address all four regions, and the driver refuses any region it cannot address whole instead of truncating an address. **The milestone is verified by pixels, not by "the driver loaded".** At init the driver builds a 128x128 test resource (a magenta marker plus a red/blue checkerboard in 16x16 blocks), hands it over — create 2D -> attach backing -> transfer-to-host 2D — puts it on scanout 0 and flushes it. `scripts/virtiogpu_test.py` attaches its own `virtio-gpu-pci`, recomputes the pattern's FNV-1a from its own definition of it (agreeing with the driver's `ck=0x63475DC5`), and then asks QEMU's monitor for a `screendump` **of that device's own head** (not the machine's `-vga` console): the dump is 128x128 and matches the pattern in **all 16384 pixels** — guest memory -> host resource -> scanout -> pixels, end to end. The new `gpustat` builtin reports the same state in the window and mirrors one `[GPU] gpustat:` line to serial, and the suite requires that line's checksum to be the self-test's, so the command is proven to read live driver state rather than a constant. **3D is probed honestly, and the probe found the constraint the renderer work will live under.** With `MECTOV_GPU=gl` (`virtio-gpu-gl-pci` under `-display egl-headless`) the device offers and we accept virgl, answers `capset0 id=1 ver=1 size=0x134`, and the driver's 3D round trip — transfer-to-host 3D, poison the guest buffer with 0xCD, transfer-from-host 3D, compare — returns the pattern byte-exact (`readback: 3d=ok ck=0x63475DC5`, also visible in `gpustat` as `readback=ok`). That path matters because of a measured limitation worth writing down before a line of GPU rendering is written: **QEMU cannot screendump a virgl console** (`Error: no surface` — the scanout lives in a host GL texture and egl-headless keeps no readable surface for it), while the same command happily returns pixels for the 2D device. On a GL host the readback path is therefore not a nicety but the only way to see what the GPU produced, and the suite's `--gl` mode accepts it in place of the screenshot — but only when the round trip really returned the pattern. On the plain 2D device the driver correctly reports `readback: 3d=skip no-virgl-feature`. Regression at ship time: virtiogpu 26s PASS, boot 12s PASS, login 27s PASS, ISO warning-free outside the pre-existing `ld: warning: ... LOAD segment with RWX permissions`, `third_party/q3a/` untouched. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.115. |
| v38.114 | **The map is lit by its own lightmaps: the `.bsp`'s nine baked 128x128 RGB pages are loaded, sampled bilinearly at every vertex's own page-space coordinate, and modulated into the face TinyGL draws — so a level's lighting is q3map's data instead of the fixed sun this port invented in v38.108 (Q3 phase 11).** Every earlier release shaded a face from its own normal against `(0.45, 0.35, 0.82)`, which is honest for a generated arena with no lightmap lump and flat-grey for a retail map: q3dm1 ships `LUMP_LIGHTMAPS` and nothing looked at it. **The lump was measured before a line of loader was written, and the measurement changed the plan.** A first count read the lump as 3456 x 128 bytes and the fear was ~170 MB of pages against a 56 MB heap; the header says `filelen=442368`, and 442368 / (128*128*3) is exactly **9 pages** of id's `LIGHTMAP_SIZE`, so no streaming, no downsampling, no per-surface budget — the cap is a heap guard (`Q3BSP_MAX_LIGHTMAPS` 64 = 3 MB) and q3dm1's whole lighting costs 432 KB. **The file's coordinates are already what a sampler needs**, so nothing is projected: a surface names `lightmapNum` and fills `lightmapX/lightmapY/lightmapW/H` of that page, and every `drawVert_t.lightmap` is PAGE space (0..1 across all 128 texels), which the loader verifies by construction — q3dm1's surface 113 (page 4, X=87, W=19) has vertices at 0.6895..0.8242, i.e. texels 88.2..105.5, inside 87..106. Patches get their coordinate the same way their texture coordinate does, through the SAME Bernstein weights (v38.109's tessellator evaluates `xyz`, `st` and now `lm` in one pass), which is exact for the same reason `st` is: q3map writes both as linear functions of position, so a curved arch is lit by its own gradient rather than one flat quad colour. **The pages are dark on purpose, and id says why**: raw page mean is `33,22,18` because id brightens lightmaps at upload — `tr_map.c R_ColorShiftLightingBytes` shifts each byte by `r_mapOverBrightBits - tr.overbrightBits`, which is 1 on gamma-capable hardware and 2 when the card cannot do gamma (this port's case: a software rasterizer with no ramp), with id's own overflow rule (when any channel passes 255 the whole texel is scaled by 255/max, so a torch keeps its hue instead of clipping to white). That shift is applied once, at load, exactly as id applies it at upload, and the log reports both ends of it: `[Q3ARENA] lightmaps: pages=9 dim=128 bytes=442368 shift=2 raw=33,22,18 lit=6747 unlit=33 litmean=152,96,82 dropped=0`. **The renderer cost is a colour per vertex, not a texture pass.** TinyGL draws one texture per primitive and Q3's lightmap stage is a second one, so the product has to be taken before the draw: the loader samples each vertex's coordinate (bilinear, clamped at the page edge like `GL_CLAMP`), stores the result in the vertex, and `q3world_render.c` emits one `glColor3f` per vertex — TinyGL interpolates those per pixel through `TGL_FEATURE_LIT_TEXTURES`, which is the mechanism the sun already used. `glShadeModel` becomes `GL_SMOOTH` ONLY for a mesh that has lightmaps (`GL_FLAT` collapses a gradient to one vertex's colour); a mesh without a lump keeps `GL_FLAT`, one flat colour per face, and therefore draws pixel for pixel what v38.113 drew — the fixture suites' own logs say so: `[Q3ARENA] lightmap faces: pages=0 dim=0 lit=0 unlit=74 dropped=0`. The light is resolved once per map, not per frame, so the draw path grew no sampling and only one extra API call per emitted vertex. **Verified on the retail map, live (headless TCG, `q3arena q3dm1`)**: `lit=6747 unlit=33` of the mesh's 6780 faces (the 33 are the triangle-soup surfaces q3map emits without a page), and the session's own totals — `frames=205 faces=560257 tris=1249391 wall_ms=320590 litfaces=2400 litfaces_total=556869` — mean **99.4% of every face drawn in the whole run was modulated from a lightmap page**, with the renderer's histogram moving the way illumination should: `warm` pixels (its bucket for `r > g+15 && g > b+10 && r > 60`) run 2869..13390 across the session where the same walk measured 89..7947 on the pre-release build, `distinct` (4-bit colours) reaches 168 from a peak of 101, `sky=0` throughout (no clear colour, so no tear), and the culling numbers identical to v38.113 (`drawn=2415`/`3461`, `cull_pvs=82`/`352`, `cull_frustum=3319`/`4365`) because none of this release touches visibility. **Two honest notes on what the pixels now look like.** (1) A dark map gets darker in the places q3map did not light: at the same stationary view (pos=(1060,1431) yaw=-45, the walk both sessions end at) the window crop's mean luminance falls 33.7 -> 22.4 against the v38.113 ISO's own live screendumps and the share of pixels under luminance 40 rises 80% -> 92%, while the warm share of the crop rises 4% -> 59% — the old sun never went below 0.40 by construction, whereas the baked light does (28.8% of q3dm1's sampled vertices land under 60 after the shift), and real Q3 fills the same dark data in with the dynamic lights (muzzle flashes, lava, torches) that this port has no server to send yet. The numbers are in the release rather than smoothed over, because "the map looks dark" and "the map is lit from the file" are both true here and the second one is the property that generalises. (2) Frame cost did not regress: 205 frames in 320 s against v38.113's 134 in 322 s in the same harness on the same host, with the same `drawn` counts. Regression at ship time: q3arena 82s PASS, q3vis 87s PASS, q3retail 64s PASS (each rebuilt ISO warning-free outside the pre-existing `ld: warning: ... LOAD segment with RWX permissions`), `third_party/q3a/` untouched. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.114. |
| v38.113 | **The user's retail session drove this release: q3dm1 ran at 5–19 fps under KVM, and the three things the player felt all traced to real defects, each fixed and each now asserted by evidence.** (1) **The player never moved — the game module's clock started 80 seconds ahead.** `q3arena_frame` had called `GAME_INIT` with `Com_Milliseconds()` as `levelTime`, so after the ~80 s of texture decoding q3dm1 needs the module's `level.time` began at ~80540 while the driver's per-frame clock started near zero — and id's own `ClientThink_real` gate (`msec = cmd.serverTime - ps.commandTime; if (msec < 1) return;`) rejected every movement command until wall time caught up, which the old 300-frame session cap never allowed. The arena CI suite had never seen it because its tiny map loads in a blink; the fix is the one id's own server uses (`svs.time` starts at 0): `GAME_INIT` gets 0, verified by a `ps` diagnostics line (`cmd_time` == `commandTime`, velocity and groundEntity changing) and by the player actually walking — spawn (212,2360) to (1060,1513) under auto-walk, sliding along walls with id's own Pmove. The session cap moved with it (300 frames → 3600 / a 600 s wall budget; ESC is still the normal exit), because at 5–7 fps the old cap felt exactly like a forced close. (2) **The PVS guard was wrong about id's own data — culling never ran on a retail map.** The leafsurface sanity check (`total > 4×numFaces + 64` disables culling) treated 31828 face references for a 6780-face mesh as corruption; id's `cm_local.h` indexes `cm.surfaces` by RAW surface index ("non-patches will be NULL", `cm_load.c` sets `numSurfaces` from the whole lump) and a leaf's list legitimately repeats surfaces (113 patch surfaces appear in many leaves). The cap now accepts the raw-index + duplicate semantics; on q3dm1 the loader reports `leafs=2109 clusters=942`, the leaf walk agrees with `CM_PointLeafnum` at all 9 samples, `cull_pvs` reaches 4365 faces per frame, and `drawn` falls from 5768 to 2415 — 2.4× less raster work per frame, on top of the v38.112 design rather than instead of it. (3) **The "blue floor" screenshots were a tear, not a culling bug — and the first "fix" was a lesson in its own right.** The in-game histogram (read after `end_frame`) always showed a complete scene with `sky=0`, while screendumps showed 85–99% clear colour `RGB(25,45,114)`: the WM recomposites windows on its own schedule, and with one 3D frame taking seconds it copied the framebuffer MID-RASTER — the new frame's finished top rows above the clear colour of the not-yet-rastered rest. Declining blits while rendering (`r_in_frame`) was the obvious fix and the wrong one: under TCG the compositor never runs outside the frame, so the window froze on a blank buffer (a second run proved it — `distinct=12`). The shipped design is a presentation buffer: `end_frame` snapshots the finished pbuf (one flat 320×240 copy, HUD included) and the compositor always scales from that snapshot — a tear is impossible by construction, and the same live run that froze at `distinct=12` now reads `distinct≈10.7k` with 0.0% clear colour in all three screendumps. **Bonus: a real memset bug died.** Chasing the tear found `memset_l` in `third_party/tinygl/src/zbuffer.c` running `n = count >> 2` iterations of FOUR 32-bit stores — sixteen pixels per four-pixel group, four rows past the caller's row on every `ZB_clear`, smearing the clear colour over geometry the raster had just drawn; both the 32-bit and 16-bit variants now store exactly `count` elements. **Resolution: DOOM-style integer upscale.** The render stays 320×240 (the fps does not move), but `Q3ARENA_SCALE=2` (now the default in `quake.sh`) blits every pixel 2× into a 642×502 window — window `rect=(191,119) 642x502 content=320x240` in the log, `q3ref_set_blit_scale()` in the renderer, geometry still centred by the driver. **Suite harnesses went size-aware**: `scripts/q3_images.py` sizes the ext2 volume by the staged payload (512 MB when >8 MB, as `run.sh` already ruled) after 16 MB images made q3arena/q3retail fail with `missing=1` once the 8.9 MB q3dm1 staging rode along. Regression at ship time: q3arena 81s PASS, q3vis 93s PASS, q3retail 63s PASS, both ISO variants 0 warnings outside the pre-existing RWX note, `third_party/q3a/` untouched. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.113. |
| v38.112 | **The map's own visibility data decides what is drawn: the renderer locates the camera in the `.bsp`'s own tree, walks q3map's PVS, and culls against the projection's six planes — so the surfaces a frame submits become what the map's compiler said was reachable from there (Q3 phase 10).** Every earlier release submitted every face of the mesh on every frame — 74 of 74 for the generated arena, and for a retail q3dm map that is thousands — because an empty visibility lump was treated as "draw everything", which is right but useless at retail scale. Two stages now run per frame, counted separately. **PVS** (`third_party/q3mectov/q3bsp.c`) parses LUMP_PLANES/NODES/LEAFS/LEAFSURFACES and the VISIBILITY lump, locates the camera's leaf with `q3bsp_leaf_for_point()` (the same `d >= 0 -> children[0]`, `leaf = -1 - child` walk id's `CM_PointLeafnum_r` makes), reads that leaf's cluster row out of the bit-packed matrix, and marks the faces of every cluster the row names. **Frustum** (`q3world_render.c`) rejects what the projection cannot show, with the six planes built from the camera basis AND the backend's own `glFrustum` tangents — `q3ref_begin_frame` hands its `-sx, sx, -hh*sx, hh*sx` values down, so the cull is measured against the frustum that was actually installed rather than a second guess at it. The visdata names SURFACES where the renderer draws FACES (one `MST_PATCH` surface became up to 32 quads in v38.109), so the loader records each surface's face range as it builds and translates the lump through it; the lump's own header is validated before a byte of it is indexed (`clusterBytes >= (numClusters+7)/8`, `clusterBytes <= 16384`, `numClusters <= (visLen-8)/clusterBytes` — the arithmetic that keeps a hostile or truncated lump from walking off the bitvector), the trees are capped at 0x10000 planes/nodes/leafs, and **the second implementation of the tree is checked against the first**: `bsp_vis_check_against_cm()` compares `q3bsp_leaf_for_point()` with id's `CM_PointLeafnum()` at sampled face centres and, on any disagreement, turns culling OFF and says so, because a tree that quietly disagrees is worse than no culling at all. **Two frustum sign bugs died on the way, both found by a count rather than by eye.** (1) The far plane is `-(o + FAR)`, not `-(o - FAR)`: inverted, it culls the ENTIRE level, and the arena suite's "the most faces a frame drew was 0" is what said so. (2) The sign belongs on the BASIS vector for the second plane of each pair (`-right + tan_hx*fwd`, `-up + tan_hy*fwd`); writing `±tan*fwd` on an unchanged basis leaves one plane per pair with its interior on the wrong side, and at 45 degrees yaw the pair collapses to `x = cam.x` / `y = cam.y`, so half the level is culled wherever the camera looks — at the spawn pose exactly the north-east half, i.e. the step block and the whole curved cove (the arena's missing step and the retail suite's "the curved surface never appeared on screen" were one bug).
