Serial output is the only debugger this kernel has, so the frustum is dumped once per run (`[Q3ARENA] frustum: cam=(-384,-384,129) fwd=(707,707,0)` plus its six planes) — and its first version printed those normals with `(int)`, which turned a **correct** up plane of `(0.530, 0.530, 0.999)` into `0 0 0` and cost a round of "is the vertical pair degenerate?"; scaled by 1000 it now reads `[Q3ARENA] plane 2: 530 530 999 -278`. The frame line grew `vis=marked/total cluster= cull_pvs= cull_frustum= planes=`, and the arena's own line is the counter-evidence that a map WITHOUT vis data is untouched: `[Q3BSP] vis: planes=23 nodes=1 leafs=1 faces-in-leafs=74 clusters=0 (no PVS lump: every face drawn)` with `vis=-1/74 cluster=-1 cull_pvs=0 planes=6` — the same 74 faces and the same `drawn=70 tris=140 culled=4` on frame 20 that its suite already asserted. The fixture is new because the arena cannot demonstrate any of this: `scripts/build_test_bsp.py` also writes `mectovvis.bsp` — the same brushes, surfaces and collision, but a two-leaf tree split at x = 100 and a real two-cluster lump where cluster 1 sees only itself and cluster 0 sees both (26 faces west, 48 east, spawn at (-384,-384) in the west half). `build()` returns that split's stats as a seventh value, which `build_q3pak.py` needed one line for. New `scripts/q3vis_test.py` (check.py row `q3vis`, `make check-q3vis`, a step of the CI quake3 job) boots, types `q3arena mectovvis`, and asserts: the mesh came from the staged fixture; the loader kept the FILE's tree and lump (`planes=24 nodes=1 leafs=2 clusters=2`, 74 faces reachable from the leafs); its leaf walk agreed with `CM_PointLeafnum` at all 9 sample points; at the spawn the marked count is exactly the 26 west faces **computed from the generator** rather than hard-coded; the walk crosses the split, the cluster changes 1 -> 0, and the marked count rises to 74; on EVERY sampled frame `marked + culled-by-PVS == the mesh's face count` and `planes == 6`; and the culled frames are still the level rather than a disappearance (>1000 classified pixels, at least 8 distinct colours, clear colour under a quarter of the frame). Two bugs were the suite's own, and both are recorded because their shape is the general one: its frame tuple read `culled` as `marked` (a regex group off by one) and failed with the impossible `vis=51/26`, and the crossing does not land on a fixed frame index because the walk is wall-clock paced (x = 47 at frame 80 in one run, x = 112 in another), so it now waits for a cluster-0 frame marker instead of a frame number. Measured on the dev VM: q3vis PASS (at the spawn `marked=26/74` with `cull_pvs=48`, then `marked=74/74` once the walk crosses the split; the frame it crosses at and the run's totals move with the host's speed — 82 sampled frames / 2064 faces in one run, 101 / 2491 in another — which is exactly why the suite waits for evidence instead of for a frame number, and why the numbers a reader can check in any log are the structural ones), q3arena PASS (74 faces, `x -384 -> 496`, 123 frames, 6023 surfaces), q3retail PASS, q3vm/q3gl/q3play PASS, 0 warnings in both ISO variants, `third_party/q3a/` untouched. The retail half of this phase is **data-blocked and says so**: booting a real q3dm map needs the demo's `pak0.pk3`, which nobody may commit here, so the README now documents the legal path end to end under "Game data" (the archive.org demo with its md5/sha1, where to drop `pak0.pk3`, and how `q3a_data.py` stages one map's closure). Recorded so the search is not repeated: the InstallShield self-extractor id shipped for the demo (`Q3ADemo.exe`, VISE-compressed overlay) is opaque to 7z, unshield, cabextract and a zlib-stream scan — the same data is a plain zip on archive.org. **The regression also turned up a harness defect worth its own paragraph.** `q3arena` failed with `textures/mectovtest/curve resolved to textures/mectovtest/curve_jpg.jpg (jpg), expected the direct textures/mectovtest/curve.tga fallback` — not from the kernel, but because `q3retail`'s synthetic pak0 (whose `scripts/mectovtest.shader` REBINDS that name to a JPEG) had been overlaid onto the same `ext2.img` and the arena suite reused whatever volume it found. That is v38.111's stale-ext2-tree lesson one suite over: state that survives a run and quietly answers a later suite's question, so the suite's verdict depends on what ran before it. The fix is shared and small — `scripts/q3_images.py` (`fresh_images()` + `seed_volume()`): the suites whose assertions depend on the volume's contents now format both drives and seed them in-run, and `q3retail` uses the same helper for the images it already recreated. The fix is verified the honest way: the failing volume was left on disk, and `make check-q3arena` then PASSED (91s) because the suite built its own. One flake is reported rather than hidden: `make check-q3tgl` failed once with "shot1 does not show rendered gears" (the screendump caught a partially composited frame — the blue gear present, green absent, `blue=7` out of five tries) and PASSED on an immediate re-run; that is the known v38.104 screendump-timing weakness, untouched by this release. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.112. |
| v38.111 | **The retail-map path: `q3arena <map>` loads a real .bsp out of a staged pak0, resolves its shaders through `scripts/*.shader`, and decodes the JPEGs those scripts name (Q3 phase 9).** Every earlier phase drew the one generated arena, whose textures are TGAs sitting exactly where the .bsp points — a world with no *naming indirection* in it. A retail q3dm map has three layers the arena did not: the game data arrives as a `pak0.pk3` (id's own zip) that must be staged onto the volume, the .bsp's shader lump only NAMES textures (`textures/q3dm1/floor`) and the file that name means is decided by a shader script shipped beside the map, and retail texture sets ship JPEGs (id's `LoadJPG`), which nothing in this kernel could decode. All three are now real. `q3arena <name>` (any `[a-z0-9_]` map on the staged volume) builds its BSP paths at RUNTIME in `q3_vm.c` (`[Q3VM] map arg: 'q3dm1'`) instead of the macro-expanded single map name, so one binary plays any staged map. `scripts/q3a_data.py --pak <your own pak0> --map q3dm1` is the user-facing path to stage a map onto the volume — and because pak0 is not redistributable, `scripts/build_q3pak.py` builds a synthetic one from the generated arena (15 zip entries: the .bsp, a `scripts/mectovtest.shader`, a JPEG, the TGAs, a sky box), which is what CI stages. Free wins: the three pieces are separable, and staging is *data*, so the map a test plays is whatever its pak0 contains. **The JPEG decoder** (`third_party/tinygl/q3jpeg.c`, ~600 lines, no libgcc — two 32-bit divides in the whole file, no 64-bit ones) is baseline-only, fixed-point, and was brought up against 19 host fixtures with `-Wall -Wextra` clean and a worst pixel delta of 11/255; five real bugs came out of that harness, and every one of them would have shown up as "the texture is wrong" rather than a crash: the IDCT's cosine table was garbage in 5 of its 7 rows (a plausible-looking table with one nonsense expression in it — "22725 - 1021" — is worse than none), the AC-quant dequantiser scaled by 32768 where it needed 65536, `bits_fill` sets its `eoi` flag while PREFETCHING (so up to 8 bits before the end of the scan), and the decoder checked that flag in the middle of its symbol loop — which chopped the last MCU of every component; the fix decodes every MCU and lets a soft -1 out of `decode_block` mid-gray the tail block, and the IDCT needed round-to-nearest at both stages (`(t + 16384) >> 15`, `(acc + 32768) >> 16`, `((row[j] + 2) >> 2) + 128`) to stop a half-LSB bias from eating the last row. Chroma needed libjpeg's *fancy* upsampling (separable half-pixel bilinear, Q8 weights) or every 4:2:0 image shows visible block edges at 2x. **The shader script** is parsed by `q3bsp_read_shader_decls` (q3bsp.c) over `FS_GetFileList("scripts", ".shader", ...)`, keeping the FIRST `map` operand of each definition (nested stages counted, `//` and `/* */` stripped so a commented-out `map` cannot win, quoted or bare operands, definition names lowercase-matched prefix-wise with the shortest name winning, duplicates resolving to the last — id's own rules), and `q3world_render.c` now tries the definition's image first and the shader's own name second, in id's extension order (.jpg/.jpeg/.tga/.png), so a script that rebinds a name to a JPEG is observable in the log: `[Q3ARENA] tex 4 textures/mectovtest/curve path=textures/mectovtest/curve_jpg.jpg fmt=jpg size=64x64 bytes=1104 gl=5`. Under all of it, the kernel's VFS had to grow teeth: `FS_GetFileList` reaches `Sys_ListFiles`, which is now a real walk of the VFS node table (the entries id sees are RELATIVE to the directory asked for, because `FS_ListFilteredFiles` adds directory-branch names verbatim while stripping the path prefix from pak-branch ones), with `MAX_NODES` 256→1024, a 512 KB node table, and `VFS_LAYOUT_VERSION` 4 so the wider table can never be misread by an older kernel. **Four bugs died on the way, and two of them were not Q3 bugs at all.** (1) A `.shader` file is read through `FS_ReadFile` (the hunk allocator) but was freed with `kfree` — a heap-corruption panic at the exact moment the renderer began resolving textures, located by resolving the panic EIP against `nm -n iso/boot/myos.bin`. (2) **The kernel's ext2 tree was never rebuilt from the image on boot.** `vfs_init` populates `/ext2` from the mounted disk, but unlike the FAT32 branch beside it it did not `vfs_clear_children()` first, and the node table is PERSISTED (vfs_save) — so `vfs_create_node` hit the previous boot's stale `/baseq3` entry, returned -2, and `ext2_populate_vfs_depth`'s `if (new_dir >= 0)` **skipped the whole recursion**: the guest browsed last boot's directory tree while reading this image's bytes. It went unnoticed for releases because every consumer opened fixed paths (`/ext2/baseq3/vm/qagame.qvm`) that existed in both trees; it became visible only when something LISTED a directory that existed in only one of them, which is exactly what a shader script needs. (3) The script parser's tokenizer treated `{` and `}` as token *separators*, so a brace produced a zero-length token that did not advance the pointer and the parse bailed out at the first one — every script yielded zero definitions, silently, and the renderer fell back to the name-as-path convention and drew the .tga the script was meant to replace. Both (2) and (3) are the same failure shape: a lookup that quietly returns *something else* rather than an error. (4) Three comments containing `scripts/*.shader` tripped `-Wcomment` under `-Wall`; the "0 warnings" bar is what caught them. The suite is new: `scripts/q3retail_test.py` builds the synthetic pak, stages it through the real `q3a_data.py --verify`, asserts the staged tree carries the script and the JPEG and **not** the `.tga` the script rebinds away, seeds a fresh ext2 (this suite now recreates its disk images like its siblings, because the persisted node table made a stale volume indistinguishable from a correct one), boots, types `q3arena mectovtest`, and requires the whole chain in the serial log: the map argument reached the driver, the mesh came from `maps/mectovtest.bsp`, the engine FS listed the script and the parser produced definitions, `curve` resolved to `curve_jpg.jpg` while the four directly-named textures resolved to their own TGAs, 68 faces were submitted in a frame, the JPEG's magenta reached the screen (1,218 px, 41 distinct colours), and ESC ended the session with the OS alive. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.111. |
| v38.110 | **The frame budget is measured, not guessed: `q3arena` now reports where every frame's wall time goes — and shows it on screen (Q3 phase 8, part 3).** The desktop overlay said 4 FPS but nothing said what the *game* was paying for, so the frame is now metered phase by phase on the kernel's millisecond tick: the module's two VM_Call (GAME_RUN_FRAME + GAME_CLIENT_THINK), the software TinyGL pass (begin_frame..end_frame), the compositor's charges for the game window (its app draw callback = `q3ref_blit` + HUD, and its content-buffer blit into the desktop's back buffer, metered per-window in `draw_one` via a new `is_q3_game` tag the driver sets), the whole `wm_draw_all` pass, the driver's other work, and the pacing remainder (idle = budget nothing spent — headroom a faster renderer could turn into frames). Each phase accumulates into a 20-frame (=1s game time) window reported as ONE atomic `reportf` line (`[Q3ARENA] perf frame=.. fps=.. vm_ms=.. gl_ms=.. blit_ms=.. draw_ms=.. wm_ms=.. other_ms=.. idle_ms=.. sum_ms=..`); the suite parses it, echoes a human-readable breakdown, and asserts the accounting is arithmetically honest (non-negative parts, parts within 1.3x of their own wall window) without trusting any host-speed-dependent value. Two instrument designs died so this one could live, both under QEMU TCG: (1) an rdtsc clock converted to microseconds by a 64-bit multiply overflowed into negative `gl_ms` and seconds-large `vm_ms` for one-second windows — TCG advances the virtual TSC in irregular leaps between timer interrupts, so it is worthless as a phase meter here (the freestanding kernel cannot even link `__udivdi3`, which the conversion needed); (2) the WM's first rdtsc calibration busy-waited on `get_ticks` from inside `draw_one` — under the WM lock with IF=0 the timer IRQ never fires, the wait never ends, and the whole desktop froze on its first composite pass (mouse dead, serial silent). The kernel tick counter is IRQ-driven, monotonic, needs no calibration, never waits, and is the same clock that paces the game loop — 1 ms resolution is ample against a 50 ms frame. And the numbers are visible in the window itself: `q3ref_draw_perf_overlay` paints an fps counter plus a per-phase cost bar (vm/gl/blit/wm/other, one cell per 2 ms) straight into the finished ZBuffer AFTER the frame histogram is taken — the user sees the costs live while the suite's pixel evidence stays exactly what the 3D pass produced (the HUD's colours can only add to histogram buckets, never subtract from the ones asserted on). The verdict on the dev VM: of a 5.0-5.6 s wall window per 20 frames, the software TinyGL raster takes ~4.5-4.9 s (~87%), id's module 60-180 ms (~2%), the driver's own work ~0.6 s, the game window's composite share under 1 ms per pass, idle 0 — the renderer is the target, everything else is noise; that is what gates retail maps (PVS/frustum culling) and what does not. Verified: both kernel variants build clean, q3arena PASS with the breakdown in the log. |
| v38.109 | **The curved surfaces draw: the renderer tessellates `MST_PATCH` Bezier meshes into real geometry, so the arches and fillets of a real q3dm map stop being holes (Q3 phase 8, part 2).** v38.108 drew the level one-to-one from the surface lump and *counted* patches instead of drawing them — honest accounting, but in a retail map that accounting is visible as missing geometry, because every arch, dome and rounded trim q3map emits is an `MST_PATCH`. The difference from a planar face is structural: a patch stores a control GRID (odd-sized, interpolating and approximating points alternating), and the visible surface exists only after evaluating id's quadratic Bezier over it — the same grid `cm_patch.c` subdivides for collision, so what is drawn and what is collided now describe one shape by construction. `third_party/q3mectov/q3bsp.c` grew the tessellator: the level's surfaces are walked twice (count, then fill) because one lump entry expands into many faces; each 3x3 control block is evaluated per quad with position and `st` BOTH through the Bernstein weights — exact, not approximate, because q3map bakes patch `st` as a linear function of position, so combining the control points' `st` with the same weights reproduces it at every subdivision level (no drift, no seams between blocks); each block's subdivision N comes from how far its control points bow away from the plane through its four corners (a quadratic sits half as far as its control point, N chords cut the error N^2, flat blocks get 1 quad, tight ones up to 8x8); and each quad is emitted wound `A D C B`, reproducing `cm_patch.c`'s `cross(C-A, B-A)` so the face's baked normal lands on the collision facet's side of the surface — verified twice, first by a Python replica over the generated file (normals rotate (−0.07,0,1.0) → (−1,0,0.07) along the curve, baked light 0.87 → 0.40, the gradient that makes a curve read as a curve under the fixed-sun shading), then in the rendered pixels. The test arena grew the surface class it never had: `scripts/build_test_bsp.py` now writes a quarter-round cove along the +X wall as a 5x3 control grid (de Casteljau-split so two blocks share a middle column — exactly the continuity case a tessellator has to get right), non-solid like a retail map's decorative patches so `CM_TraceThroughLeaf` skips it (`if (!(patch->contents & tw->contents)) continue;`) and the collision world v38.107's walking assertions live in stays byte-for-byte; its magenta texture is chosen so no planar face can produce its colour at any light level (red and blue both >2x green), which makes "the curve reached the screen" a pixel test; and the +Y wall now runs past the +X wall's outer face, because two slabs merely meeting at (512,512) leave the corner quadrant open — a camera standing in it saw clear colour through the seam, frame 120's `sky=24480`. Two real bugs surfaced while verifying, both found by measurement rather than eyeball. (1) **id's patch collision was a stack bomb this kernel could not survive**: `CM_GeneratePatchCollide` works in a `MAC_STATIC cGrid_t` (129*129*3 floats) plus `int[129][129][2]`, and id's own `MAC_STATIC` is EMPTY on every platform — the Linux comment says "we just waste space and make big arrays static" and then does not — so the frame measured 332,976 bytes against a 64KB stack; the first .bsp with a patch mesh double-faulted inside `CM_LoadMap`. The fix keeps `third_party/q3a/` verbatim: `third_party/q3mectov/cm_patch_stack.c` redefines `MAC_STATIC` to `static` (after reading `q_shared.h` — the only defining header, and guard-protected; `cm_local.h`/`cm_polylib.h` have NO guards, which is why including them directly redeclared everything and failed) before including `cm_patch.c`; the grids became 332KB of `.bss`, and `CM_GeneratePatchCollide`'s stack frame dropped to 3.5KB. The same `-fstack-usage` audit found `FS_Seek`'s dead `char foo[65536]` measuring 65,584 bytes — 48 over the variant's 64KB and reachable from a module through the FS_SEEK trap — so `TASK_KSTACK_SIZE` under `MECTOV_Q3` is 96KB, with every remaining engine frame measured under 17KB. (2) **the serial log was not atomic per line**: `write_serial_string` takes the lock per call, but every parsed line was assembled from 5-20 calls, and a run died with `verts=.[LOAD] c0=0x00000044 296...` — another task's output landed inside the mesh line and the suite's regexes found nothing. Every parsed line (mesh, window rect, per-texture, frame, pixels, movement, totals, probes, spawn, locate, vm-created, GAME_INIT) is now composed into one buffer and written once — `reportf()` through the kernel's own `vsnprintf` (the `doom/doom_libc.c` symbol, which supports `%08x`; the stub stdio's rename makes declaring it impossible, hence `q3_vsnprintf`), with hand-composed variants for the hex-bearing lines. `q3arena_test.py` now asserts the patch accounting exactly (patchdrawn == patches, patchverts == 4xpatchquads, faces == planar + patchquads) and the cove's magenta on screen (peaked 1,244 px), and the mesh line reads `74/43 surfaces, 296 vertices, 5 shaders (42 planar, 1 curved, 0 skipped)` — 32 tessellated quads where v38.108 drew nothing. Verified locally on the final 38.109 ISOs: q3arena 132s, q3vm 36s, q3 55s, q3gl 45s, q3play 105s all PASS, boot + app_smoke PASS on the rebuilt default ISO, 0 warnings in both variants, `third_party/q3a/` untouched (the one file that needed different compile flags wraps it rather than edits it). `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.109. |
| v38.108 | **The level the official game module has been running inside since v38.107 is finally on screen: the kernel builds the `.bsp`'s render mesh, decodes the map's textures off the game volume, and draws them through TinyGL, with the camera on the module's own `playerState` and the window's input going back in as the `usercmd` id's `Pmove` consumes (Q3 phase 8).** v38.107 gave the module a world made of id's own brushes, but its only evidence was serial text — `q3play` draws a Mectov-made arena, and `q3vm` draws nothing at all. The new `q3arena` shell command (`cmd_q3arena.c`, `q3arena_start()`) runs *the same session* as `q3vm` — id's QVM loader, the official `qagame.qvm`, the retail connect sequence — and additionally opens a 320x240 WM window and renders every frame of it, because the honest answer to "what does the module's level look like" is the module's own geometry and nothing else. Two readers, one file, which is how id itself splits it: `CM_*` keeps planes, brushes and leafs for tracing, and the new `third_party/q3mectov/q3bsp.c` keeps what a camera needs — `MST_PLANAR` surfaces expanded into triangle fans with their `st` coordinates straight out of the lump, a per-face unit normal derived from the winding, a baked light factor, and the shader names (it uses `qfiles.h` from the vendored tree so the struct layout cannot drift, bounds-checks every lump before touching it, and reports what it skipped rather than drawing a silent hole: `MST_PATCH` surfaces are counted and not drawn, because the Bezier tessellator is not in this port yet). No lightmaps are involved, so each face is shaded from its own normal against a fixed sun and applied as the vertex colour — TinyGL's `TGL_FEATURE_LIT_TEXTURES` modulates the texel by it — which is also what makes the palette assertable (a face with no light would be indistinguishable from a hole). Textures come off the volume through the same engine FS the map came from: `third_party/tinygl/q3world_render.c` has a small TGA decoder (uncompressed and RLE, 24/32-bit, either origin — the formats id's tools emit) and uploads each shader's image once; a shader whose file is missing gets a generated magenta-diagonal placeholder and a `missing=1` line in the log, because "the map has a texture I do not have" and "the renderer is broken" must never look the same. The camera is a full basis, not a yaw/pitch pair, because the module hands over an arbitrary forward vector: `ps.origin + ps.viewheight` and id's own `AngleVectors()` on `ps.viewangles`, fed to `q3ref_set_camera_basis()`. Input travels the other way — the WM's raw scancodes and captured mouse motion become the `usercmd` the module consumes (`cmd.angles` from the driver's own accumulation, `forwardmove`/`rightmove` from WASD/arrows) — so the walking is id's `bg_pmove.c`, pushed through the window instead of a constant. The camera keeps its identity by construction, not by assertion: because the module adds its own `delta_angles` (seeded by `SetClientViewAngle` at spawn) to whatever a command carries, feeding `ps.viewangles` back into `cmd.angles` counts that delta again every frame and the view walks itself away — the first version of this driver drifted +45°/frame and the player oscillated on the spot. Three more findings, all three of which produced a wrong picture rather than an error: (1) TinyGL's `glLoadMatrixf` takes the standard **column-major** array (`glopLoadMatrix` transposes as it stores), so writing the view matrix row-wise turns it into a transposed rotation — the camera then points somewhere else entirely and the frame comes out 100% clear colour, which is exactly what the new in-renderer histogram reported; (2) a screendump cannot be the pass/fail gate here — the WM owns window placement and the Terminal window sits on top of the game window, so the pixel test's first version measured the wallpaper and called it a floor; the finished frame is now classified **out of the renderer's own ZBuffer** (`q3ref_frame_histogram`/`q3w_histogram`) and the screendump is kept as evidence for a human, with a geometry check that the window really appeared where the driver asked; (3) the arena's ceiling, lit at ambient only, landed at `0x0E0E15` — indistinguishable from the desktop's own dark violet in a screendump — so the generated ceiling texture is bright and the ambient term is 0.40. `scripts/build_test_bsp.py` now also generates the four 64x64 TGA textures the arena's shaders name (nearest-neighbour, 5-bit-per-channel, colours saturated and mutually distinct so a screendump can be asserted on and line up with the geometry), which `seed_ext2.sh` mirrors onto the volume as `/baseq3/textures/mectovtest/…` — still no id art anywhere in this repository. New `scripts/q3arena_test.py` (check.py row `q3arena`, `make check-q3arena`, a step in the CI `quake3` job) asserts: the mesh is the level's own file (42/42 surfaces, 168 vertices, 4 shaders, nothing truncated), all four textures decoded from the volume with no placeholder, surfaces are submitted (peak 36 faces in one frame), every sampled frame is mostly level (clear colour 0 of 76800 pixels) and contains floor, walls, ceiling and the step block in the palette, the window is on the desktop where the driver asked (322x262), the scene is live (two frames ~100 ticks apart differ), official gameplay code moves the player inside the drawn level (`x -384 -> 496`, 880 units), and ESC ends the session cleanly with the OS alive. Verified locally: q3arena 74s, q3vm 32s, q3play 88s, q3gl 53s, q3 48s all PASS, boot + app_smoke PASS on the rebuilt default ISO, 0 warnings in both ISO variants, `third_party/q3a/` untouched. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.108. |
| v38.107 | **The official game module finally runs in a level: the kernel compiles id's OWN collision model and loads a real `.bsp` through the engine's filesystem, so `trap_Trace`, `trap_PointContents` and the level's entity string are id's — real brushes, real planes, real content flags (Q3 phase 7).** Until now `G_TRACE` answered from `q3vm_trace_world()`: one infinite floor plane at z = 0. That made "id's own gameplay code runs" true and "in a level" false, and it is the reason the player could only ever fall or walk on a single surface. `Makefile` now builds `qcommon/cm_load.c`, `cm_trace.c`, `cm_test.c`, `cm_patch.c` and `cm_polylib.c` into the kernel, and `q3_vm.c` calls `CM_LoadMap()` before `GAME_INIT` (during which `G_InitGame` walks the level's entity text), then answers `G_GET_ENTITY_TOKEN` from `CM_EntityString()`, `G_TRACE`/`G_TRACECAPSULE` from `CM_BoxTrace()` with the module's real content mask and capsule flag, and `G_POINT_CONTENTS` from `CM_PointContents()`. Two things had to be discovered rather than assumed. (1) id's Linux/i386 build was expected to supply `BoxOnPlaneSide` from an assembly object this port does not build — its only C definition sits behind `#if !((defined __linux__ \|\| __FreeBSD__) && __i386__ && !defined C_ONLY)`, so `cm_test.c` was an instant `undefined reference` the moment it joined the link; the port now compiles the tree with `-DC_ONLY`, which changes nothing else (it appears three times in the whole source: that guard, the branch inside it, and a PowerPC path we never take). (2) `trace_t.entityNum` is the CALLER's field — `CM_Trace` never touches it — and the retail engine's `SV_GameTrace` sets it immediately after the same call. Left unset, a world hit reported entity 0, the worldspawn gentity, so `bg_pmove.c` gave the player `ps.groundEntityNum == 0` on the floor while `PM_AddTouchEnt` treats `ENTITYNUM_WORLD` as "not a touchable entity"; the log showed a player standing at the floor's own height with `ground=0`. Also freed by the move: `c_traces`/`c_brush_traces`/`c_patch_traces`/`c_pointcontents` used to be dead stubs in `q3_platform.c` (now id's cm code owns them and the counters finally mean something), and `cm_patch.c`'s debug surface needed a `BotDrawDebugPolygons` stub because botlib is phase 8. Since `pak0.pk3` may never enter this repository, the arena under test is **generated**: `scripts/build_test_bsp.py` writes a valid v46 BSP out of nothing (shader/plane/node/leaf/model lumps all non-empty because `CM_LoadMap` rejects a map without them; every brush emits its six axial sides first, in `CM_BoundBrush`'s -X +X -Y +Y -Z +Z order, because that function derives the brush's bounds from them; planes face out of the solid; node children are `-(leaf + 1)`; planar faces are emitted with absolute draw indexes so the renderer phase has geometry to draw). It is built to make "did the real loader run?" answerable instead of assumed: its floor's **top face is z = 64** — the fake world put the player at exactly z = 24 — and it is **walled**, where the fake world returned fraction 1.0 (open air) for every horizontal move. `q3vm_test.py` now asserts the loader's own build report, the level's entity text and the spawn point read out of it, and two probes whose answers the old world could not have produced: `trace down fraction=0.253 endz=64.125 normal=(0 0 1) contents=1` and `trace -x fraction=0.250 endx=-511.875 normal=(1 0 0) contents=1`, followed by the walk — spawn at the map's own `(-384,-384,120)`, landing on the map's floor at z=88 (64 + the 24-unit player box) with `ground=1022` (`ENTITYNUM_WORLD`), 880 units of travel in 10 s of game time, and the player still inside the arena because the walls stopped it. Alongside the code, this release stages the data such a map needs: `scripts/q3a_data.py` walks **your own** `pak0.pk3` for one map's closure (the bsp's shader lump → the `.shader` sources that define those names → the images they name, with Q3's `.tga`/`.jpg` extension fallback, `animMap` chains and `skyParms` skyboxes; `--list` to plan, `--verify` to report any shader name that resolved to nothing) and writes it as a loose-file tree that `scripts/seed_ext2.sh` mirrors onto the volume as `/baseq3/...`; `run.sh` and `check.py` size that volume from the payload (16 MB unchanged for the generated arena, 512 MB with 4 KB blocks once real assets are staged — the kernel's ext2 driver walks 32 block groups, and 4 KB blocks put 512 MB at four of them); `.gitignore` refuses `*.pk3` and `assets/q3/` outright, because id's data is not ours to commit. The extractor is verified against a synthetic pak built from the generated arena (comments stripped, extension fallback, `animMap`, `skyParms`, a shader with no `map`, and a commented-out `map` line that must NOT be staged) — not against retail data, which cannot exist in this repository, so it says so rather than pretending. Three findings from v38.103-v38.106 that made a current ISO look older than it was are fixed too: the release string moved to `src/include/version.h` so Ring 3 apps can print it, and `apps/terminal.c` — which had hardcoded "Mectov OS v36.3" — now prints `OS_VERSION` like the kernel's own banner; `q3play` says on screen and in its window title that it is id's engine drawing the Mectov arena with no retail content; and the v38.105 headline ("Real Quake III Arena is what runs now") is reworded with a note explaining what it got right and what it read as. One thing found while verifying, reported rather than swallowed: re-linking any Ring 3 app emits `ld: warning: apps/<app>.elf has a LOAD segment with RWX permissions`. It is not from this release — `hello.elf`, which nothing here touches, produces it too — and it is invisible on an incremental build because `build_mct.py` leaves no dependency files, so the app ELFs are only re-linked when an app source changes or the object tree is wiped. Verified locally: q3 49s, q3gl 43s, q3play 96s, q3vm 32s all PASS, boot + app_smoke PASS on the rebuilt default ISO, and the kernel plus every engine object warning-free in both ISO variants. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.107. |
| v38.106 | **The official game module's world comes alive (Q3 phase 6): qagame.qvm no longer just initialises — id's own gameplay code runs the retail server's frame and a player moves through it, all inside the VM.** The driver in `q3_vm.c` now replays sv_client.c's connect sequence before the loop: `GAME_CLIENT_CONNECT` (userinfo `\ip\localhost\name\Mectov\team\0` served by a real `G_GET_USERINFO` — localhost skips the password path and marks the local client), `GAME_CLIENT_USERINFO_CHANGED` (propagates the name into pers.netname), then `GAME_CLIENT_BEGIN`, whose `ClientSpawn` picks the deathmatch spot out of the entity string `G_GET_ENTITY_TOKEN` now serves (worldspawn plus `{ "classname" "info_player_deathmatch" "origin" "0 0 24" }`) and whose `ClientBegin` prints the classic `Mectov entered the game` — because `G_SEND_SERVER_COMMAND` (and `G_DROP_CLIENT`) now log their strings instead of vanishing. The collision traps are real for the first time: `G_TRACE`/`G_TRACECAPSULE` implement the minimal world id's physics can stand on — a floor plane at z=0; a sweep whose box (mins[2] = -24) crosses the plane clips with a proper `trace_t` (fraction, endpos, normal (0,0,1), `ENTITYNUM_WORLD`), everything else returns empty air. `G_GET_USERCMD` fills a genuine usercmd — serverTime stamped with the frame's level time and forwardmove 127 — so `ClientThink_real`'s own command-time gating accepts it and `Pmove` accelerates. The lcc backend turned out to compile the module's float math into the shared traps (qcommon.h `sharedTraps_t`), so the kernel grew sin/cos/atan2/sqrt as trap 103-106 with floats travelling as IEEE bit patterns: range reduction plus short series (~1e-6, plenty for movement math; the kernel has no libm), `atan2` via two half-angle steps, and `BOTLIB_AAS_INITIALIZED` answering 0 so `BotTestAAS` bails silently each think. The loop itself is the retail pair — 200 × (`GAME_RUN_FRAME` with level time advancing FRAMETIME, then `GAME_CLIENT_THINK`) — and `G_LOCATE_GAME_DATA` additionally records the playerState offset so the driver can read gclient[0]'s `playerState_t` straight out of the VM data segment every 20 frames (`[Q3VM] frame N t=T origin=(…) ground=… velocity=(…)`). `q3vm_test.py` now proves motion, not just liveness: the player falls (velocity -79, ground NONE), lands on the floor (z=24, ground `ENTITYNUM_WORLD`), and walks 2,383 units in 9 seconds of game time — the official 320 u/s walk speed, which is id's own bg_pmove.c executing inside the bytecode; every v38.105 assertion stays. Bring-up hit three authentic retail behaviors worth recording: `G_RUN_FRAME` is what assigns level.time (the server owns the clock), `ps.commandTime` is seeded at `ClientSpawn` from level.time (a driver clock behind the spawn time makes every think a no-op with `msec < 1`), and the usercmd pointer is the trap's **second** argument (`args[2]`) — filling args[1] wrote to VM address 0 and the player could only ever fall. Verified locally: q3vm 33s, q3 55s, q3gl 48s, q3play 115s all PASS, boot + app_smoke PASS on the rebuilt default ISO, 0 warnings in both variants. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.106. |
| v38.105 | **The engine core is id Software's official GPL release, and id's own game bytecode (`qagame.qvm`) executes inside it through id's own QVM loader and bytecode interpreter — no ioquake3 subset, no hand-rolled game loop (Q3 phase 5).** The phase 1-4 ioquake3 subset is deleted outright (`third_party/q3/`, unreferenced once `Q3_DIR` moved — git history keeps it): `third_party/q3a/` is id-Software/Quake-III-Arena taken verbatim (`code/`, `lcc/`, `q3asm/`, `ui/`, `COPYING.txt`, `README.txt`, `UPSTREAM.md`; `scripts/vendor_q3a.sh` reproduces the checkout), the Makefile's `Q3_DIR` now points at it, and q3/q3gl/q3play compile against id's own engine too. That forced the port layer to supply what id expects from its host: `Com_Memset`/`Com_Memcpy` (id only defines them for non-Linux hosts — on Linux the linker was expected to find them in libc), `Sys_LoadDll` with id's real signature (native modules are impossible here, so it always fails and the loader falls back to bytecode — exactly the path we want), `Sys_Mkdir` as void, `Sys_SnapVector`, and a real `Sys_GetEvent` draining the queue `Com_QueueEvent` fills from the WM. One contract in that list is load-bearing and cost a bring-up round: an idle event must carry the current clock. `Com_Frame` paces itself with `do { com_frameTime = Com_EventLoop(); msec = ...; } while ( msec < minMsec )`, and `Com_EventLoop` returns an idle event's `evTime` — a zeroed struct pins that loop at `msec == 0` forever, so the engine task pegged a core and phase 1's ten ticks never printed while the desktop kept painting (id's own platform layers stamp `evTime` on every path; same contract, not a workaround). Bytecode side: `scripts/build_qvm.sh` builds `qagame.qvm` (469,796 bytes) and `qagame.map` (33,719 bytes) from the vendored tree with **id's own lcc + q3asm** — after patching the 15 symbols the official source needs that the fork never did — and the new `q3vm` shell command (`cmd_q3vm.c`) forks a kernel task that stages only `default.cfg` into the tmpfs homepath, runs the engine's `Com_Init`, then `VM_Create("qagame", Q3VM_SystemCalls, VMI_BYTECODE)`. The QVM is deliberately NOT staged: at 470KB it is far past tmpfs's 256KB per-file cap, so it rides the ext2 volume as `/ext2/baseq3/vm/qagame.qvm` and **id's loader reads it through the engine's own filesystem**, the way it would read a retail mod. `Q3VM_SystemCalls` in `third_party/q3mectov/q3_vm.c` is the kernel-side twin of retail `SV_GameSystemCalls`: the three LCC helper traps (100/101/102 = memset/memcpy/strncpy — without them every string routine inside the module is a silent no-op, which is what produced an empty `G_ERROR` message during bring-up), print/error, cvar register/update/set, argc/argv/console, the whole FS surface, `LOCATE_GAME_DATA`, configstring get/set, `GET_ENTITY_TOKEN` (a real worldspawn entity string parsed with `COM_Parse`), and inert stubs for everything a dedicated-style kernel has no server for — any trap that falls through logs `unhandled trap #N` instead of failing silently. Two authenticity traps had to be solved, both from id's retail code and neither present in the fork: (1) symbols only load when `com_developer` is set, and that pointer is bound to a cvar actually named `developer` (`Cvar_Get("developer", ...)` in common.c) — `+set com_developer 1` silently did nothing and `symbols=0` was the symptom; (2) `FS_SetRestrictions()` refuses to accept a directory install without `productid.txt` and restarts the filesystem in **restricted demo mode** (`demota`), where `FS_FOpenFileRead` rejects every non-.cfg/.menu/.game/.dm_*/.dat file — that is what killed the phase-1 and client drivers with `Couldn't load default.cfg` the moment they switched to official source. `scripts/build_q3a_productid.py` reproduces id's own scrambled-constant check to emit the file, `seed_ext2.sh` writes it to `/baseq3/productid.txt`, and `Sys_DefaultCDPath()` now answers `/ext2` so every driver's search path reaches it (the ext2 volume plays the CD's role, which is also what makes the bytecode visible to the loader at all). Kernel side: the official core's 20MB hunk + 6MB zone cannot fit the 56MB heap (its largest free block measured 9MB on the already-loaded desktop arena), so guests with RAM to spare get `KERNEL_RESERVED_PAGES_BIG` (160MB, 136MB heap) while smaller guests keep the old nominal bit-for-bit; and `TASK_KSTACK_SIZE` is 64KB under `MECTOV_Q3` because id's `Com_sprintf` formats through a 32,000-byte stack buffer that a 32KB kernel stack cannot hold. New `scripts/q3vm_test.py` proves the whole chain end to end (check.py row `q3vm`, a step in the CI `quake3` job whose timeout went 25 → 35 min, and the CI artifact step now re-seeds ext2 *after* the QVM is built, since the seed ran before the bytecode existed): the staged bytecode must be byte-for-byte the checkout's build, id's loader must report `Loading vm file vm/qagame.qvm.` and `<n> bytes on the hunk`, the log must show `symbols=1050` with real symbol names from `vm/qagame.map`, `vminfo` must list `qagame : interpreted`, `vmMain(GAME_INIT)` must return with `traps=96 errors=0 unhandled=0`, `locate_game_data` must report 72 gentities of 808 bytes, `G_InitGame` must finish (worldspawn spawned, items registered), the module must take `GAME_SHUTDOWN`, and the OS must still be alive. `q3_test.py` lost its ioquake3 assumption: id's own version string in the banner is `Q3 1.32b`, and `q3gl_test.py`'s animation check now takes the best of three sample pairs (under host load a single pair measured 93 against a threshold of 80 — too close for a CI runner). The one red job in this release's CI run was not a kernel failure either: `config Nehalem / 48MB / SMP 4` failed at the `Fork / waitpid / signal smoke` step with the Terminal loading and then never becoming ready, while the same ISO passes that exact config locally and the job passed on the CI re-run (the run is green). It did expose a harness weakness worth fixing rather than shrugging at: the Terminal creates its IPC queue microseconds into `_start()` (`apps/terminal.c`: `sys_create_window` -> `setsid`/`tcsetpgrp` -> `add_default_aliases` -> `sys_ipc_create`), so a 30s miss is not a slow boot — either the instance died on the way in (a failed `sys_create_window` calls `sys_exit()` silently) or the guest was starved — yet 38 suites each re-implemented that wait with their own 30s timeout and a misleading "icon double-click missed?" message. `scripts/terminal_launch.py` now owns the whole choreography: `launch_terminal()` returns only once the Terminal is READY, retrying from a screendump-verified double-click (with a press that lengthens as attempts fail, because a starved desktop polls the button state slower) to a **fresh instance plus a longer wait** (30s then 60s of escalating patience), counting new `[LOADER] start`/IPC lines so a stale marker from an earlier instance can never pass as ready, and on failure printing the evidence that separates the three cases — click never landed, app died before the queue, guest starved — with the waiting/clicks counters and the serial tail; the whole failure path stays inside the smallest suite timeout (checked against fork_test's 240s, boot included). Verified: q3 55s, q3gl 48s, q3play 115s, q3vm 33s all PASS locally; fork at Nehalem/48MB/SMP4 (the CI config), tmpfs, lock, procfs, lseekfile and rlimit PASS with the new helper; boot + app_smoke PASS on the rebuilt default ISO; build 0 warnings in both ISO variants. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.105. *(Headline reworded in v38.107: the original said "Real Quake III Arena is what runs now". That was true of the engine and of the bytecode — and false as an answer to "can I play Quake III Arena", because the port ships no retail game data, nothing rendered the VM's world, and `q3play` drew a Mectov-made arena. The wording, not the engineering, is what made the release read as a playable game.)* |
| v38.104 | **The Quake III world becomes data: the client loads its map (geometry, player start and bots) from its own MCTBSP1 file through the engine's filesystem — no pak0, no hardcoded arena (Q3 phase 4).** The map format is a small text file, `MCTBSP1` (brush-based like Quake's map sources, but AABB brushes with per-face colours instead of planes/textures — this renderer has no lightmap or texture machinery and the port ships no game data): `assets/maps/mectov1.map` declares `map`/`bounds`/`spawn x y z yaw pitch`/`brush min…max r g b`/`bot x y z w d h r g b`; mectov1 is a 960x960 field inside perimeter walls, four 320-high pillars, a 320x240 **magenta platform** south of centre (the collision + colour proof), and three bots. Data path: `seed_ext2.sh` writes it to `/ext2/mectov1.map`; `q3play_start()` stages it ONCE into the tmpfs homepath as `baseq3/maps/mectov1.map` (or the byte-identical embedded fallback string when ext2 carries none — markers say which), so `q3map_load("maps/mectov1.map")` in `CL_StartHunkUsers` reads it through the **engine's own FS** (`FS_ReadFile` — the same entry `cm_load.c` uses for real .bsp files) and parses it with a deliberately minimal scanner (no sscanf in this build; a bad directive fails the WHOLE map, a half-loaded map is a bug not a degraded mode). The renderer gained `q3ref_set_map()` (`third_party/tinygl/q3cl_render.c`): with a map loaded every brush is a shaded box and the bots come from the same data — the phase-3 duplicate bot tables in renderer and client are gone; the hardcoded arena remains only as the failed-load fallback. The client spawns from the map's own start state (`[Q3CL] spawn` marker) and replaces the phase-3 flat clamp with real collision: axis-separated slide movement (X pass then Y pass, Q3-style) against brush AABBs with a 32-unit eye stand-off, bot pushout from map data, and a one-shot `[Q3CL] move blocked brush` marker on the 5th consecutive blocked frame. `scripts/q3play_test.py` grew the phase-4 assertions: the `map loaded` marker (brushes/bots/spawn parsed), first frame position == map spawn, the magenta axis in the screendump, then a held `w` down the open lane must BOTH reach the blocked marker AND stop at the brush (pos y 390 → ~24, brush face at −16) — and after a mouse turn, walking again must move the player (collision blocks, it does not wedge). Two test-robustness lessons this phase paid for with a bisect round: (1) under a **loaded host** TCG's effective engine fps drops to ~0.3, so an input hold shorter than one engine frame is pressed AND released between two `CL_Frame`s and never seen — holds must span several seconds and assertions must read serial markers, not sleeps; (2) single-screendump pixel assertions became timing-flaky when the host was busy (the identical binary passed at night and failed under desktop load — proven by running the SAME ISO from two directories, so it was never a kernel bug): `q3gl_test.py` and `q3play_test.py` now retry their pixel sample up to 5 times, 2 s apart. Verified: q3/q3gl/q3play all PASS locally (93s q3play), boot + app_smoke PASS on the rebuilt default ISO, build 0 warnings. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.104. |
| v38.103 | **The ioquake3 client loop becomes real: CL_Init/CL_Frame drives the TinyGL renderer, and the window manager feeds it keyboard + mouse input (Q3 phase 3).** Upstream `code/client/` is NOT vendored — a real ioquake3 client needs the ui/cgame QVMs plus pak0 assets this tree deliberately has none of (STANDALONE, no game data) — so the client layer lives in `third_party/q3mectov/q3_client.c` against the same qcommon engine: `CL_Init` sets the client cvars (`sensitivity`, `m_yaw`, `m_pitch`, `cl_yawspeed`, `cl_run`) and the default binds through a real 256-entry binding table, `CL_InitKeyCommands` registers `bind`/`unbind`/`unbindall`/`bindlist`, `CL_KeyEvent` executes the binding (`+forward`/`+speed`/`+attack` set movement flags, plain binds go through `Cbuf_AddText`), `CL_MouseEvent` applies Q3's `m_yaw`/`m_pitch` model at event time, and `CL_Frame` integrates the player and renders an arena through a new TinyGL backend (`third_party/tinygl/q3cl_render.c`: 1024x1024 checkerboard floor, 192-unit walls with base and rim bands, four pillars, three spinning/bobbing bots, a camera-space crosshair, per-face shading instead of GL lighting to keep the TCG frame budget). The input chain is the point of the phase: the WM routes raw PS/2 **press and release** scancodes to the focused game window (`wm_request_scancodes`/`wm_handle_scancode`) and — new — **captures the mouse** (`wm_capture_mouse`, plus `mouse_take_delta()` in the driver, which accumulates relative packet motion that the clamped absolute cursor throws away) so `mouse_fn` receives deltas with no button held and no dead-end at a screen edge; the cursor is pinned inside the window and hidden while the capture lasts. Those callbacks (compositor context) push into a lock-protected ring buffer that the client task drains in `clq_pump()` straight into **the engine's own event queue** (`Com_QueueEvent`), so the events take the same path a real ioquake3 build's SDL events do: `Com_Frame` -> `Com_EventLoop` -> `CL_KeyEvent`/`CL_CharEvent`/`CL_MouseEvent`. `NET_Sleep` became a real `hlt` wait, so the engine's own `com_maxfps` limiter paces the client at 20 fps. New `scripts/q3play_test.py` (a `quake3` CI job step, `make check-q3play`, `check.py --only q3play`) proves it end to end: serial markers for the client/binds/window/world, pixel assertions on a QEMU screendump (sky blue, warm wall+floor, both bot colors, a bright crosshair at the window centre), then REAL input — a held `w` must arrive as `[Q3CL] key down w bind=+forward` AND move the camera (`pos=` changes between frame markers, releases included), injected mouse motion must reach `CL_MouseEvent` and change `yaw=` with the rendered frame differing afterwards, and ESC must shut the loop down and restore the desktop. Two bugs this phase surfaced, both fixed: the capture's hand-over path also cleared the raw-scancode request (so keys worked but releases never arrived), and the phase-2 blit swapped R and B — TinyGL's 32-bit PIXEL is byte-for-byte the framebuffer's 0x00RRGGBB layout, which the kernel's own theme constant `GUI_DESKTOP 0x0011111B` reading back as (17,17,27) in a screendump settled. Verified: q3/q3gl/q3play all PASS locally (q3play 85s), build 0 warnings in both ISO variants. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.103. |
| v38.102 | **TinyGL software-GL inside the kernel — the first real 3D pipeline renders into a WM window (Q3 phase 2), proven by a pixel-level visual test.** New `q3gl` shell command forks a kernel task that runs the TinyGL OpenGL 1.1 rasterizer (vendored at `third_party/tinygl/` from jserv/tinygl, MIT — core only, `ztext` kept because the op-dispatch table references it, `memory.c` dropped because our shim replaces it) rendering the classic lit/animated gears scene (Brian Paul's mesa gears, trimmed to three gears + one light) at 320x240 with a ~20 fps `hlt`-based limiter. Present goes through the compositor exactly like windowed DOOM (v38.29): the ZBuffer is opened over a kmalloc'd pixel buffer, the window `draw_fn` swizzles TinyGL's 0x00RRGGBB pixels into the framebuffer's 0x00BBGGRR layout and scales into the content buffer, `wm_invalidate` + `needs_redraw` trigger the composite; ESC (via the window `key_fn`) shuts the task down and it parks in `hlt` (a forked kernel task must never return — v38.99's garbage-EIP lesson). Everything routes through the existing Q3 libc shim: `gl_malloc`/`gl_zalloc` → kmalloc (new `kernel_shim.c` replacing TinyGL's `memory.c`), `sin`/`cos`/`sqrt` → kernel FPU shims, plus one new shim `q3_isfinite()` (exponent-field test) for TinyGL's viewport clamp. Build: `TGL_CFLAGS` mirrors `Q3_CFLAGS` (`-Ithird_party/q3mectov/stubs` so libc names alias to the shims), only under `MECTOV_Q3=1` — the default ISO stays lean. `scripts/q3gl_test.py` (registered as `make check-q3tgl`, a step of the CI quake3 job, and `check.py --only q3gl`) boots the Q3 ISO, types `q3gl`, asserts `[Q3GL] window id=` / `scene ready` / `frame=` markers, then pixel-checks a real QEMU screendump: the window band must contain all three gear body colors (blue/red/green, asymmetric thresholds so a flat desktop can't false-positive) and specular highlights, and two captures 3s apart must differ — the scene is genuinely animated on the composited desktop. Verified: q3gl_test PASS end-to-end (blue=933 red=3768 green=731 lit pixels; animation diff=161), build 0 warnings in both ISO variants. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.102. |
| v38.101 | **RAM grid in CI, and a boot smoke that can no longer be green on a broken allocator.** v38.100's reservation fix had two holes, both found by pushing the RAM axis down instead of up. (1) The heap ceiling still carried an independent 4MB floor, so on a guest whose reservation shrank below 24MB+4MB the heap was placed *outside* the reserved region — the exact overlap the layout exists to prevent, and it was silently live at 24MB (heap ceiling printed as zero, every kmalloc returning NULL, and the desktop still painting). `phys_init` now raises the reservation to cover `KERNEL_HEAP_BASE_BYTES + KERNEL_HEAP_MIN_BYTES` before clamping, so the heap can never fall outside it, and prints `[PHYS] FATAL: guest RAM below the supported floor` when even that leaves no frames. (2) The boot smoke happily passed a starved guest — 24MB reached login with a zero heap and no shared zero page. `boot_test.py` gained `memory_sanity_errors()`, which asserts no `[PHYS] FATAL`, a real `[PHYS] shared zero page` (proof the frame allocator found a frame) and a non-zero `[MEM] heap ceiling`; it fails with each specific reason. Boot now also logs `[MEM] heap ceiling 0x… bytes above 0x…` and `memstat` prints a `Heap Max` line, so the derived boundary is auditable from the console. CI: the config matrix's RAM axis moves 64→48MB (the measured floor) and a new `ram-sweep` job walks the interior at both CPU models / 4 cores — 64MB (comfortably capped), 96MB (capped, heap ~48MB), 128MB (the cap/nominal boundary, first point that takes the full 80MB reservation and the heap's full 56MB) — for 18 config jobs total. Floor measured on qemu32,+nx/SMP 4 with boot **and** fork: 48/64/96/128/256 MB PASS; 32 and 40MB boot and log in but cannot launch an app (the heap is too small for the desktop assets — the wallpaper falls back and the launch path starves), so they are deliberately not CI points; 24MB and below lose the frame allocator entirely. `KERNEL_MIN_RAM_BYTES`, `KERNEL_HEAP_BASE_BYTES` and `KERNEL_HEAP_MIN_BYTES` now live in mem.h as the single description of the heap/​frame layout. Verified: boot 24/32/40/48/64/96/128/256MB and fork 48/64/96/128MB locally, tmpfs + app smoke 4/4 PASS, build 0 warnings. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.101. |
| v38.100 | **Adaptive kernel reservation — v38.99's 80MB reserve broke every 64MB guest, and the fix forced heap and frame boundaries onto one source of truth.** The config matrix (CPU model x RAM x cores) went all-red on `mem: 64` after `KERNEL_RESERVED_PAGES` was raised 48→80MB for the Q3 heap: on a 64MB guest the reservation exceeded physical RAM, so `phys_init` marked every frame used and `frame_alloc` had nothing left — boot died before login. The reservation is now runtime-adaptive: `phys_init` keeps a 25% free-frame floor and caps the reserve when the nominal 80MB would eat it (64MB guest → 48MB reserved, 16MB allocatable), printing `[PHYS] small-RAM guest: reservation capped.` so the console says which mode it took. All frame paths (`frame_alloc`, `frame_free`, the COW address threshold, `phys_used_count`, the shared zero page scan) already read the runtime value. The second half was subtler and would have been a latent corruption bug: the kernel heap is a fixed physical span starting at 24MB, and its ceiling was computed independently from the RAM size — on a 64MB guest that put the heap's top at 64MB while the frame allocator considered everything past 48MB free, so `request_space` could hand out memory the allocator would also hand to a process. The heap ceiling is now derived from the reservation itself via the new `phys_reserved_bytes()` accessor (`mem.c` reads the boundary `phys_init` just settled on), so the two regions are disjoint by construction rather than by coincidence; the literal heap base became `KERNEL_HEAP_BASE`, and the old fixed `max_heap = 56MB` in `request_space` is gone. Big-guest behavior is byte-identical: 128MB reserves 80MB and the heap keeps its 56MB (verified — no cap line, `0x05000000` reserved), 512MB (the Q3 config) likewise. Verified: boot_test at 64MB / qemu32,+nx / SMP 4 PASS, default 128MB PASS, tmpfs + q3 regression PASS, build 0 warnings. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.100. |
| v38.99 | **ioquake3 engine core boots on Mectov OS — first timer-dependent Ring-0 third-party app, and it uncovered a fundamental kernel issue.** New `q3` shell command runs a subset of the ioquake3 engine (id Tech 3) inside the kernel: vendor tree in `third_party/q3/` (STANDALONE mode), port layer `third_party/q3mectov/` (q3_kernel.c libc shim aliasing every libc name via `stubs/*.h` — string/mem to kernel utils, stdio to VFS-backed fopen/fread/fwrite/fseek with offset table, vsnprintf port, math shims on kernel sin/cos/atan2), `q3_platform.c` hosting Com_Init/Com_Frame like doomgeneric hosts DOOM, and `cmd_q3.c` entering it. Three real kernel findings fixed along the way: (1) **shell builtins run inside the SYS_EXEC_CMD syscall with IF=0** — polling apps (DOOM) survive that, but Q3's frame loop needs the timer interrupt, and `Sys_Milliseconds()` never advanced; hardware probes proved PIT counting + IRQ0 pending + unmasked while IF=0. Fix: `q3` now `task_fork_kernel()`s into its own kernel task, which starts with EFLAGS 0x202 — timer alive, shell syscall returns normally. (2) **16KB kernel stacks were too small** for deep engine call chains — `Com_EventLoop` overflowed the guard page and double-faulted (peak ~16.5KB observed); `TASK_KSTACK_SIZE` doubled to 32KB. (3) engine config writes stalled on the ATA disk; all engine I/O now lands on the v38.96 tmpfs (`/q3home`, `/tmp/q3home` — 13 offset-writes verified). Also: `KERNEL_RESERVED_PAGES` 48→80MB for the Q3 hunk/zone heap, guest RAM 128→512MB in run.sh, `vfs_get_file_size()` accessor, FS paths normalized to the linux PATH_SEP block. Two bugs found in bring-up and fixed: the wait-frame loop instrumentation was traced to a genuinely frozen timer (not interleaved logs), and `q3_exit_marker` returned into a garbage return address (EIP 0x1EFDF034) — the engine task now parks in a hlt loop after shutdown. New `scripts/q3_test.py` boots, logs in, launches `q3`, asserts `Com_Init` completes, 10 timed `Com_Frame` ticks (proving the timer advances inside a kernel engine task), config round-trip through tmpfs, clean park, and the OS staying alive; registered as `make check-q3` + a dedicated CI job with its own `MECTOV_Q3=1` ISO artifact (the Q3 tree is opt-in at build time, so the default ISO stays lean). Verified: q3_test full PASS; tmpfs + fork regression PASS (32KB stacks); boot/core unaffected; build 0 warnings in both ISO variants. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.99. |
| v38.97 | **Antialiased text** — DejaVu Sans Mono rasterized to 4-bit coverage (`scripts/gen_font.py` → `font_aa.h`), blended through `vga_blend_px()`; same 8x16 cell so no layout changes. Every GUI text (terminal, titles, taskbar, labels) gets smooth glyph edges; verified per-label on the real desktop by `scripts/font_render_test.py`.
| v38.96 | **tmpfs — a RAM-backed filesystem mounted at /tmp, with an end-to-end regression suite.** Classic kernel feature, genuinely useful: scratch files, editor swap files and future package temp-staging no longer burn the 1MB disk or survive a reboot. New node types `FS_RAM_DIR`/`FS_RAM_FILE` are appended to `fs_type_t` at the END on purpose — the node table persists on disk with numeric types, so inserting mid-enum would renumber `FS_SYMLINK` and misread every existing disk image. Content lives in kmalloc'd buffers tracked by a side table in vfs.c (never in the node's pad area, so no heap pointer can ever be persisted to disk by vfs_save); reads/writes are plain memcpy — no ATA command, no pcache. Volatility is enforced, not hoped for: `vfs_purge_stale_tmpfs()` drops FS_RAM_* records a loaded disk table may carry from a previous boot (their bytes are gone with the RAM), then `vfs_seed_tmpfs()` (re)mounts /tmp fresh — the mount point itself is permanent (delete returns -7 like /proc). Budgets: 256KB per file, 2MB global (`VFS_TMPFS_MAX_BYTES`), growth rounds to 512B and holes read as zeros (POSIX). Integration points: `vfs_create_node` remaps FS_DIR/FS_FILE requests under an FS_RAM_DIR parent to the RAM types (so mkdir/touch/redirection just work), create-mode defaults match regular dirs (root 0777 / user 0755, files 0644), `vfs_clear_children`/delete free the RAM buffer, `vfs_read_file_offset` gained an offset-aware RAM backend (fd lseek+sequential reads work), the fd write path gained a `vfs_write_file_offset` fast path (writes the buffer in place instead of the generic read-modify-write-everything round trip), `df` shows a tmpfs row, and /proc/meminfo reports `TmpfsUsed:` against the budget. One honest shim: `mark_dirty()` now rejects empty rects — during bring-up a zero-size damage rect collapsed the dirty-rectangle bounds and black-screened the compositor. The demo app `apps/tmpfsdemo.mct` (seeded like the other demos) exercises create/write/read/lseek/O_APPEND/mkdir/rename/unlink/64KB-verify through the syscall layer; new `scripts/tmpfs_test.py` (in `make check` + CI) boots, runs it, asserts all 12 markers plus the boot-time `tmpfs mounted at /tmp` line, then drives the shell's `echo > /tmp/x` + `cat` redirection path. One test-discovery worth noting: `SYS_OPEN` never creates — the app discipline (same as fat32demo) is `sys_create_file` first, then open-for-write; the first tmpfsdemo draft got this wrong and the test caught it immediately. Verified: tmpfs/procfs/procsys/boot PASS locally, app smoke 4/4 (procfsdemo, smpstress, sigdemo, crashme) PASS, build 0 warnings. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.96. |
| v38.95 | **Desktop icons rebuilt as vector artwork with real anti-aliasing — plus a renderer regression test that caught two bugs.** Feedback on v38.93/38.94 was still "this looks AI-made", and the cause was structural, not cosmetic: glyphs were 16x16 bitmaps blitted at 2x (every edge a 2x2 block, circles jaggy) on flat saturated fills. Artwork is now defined as *vector* shapes (rounded rects, circles, rings, ellipse rings, capsule strokes, triangles, with boolean subtract for negative space) in the new `scripts/gen_icons.py`, which rasterizes them with analytic signed-distance coverage — no supersampling, no scan conversion — into two 4-bit coverage masks per icon (32x32: a bright layer plus a 55% secondary layer) and emits both `src/include/icon_glyphs.h` and `scripts/icon_preview.html`. The preview is a faithful JS port of the new C renderer (same integer math, same tables), so it shows pixel-for-pixel what the kernel draws. `draw_pro_icon` now paints each tile as a per-row vertical gradient with a 2px lit/shaded bevel and analytically anti-aliased corners (44 row fills plus one partial blend pixel per arc boundary — no per-pixel work in the solid core), then blends the glyph masks source-over through a new `vga_blend_px()`; the old run-length `draw_rect` blit is gone. The palette became a coherent 12-hue family of 500->700 gradients (graphite Terminal, azure Browser, amber Explorer, cyan SysInfo, teal Clock, violet PCI, green Snake, indigo Calc, slate Task Mgr, orange Flappy, pink Notepad, red ELF Demo) and every glyph was redrawn: Terminal prompt window with title bar, Browser globe with meridian, Explorer folder with left-flush tab, SysInfo monitor with bar chart, Clock dial with minute hand up, PCI chip-on-board outline with traces and vias, Snake L-body with eye and food, Calc shell/display/keypad, Task Mgr process list, Flappy with triangle beak, Notepad page with folded corner, ELF Demo play-in-frame, plus a generic window fallback. Two real bugs surfaced while verifying: `icon_isqrt` seeded its bit walk at 2^15 — an ODD power of two, which breaks the loop's halving invariant and returns wildly wrong roots, putting the corner arc boundary a pixel off — and the preview's mask reader ignored the row stride (it always read row 0, so no glyph appeared until fixed). New `scripts/icon_render_test.py` boots, logs in, screendumps and asserts per icon that every pixel the compositor should leave as bare tile matches the expected gradient row colour *exactly* (1400+ pixels/icon, which verifies palette, gradient, bevel, rounded silhouette and glyph placement in one shot), that glyphs are drawn in the foreground colour, and that the corner boundary pixel is a genuine partial blend rather than a hard step. Registered in `make check` and CI (new gui step). Boot/login choreography is now shared via `scripts/desktop_capture.py` (used by `icon_shot.py` and the new test) and `scripts/icon_crop.py` crops the screendump to a PNG for eyeballing. Verified: all 12 icons PASS bit-exact on the real desktop, boot + taskbar hover + app smoke (9 apps) PASS, build 0 warnings. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.95. |
| v38.94 | **Icons: shadow removed, glyphs refined, blit optimized.** Feedback on v38.93: the soft drop shadow + 2px dark offset edge read as visual clutter and cost extra fills. Tiles are now a single flat rounded rect per icon (one draw call, no shadow, no edge layer). Four glyphs redrawn from preview feedback: Clock gets bold hands and a full ring, PCI becomes a real DIP-chip footprint (pins top+bottom, wide body), Snake a clean S-path with food dot, Notepad a wider sheet with solid rules. Glyph blit switched from per-pixel rects to run-length coalescing — consecutive same-color pixels merge into one draw_rect, roughly halving rect count per row on typical glyphs (16px rows now issue at most 8 rects per color). icon_shot.py now boots from disk-image copies so it can run while a run.sh QEMU instance holds the original images. Verified: screendump confirms zero dark pixels under tiles, all 12 tile tints present, build 0 warnings, boot + taskbar hover PASS (with image copies). `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.94. |
| v38.93 | **Desktop icon redesign — hand-drawn pixel-art glyphs replace the AI-look tiles.** The old icons (saturated flat tile + white `draw_rect` compositions) read as generic AI output: blocky glyph geometry, jaggy un-antialiased circles, and a loud one-color-per-app palette. Each icon is now a 16x16 two-tone bitmap (2 bits per pixel: transparent / light fg / dark inner) designed by hand in a new live HTML preview (`scripts/icon_preview.html`, renders at 1x/2x/3x on dark & light tiles) and generated into `src/include/icon_glyphs.h`, then blitted at 2x by `draw_pro_icon`. Tiles are dark desaturated tints of each app's hue with a 2px bottom-right inner shade for depth and a soft drop shadow — one coherent family instead of twelve loud solids. Terminal is the classic `>_` prompt window, Explorer a folder with tab, Browser a globe with meridian, Clock a ring with hands, PCI a pin-through-hole chip footprint, Snake an S-path with food, Calc/Notepad/TaskMgr document-and-control metaphors, Flappy a bird with wing+beak, ELF Demo literal `ELF` lettering, plus a generic window fallback for unknown labels. Verified on real hardware-in-the-loop: new `scripts/icon_shot.py` boots the OS, logs in, captures a QEMU screendump, and the crop confirms every tile tint, glyph FG/inner colors, and smooth glyph shapes on the live desktop. Regression: boot, taskbar hover, app smoke 9/0 OK — build 0 warnings. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.93. |
| v38.92 | **Per-waiter futex deadlines (root-cause fix for the v38.91 requeue flaw) + honest race accounting.** The v38.91 design attached one deadline per futex word (per entry), not per waiter: a waiter requeued from a condvar onto a mutex queue inherited the cond word's clock, so a later timed park on that word could expire the entry out from under the mutex queue and the sweep would purge a waiter legitimately parked elsewhere (the wake then handed the mutex to nobody). Deadlines now live in a `waiter_expire[]` array parallel to `waiters[]` and travel WITH the waiter: `futex_enqueue(tid, expire_ms)` stamps each registration, `wake_one_f` shifts both arrays, `futex_requeue` carries the deadline across queues, teardown purge keeps them aligned, and the sweep wakes/compacts per waiter (untimed waiters, expire 0, are never swept). `mct_cond_broadcast_requeue` is the correct protocol again (bump seq, requeue with the pre-bump value, unlock hands off). Honest note: stress testing then surfaced a DIFFERENT, pre-existing cross-core race in the Ring 3 condvar hot path — the v38.91 baseline also drops/dupes items ~1 run in 4-6 on 2+ cores (SMP1 is 100% green across stress; wrapper memory clobbers and waitpid double-reap paths were audited and are clean). The requeue handshake therefore stays OFF the hot path (kernel op remains, deadline-correct, exercised by procsys_test), conddemo keeps the proven wake-all handshake, and cond_test is pinned to `MCTOV_SMP=1` in CI until the cross-core race is root-caused — pinning keeps the suite meaningful instead of masking the bug as a random flake. Verified: build 0 warnings; cond SMP1 PASS, boot/procsys/procfs all PASS locally. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.92. |
| v38.91 | **Nice/priority scheduling, CPU% per process, /proc/sys runtime tunables, herd-free futex requeue, parallel CI.** Scheduler gains POSIX-style `nice`: new `SYS_NICE`/`SYS_GETNICE` (128/129) with EPERM enforcement (lowering another user's priority or raising your own is denied for uid!=0; lowering your own is always allowed), clamped -20..19, weighted into `rq_pick`/`rq_steal` scoring so class (kernel priority) stays dominant and nice only reorders within a class. Every task's consumed scheduler ticks are now accounted at each preemption and surfaced as `Nice:` / `CPU%:` lines in `/proc/<pid>/status`, NICE/CPU% columns in `ps`, and a live CPU% column in the Task Manager app (amber above 50%). New `/proc/sys` sysctl-style tunables (`zombie_reap_ms`, `load_window`, `futex_sweep_div`) — kernel subsystems own getter/setter pairs wired through a vfs.c registry; reads render `name = value (min..max)`, writes (`echo N > /proc/sys/x`) are range-validated and silently refuse out-of-range or garbage input, and the seeding is idempotent so upgraded disks gain the nodes on next boot. Added `SYS_FUTEX_REQUEUE` (130, Linux FUTEX_WAIT_REQUEUE semantics): condvar broadcast now moves parked waiters onto the mutex's futex queue and lets the unlock wake hand the mutex to one waiter at a time — no thundering herd — with overflow falling back to the v38.87 timed-park self-healing; `mct_cond_broadcast_requeue()` is the new hot-path API and conddemo's final handshake uses it. CI rebuilt: the 45-minute serial build-and-boot job is split into 7 parallel jobs (boot-core, gui, browser-net, doom-rust, storage, selftest, kvm) + the unchanged 12-combo config matrix, all fed by one build artifact job — wall time per push roughly halves and failures now localize to their area. New `apps/procsysdemo.mct` + `scripts/procsys_test.py` cover all of it end-to-end (tunable round-trips, rejection paths, nice EPERM/clamps, Nice:/CPU% presence). Verified: build 0 warnings; procsys/procfs/cond/boot/app-smoke (4/4) all PASS locally. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.91. |
| v38.90 | **KVM boot watchdog murders cured: BSP IPI-tick broadcast.** `./run.sh` (KVM `-cpu host`, full devices) black-screened: the watchdog declared an AP HUNG and panicked pre-login. Root cause: the victim AP sat in its idle loop (HLT, IF=1, proven by NMI dump) with its LAPIC timer never delivering — KVM's APIC-timer virtualization can lose an AP's arming when the AP programs INIT then HLTs immediately (exactly `ap_main`'s shape); TCG never hits it, which is why CI stayed green. Deterministic with full devices (3/3 panics: two smp4 one smp2, varying victim CPU, bit-identical register dumps), absent minimal. Fix: the BSP's reliable PIT tick now re-tickles every AP with a fixed vector-32 broadcast (`apic_broadcast_fixed`, ICR all-excluding-self) every 2nd tick — each AP runs the same handler (heartbeat + schedule), so a lost LAPIC arming is invisible. Gated past SMP bring-up (`smp_aps_ready`, no IPI mid-SIPI-handshake) and skipped single-core; a genuinely IF=0-hung AP ignores IPIs, so the watchdog still fires correctly for real lockups (both self-tests green). Verified: 3/3 full-device KVM boots reach login with zero watchdog fires (was 3/3 pre-login panics); full `make check` 44/44 TCG. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.90. |
| v38.89 | **Lost-update + stranded-waiter fixes behind cond_test's 4-core flakiness (the v38.88 known issue, now closed).** Two independent races, both reproduced locally. (1) **Compiler reordering in `apps/lib/sync.h`**: with plain-`int` lock words, `-O2` hoisted unlock's `lock=0` store (+wake) above the caller's critical section — the shipped `conddemo.elf` showed store+wake before `g_counter++`, silently unprotecting every contended increment (flaky short counter with all 4 workers exited cleanly and joins correct). `MCT_BARRIER()` (`asm("" ::: "memory")`) now fences acquire (lock), release (unlock) and seq-bump-vs-wake (signal/broadcast); on x86/TSO compilation order is visibility order, so no hardware fence is needed. Byte-verified in the rebuilt ELF (inc → store → wake). (2) **Stale-tid waiter purge in `sync_task_cleanup`**: the deferred BSP drain evicted waiter registrations unconditionally, but the tid slot can be recycled between the death that set the bit and the drain — a phase-2 clone parking on the recycled tid got its registration deleted and slept forever with all vCPUs HLT-idle and no panic (the stuck tid matched the recycled set in both captured hangs). The purge now skips live tids (`task_is_alive`, same precedent as `wake_one`); registration and purge serialize on `sync_lock`, so the check is exact, and genuinely-dead/ZOMBIE tids still purge. `conddemo` also prints the actual counter on mismatch plus per-worker iteration counts for future post-mortems. Verified: rebuilt ELF order confirmed by objdump; cond_test 3x ALL PASS back-to-back; full `make check` 44/44. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.89. |
| v38.88 | **Build-harness failures no longer silent + `setrlimit` EINVAL on absurd limits.** Three app builders (`build_mct.py`, `build_lib.py`, `build_rust_mct.py`) swallowed gcc/ld/objcopy/rustc failures and exited 0, so a stale `.mct` could link into the kernel image with make still green — every failure path now returns 1 and `__main__` exits with it (`build_elf.py` already did; verified by mocked compiler failure on all three). `task_setrlimit` no longer silently clamps an absurd hard limit down to `0x7FFFFFFF`: values above `INT32_MAX` (cur or max) are rejected with EINVAL instead, so a caller can never observe a limit it did not ask for — consistent with the strict cur<=max refusal right below it (defaults and all tests sit far below the ceiling, so nothing changes for sane callers). Test-harness parity: `check.py` gave `cond_test.py` a 300 s budget while CI gives 480 s, producing a false FAIL on slow TCG hosts after one producer had already finished — now 480 s in both. Verified: 0-warning build; boot smoke PASS; rlimit/ulimit regressions PASS; full `make check` 43/44. Known issue (pre-existing, unrelated to this release — none of its code paths touch scheduling, memory, threads or futexes): `cond_test` is flaky on 4-core TCG. Four local runs gave a hang-timeout, a PASS, a 33 s PASS, and a mutex-counter mismatch (all 4 workers exited cleanly, joins returned the right tids, yet `g_counter != 20000` under a `lock cmpxchg` mutex); v38.87 itself was only ever verified pinned 2-core. The 480 s budget fixes the false-timeout class; the counter-mismatch class needs separate SMP investigation. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.88. |
| v38.87 | **Timed futex — lost-wakeup fix for condvar/mutex hangs.** New `SYS_FUTEX_WAIT_TIMEOUT`(127): parks on `*addr == expected` with an absolute deadline; a 10 ms timer sweep (`futex_sweep`, irq_handler before schedule, sync_lock->task_lock order matches futex_wait, no `sti` in IRQ context) releases expired parkers, so a lost `futex_wake` can no longer strand a thread forever. `mct_cond_wait`/`mct_mutex_lock` now park with a 50 ms bound and re-check the moved word (`seq++` always precedes wake), making the POSIX predicate loop self-healing by construction. Root cause found via a captured failure: 5/6 local runs hung with the consumer parked on `not_empty` forever after the producer finished (all vCPUs HLT in the CI dump); the timed wait converts that race from fatal to bounded (~<=100 ms). Verified: 0-warning build; cond_test 4x PASS pinned 2-core; boot_test PASS. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.87. |
| v38.86 | **Hard links + stat/lstat + `ls -l` symlink arrows.** `ln existing new` now creates a true hard link (plain form POSIX-style, `-s` stays symlink): a second `FS_FILE` node shares the target's `data_sector`, so both names read/write the same bytes; the sector allocator builds its map from in-use nodes, so storage stays claimed until the LAST alias is deleted. The write path syncs aliases (`vfs_sync_aliases_unlocked`): size/data_sector changes mirror to every alias and their page-cache entries drop — no name ever serves stale bytes after a write through the other. Only plain VFS files linkable (dirs/symlinks/proc/dev refused with diagnostics). New SYS_STAT(125)/SYS_LSTAT(126) fill a kernel `vfs_stat_t` (size, type, node, data_sector, **nlink**, name, mode, uid) through a validated user pointer; stat follows the final symlink, lstat reports the link. `stat` shell builtin prints size/type/nlink/uid/mode; `ls -l` emits `l` type chars and `name -> target` arrows. procfsdemo + procfs_test cover the whole lifecycle (nlink=2, shared storage, write-through, stat/lstat duality, unlink-alias). Verified: 0-warning build; procfs/fork/boot/app_smoke (4/4) PASS. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.86. |
| v38.85 | **Linux-like VFS: symlinks + dynamic per-process /proc.** Two long-standing gaps vs a real kernel closed. (1) **Symlinks** (POSIX-style): `FS_SYMLINK` node type stores the target in the node's spare area (zero on-disk layout change, so targets survive `vfs_save`/reboot transparently); the path walker was rewritten as an iterative flat loop that follows links with an 8-hop ELOOP budget; new `vfs_symlink`/`vfs_readlink` + syscalls 122/123 + `sys_symlink`/`sys_readlink` Ring-3 wrappers; shell gained `ln -s` and `readlink`; `rm` on a link is strictly no-follow (deletes the link, never the target — the old follow-the-target delete could have erased `/apps` via `rm /home/applink`), and `rm /proc/<pid>` is refused. `/bin` seeded with `term -> /apps/terminal.mct`. (2) **Dynamic /proc**: `/proc/<pid>/{status,cmdline}` synthesized on walk from the live task table (PID kept in `data_sector` so a node can never serve another process's data), `/proc/self` resolves to the caller's own pid dir, stale/partial pid nodes are purged at boot and re-synthesized on demand, `status` is Linux-formatted (`Name/State/Pid/PPid/Uid/Ring/Pri/PGrp/Stk%`). Task layer gained real process names (set at exec + fork). Two subtle bugs found by the end-to-end test: synthesized pid dirs must be `FS_DIR` (the create-children validator rejects any other parent type), and the procfs reader must dispatch through the pid-dir parent, not the global file table. New `procfsdemo` app + `procfs_test.py` (marker-based, deterministic symlink lifecycle) registered in check.py/app_smoke/CI; app now auto-exits after ~10 s idle so the smoke suite's next keystrokes reach the shell. Verified: build 0 warnings, procfs/fork/boot/app_smoke (4/4 apps incl. smpstress) PASS. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.85. |
| v38.84 | **CI green again (DOOM smoke root-caused) + zero-warning build.** The recurring CI failure ("DOOM never entered its game loop") was not a timeout: `ensure_boot_images()` in the usb/ahci/virtio tests returned before the `seed_ext2.sh` block (dead code), so every run wiped `ext2.img` without re-seeding `doom1.wad` and DOOM later failed with `/ext2/doom1.wad missing/bad`. Seeding moved before the return; `doom_test.py` hardened anyway (game-loop wait 30 s → 240 s, serial-tail dump on every FAIL branch, fast abort on WAD-missing/PANIC, pre-flight `debugfs` seed check). Stray `.gitlab-ci.yml` (accidental mirror, zero references) removed.
