Hardening from the audit: `esp_bad()` crash-dump formatter wrote up to 135 bytes into a 96-byte buffer (overflow on the corrupted-stack path itself) — now 160 B, bounds-checked by construction; `utils.c` includes `acpi.h` (implicit declaration gone); inline asm `rep movsd` → `movsl`; `-z noexecstack` on link. Full clean build now 0 errors / 0 warnings / 0 assembler notices; boot_test PASS. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.84. |
| v38.83 | **Bulk-transfer stall fixed (TCP window-update ACK) + time-based app pacing.** A 200 KB page never finished: pcap showed the guest advertising win 0 at 16 KB received, then silence until our own 15 s FIN timeout — draining the buffer never reopened the window because ACKs only went out on inbound segments (the classic zero-window deadlock; small pages never fill 16 KB, which is why CI stayed green). `net_tcp_recv` now sends a window-update pure ACK when a drain reopens ≥1 MSS (Linux parity). Same root cause class fixed in apps: loop-iteration-paced polling (`tick%100` browser recv, `tick%5000` clock, `%25-frame` flappy physics) assumed fast-spinning loops and slowed 3–10× once loops went hlt-idle at 100 Hz — all three are now wall-clock gated, and the browser drains everything available per poll instead of one 1 KB recv. Verified by pcap (clean FIN close, windows reopening) + socktest/browser_web/app_smoke/pollselect green. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.83. |
| v38.82 | **Wakeup preemption — input latency no longer waits for the 10 ms tick.** Diagnosis of the browser-fetch stutter (EIP sampling showed the system ~93% halted, not saturated): with schedule-on-timer-only, a key/motion/packet IRQ returned to whatever spinner owned the CPU, so the compositor waited up to a full tick. Now kbd/mouse/NIC-RX handlers set a per-CPU `need_resched` flag and `irq_handler` schedules immediately (same cli-first lock discipline as the timer path, verified). Input-to-compositor bound drops 10 ms → sub-ms by construction. Verified: browser_web/app_smoke/thread/cond/fuzz/watchdog green, zero `[PANIC]`. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.82. |
| v38.81 | **Debloat: system blobs out of the kernel binary (~7.3 MB RAM back).** `doom1.wad` (4.1 MB) + `wallpaper.bin` (3.1 MB) + `music.wav` sat in `.data`, resident whether used or not; debug info (~1.3 MB) shipped in the boot image too. Now: `myos.bin` **9.4 MB → 1.1 MB** (`.data` 7.6 MB → 215 KB; symbols split to `myos.bin.debug` for GDB), and the blobs live as plain files on `/ext2` (seeded by `scripts/seed_ext2.sh` via host debugfs; ext2.img 2 MB → 16 MB) loaded on demand — wallpaper once per session with flat fallback, music copied to VFS at boot, WAD **streamed** through `vfs_read_file_offset` so gameplay never needs a contiguous multi-MB heap block (a 4.1 MB preload OOMs: heap high-water is ~21 MB of 24 MB at doom start). Two latent bugs surfaced on the way: the ext2 driver hardcoded a 4096-sector/2 MB drive cap (reads past 2 MB returned zeros — replaced with the superblock count + overflow guard, same for writes), and `fseek` rejected bufferless files (broke the streaming WAD directory read). Verified: login/wallpaper/blur, DOOM game loop, music seed, boot/mount/ahci/usb/virtio/app_smoke green. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.81. |
| v38.80 | **100 Hz tick + millisecond timebase (Linux-HZ style) and an idle that idles.** The PIT ran at 1000 Hz with a same-tick `hlt` guard that starved halting entirely under a fast TCG clock, and the login gate repainted the whole screen at 60 fps to blink a clock colon. Now: `TIMER_HZ=100`, `timer_ticks` counts wall-clock ms (every ms-based consumer — timeouts, sleeps, uptime, blink rates, Ring 3 `sys_get_ticks` — works unchanged, like Linux `jiffies`+`msecs_to_jiffies`), LAPIC follows the PIT rate so the watchdog mesh compares like with like (check/timeout constants rescaled, detection still ~3 s), sleeps stay ms-exact, and both main loop and login gate halt unconditionally + repaint only on change. Measured idle at the login screen under KVM: **~11% of a core → ~0%** (45 s cputime delta), IRQ0 1000/s → 100/s; 10 ms is also the classic HZ=100 timeslice. Verified: watchdog, poll/select, boot green. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.80. |
| v38.79 | **RTL8139 RX straddle fix — the browser "connect lag" on real pages.** Symptom: after typing a URL + Enter, the whole OS felt frozen while connecting, then fine once the page appeared. The browser loop, syscalls and `net_poll` were all proven innocent (non-blocking + yielding; a 10 s silent wait showed no CPU spike). Measurement with a 200 KB instrumented fetch showed the real story: every full-size segment straddling the 8K RX ring end failed the TCP checksum, then slirp backed off exponentially (drops ~1,2,4,8,16,32 s apart) — a multi-second stall that *looks* like a connect freeze. Root cause, confirmed against the QEMU 8.2 source (`rtl8139_write_buffer`): with WRAP set QEMU does **not** split straddling frames — it writes them linearly past 8K into the +1500 overflow area — but `rtl8139_poll_rx` copied the tail from ring offset 0 (stale bytes; the dump literally contained a boot-time DHCP packet, and a Python re-checksum of the true content proved the wire bytes were valid). Fix: copy straddling tails from `ring + 8192`. Small pages almost never straddle, which is why CI stayed green. Verified: 20 KB transfer completes with zero drops (`peer closed` clean), `browser_web_test`/`browser_test`/`socktest` PASS, zero `[PANIC]`. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.79. |
| v38.78 | **Per-task fds 16→32, per-volume backend auto-select & virtio-blk (drives 12+).** (1) `MAX_FDS_PER_TASK` 16→32 (+64 B/task): all 13 `task.c` sites now use the macro — the `j < 16` literals were a leak/corruption trap on the fork/exec/pipeline inherit paths (uncounted refcounts, fds 16–31 leaking into pipeline children and breaking EOF); RLIMIT_NOFILE defaults 32/32 with `rlimittest`/`rlimit_test`/`ulimit_test` updated (`select()`'s `uint32_t` bitmap already fit fd < 32; global table stays 256 as the deliberate system-wide cap). (2) Same-kind volumes coexist: `mount_select_for_node()` walks up to the enclosing mount point and re-runs `ext2_init`/`fat32_init` only on a drive mismatch — every op is atomic under `vfs_lock`, so `/fat32` (drive 3) + `/sata` (drive 4) + `/vblk` (drive 12) each serve their own bytes and the old silent repoint is gone; `df` pins the boot volumes for stable rows. (3) New **virtio-blk driver** (transitional legacy PCI `1AF4:1001`, poll-only with `NO_INTERRUPT`, single queue, FLUSH-after-write for IDE durability parity): drives 12–15 on the same ATA sector API, so ext2/FAT32/`mount()` work unchanged; `MECTOV_VIRTIO=1 ./run.sh` attaches a `virtio.img` disk. Bring-up bug worth documenting: the used-ring offset MUST be derived from the device's QueueNum (legacy PCI has no size register) — a smaller driver-side ring puts avail/used where the device never looks (used stayed 0, no IRQ, every transfer timed out). `scripts/virtio_test.py` proves mount/cp/host-`mtools` round-trip plus a cross-volume write landing on `fat32.img` (the auto-select proof). Verified: virtio/mount/fat32/rlimit/ulimit/ahci/usb green, zero `[PANIC]`. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.78. |
| v38.77 | **Window dragging fixed + the CI-red ATA false positive.** Two regressions, both caught by tests. First, v38.75's content-drag forwarding early-returned on every held-button mouse move, so the titlebar-drag/resize logic below it never executed — windows could not be moved at all (drag a window and it stayed frozen). Pure moves now fall through to the drag/resize blocks, and any new click clears stale content_drag flags so two windows can never receive drag events simultaneously. Second, v38.74's `ata_no_drive()` sampled the ATA Status register *before* selecting the target unit; Status is only meaningful for the currently selected drive, so a leftover selection on an absent slave read `0x00` and healthy master drives were rejected — `vfs_save` failed on any boot with a single attached disk, which is why the ASLR regression went red at v38.74 (its config attaches one disk; boot_test's four-disk config masked it). Fix: select the drive, then sample. Verified with a titlebar drag probe (browser window lands exactly at +80/+64), aslr_test (2 boots, cross-boot + per-exec randomization), paint/browser regressions. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.77. |
| v38.76 | **Pixel Paint saves/opens images to the VFS — and a launch crash exposed an undocumented MCT entry-point rule.** Paint gained Save/Open (toolbar buttons or `s`/`o`) persisting the cell grid to `home/paint.mcp` via the same Ring 3 file syscalls notepad uses (`sys_open` -> `sys_create_file` -> `sys_write`/`sys_read`), a compact binary format (`MCP1` magic + 336 palette-index bytes), status-bar feedback for every I/O outcome, and transient status messages on Clear. Integrating it surfaced a sharp edge worth documenting: the first build defined `main()` instead of `_start`, `build_mct.py` silently fell back to entry offset 0, and the app page-faulted instantly on launch (`EIP=0x08000000`, the mapping base — the loader jumps to base+offset with a zero offset). Browser always worked because it declares `void _start()` explicitly. Pixel Paint now follows the same convention; build_mct.py still warns on missing `_start` (potential follow-up: make it an error). New CI assertion in `scripts/paint_test.py`: paint a red cell, Save, Clear, Open, and assert the cell is restored — the full roundtrip through the kernel VFS. PASS stable; `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.76. |
| v38.75 | **Pixel Paint — a new Ring 3 paint app, plus WM content-drag forwarding.** The app is shaped by a hard Ring 3 constraint: windows render through a replayed display list capped at 512 commands and the WM clears the client buffer on every re-render, so a freehand canvas recording one rect per brush point would overflow within seconds. Pixel Paint instead stamps a coarse 24px cell grid — a cell is at most ONE display-list record regardless of how often it is painted and unpainted cells are never recorded, so the worst-case frame (every cell painted + full UI) stays ~400 records, with zero kernel changes. 8-color palette (click or keys 1-8; paper doubles as eraser), 3 brush sizes (B1-B3 / `b`), drag painting, Clear (`c`), status bar, Quit (ESC/`q`). Bringing it up surfaced a real WM gap: `wm_handle_mouse` forwarded mouse events to apps only on rising-edge clicks, so no app could ever receive move-with-button-held input — the window manager now tracks `content_drag` per window and forwards every move to the app while the button is held on its content (cleared on release), the enabling primitive for any future drag tool. Start menu grew to 13 items / 404px (pixel icons and indices for Lock/Logout/Power remapped; geometry updated in 4 existing tests); paint.mct is seeded to VFS both paths and registered in check.py + CI. New `scripts/paint_test.py`: boots, launches from the Start menu, asserts canvas + all 5 accent swatches, stamps two green cells, drags a full run, recolors blue, clears back to paper (permutation-tolerant color matching) — 2/2 stable runs, no PANIC. Regression: startmenu_search, explorer, browser, browser_web, boot all green. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.75. |
| v38.74 | **ATA/VFS fail fast when no disk is attached — the "infinite boot hang" is gone.** The waiters already had a 100k-iteration timeout, but an absent controller/drive reads back status 0x00/0xFF (floating bus: no BSY, no DRQ, no ERR), so a WRITE SECTORS with nothing behind it burned the full timeout *per sector* — a 256-sector `vfs_save` node-table flush spent minutes of wasted port I/O that looked like a hang (found while verifying KVM in v38.73 with a disk-less QEMU invocation). Fix: `ata.c` gained `ata_no_drive()` (status 0x00/0xFF bail) checked up front in the single-sector PIO read/write, both multi-sector PIO paths and the BMIDE DMA path; `ata_read_sector`/`ata_write_sector` now return status (header + all 5 vfs.c call sites updated); `vfs_save_unlocked`/`vfs_load_unlocked` log one clear line (`[VFS] save/load failed: ATA ... (disk attached?)`) and abort instead of grinding through every sector. Verified: with disks — normal boot to login, 0 PANIC; without any disk — boot now reaches login in seconds with a clear message where it previously ground for minutes. boot_test + browser_test green. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.74. |
| v38.73 | **KVM verified working on the dev box + clearer fallback hint in `run.sh`.** With VirtualBox off, `/dev/kvm` and VT-x are usable again — the auto-probe now succeeds and `./run.sh` runs `-enable-kvm -cpu host` without falling back. Measured on the same box, identical config: boot-to-login 8s (KVM) vs 9s (TCG), steady-state desktop CPU **~24% vs ~103%** (4x less host CPU idle; the streaming-network gap from v38.72 is far wider). Verification incident worth logging: a KVM boot appears to hang forever in `ata_wait_drq_drive` writing LBA 18 — but that run was launched without `-hda` disk images, so the kernel was polling a controller with no drive; with `run.sh`'s full drive set KVM boots clean, login + main loop confirmed via serial. The `ata_wait_drq_drive` infinite wait is a known rough edge left for a follow-up hardening pass. The KVM-failure fallback message now explains the usual cause (VirtualBox or another hypervisor holding VT-x, nested virt off) instead of only reporting "entry failed". |
| v38.72 | **Browser fetch no longer lags the whole desktop while loading.** A per-phase CPU probe (delayed + chunk-streaming HTTP server, 0.5s samples) reproduced the user report exactly: QEMU's host CPU jumped from a ~35-43% desktop baseline to ~75-88% while packets streamed in during a fetch, then fell straight back when loading finished — the lag was the data transfer, not the wait. Four hot spots, all measured then fixed: (1) per-packet RX serial logging in `net.c` (4-6 locked serial writes with port I/O *per packet* on the IRQ path) is now gated behind `net_rx_debug`/`-DNET_RX_DEBUG`, one-shot event logs (DNS/redirect/DHCP/ARP) stay on; (2) the browser repainted its whole window — a full WM recomposite — on every received chunk and now repaints at most every 10th chunk with the final page always painted on completion; (3) `rtl8139_send_packet` spun up to 10k port-I/O reads waiting for TX-ok inside the IRQ path (IF=0) and is now fire-and-forget behind the existing OWN-bit check + 4-deep descriptor queue; (4) bonus root cause of the constant idle baseline: `timer_update_rate_if_second()` read the CMOS (~1000x/s via `rtc_read_time()`'s UIP busy-wait) from the main loop despite its "one CMOS read per second" comment, now gated to two reads per second by the tick counter. Remaining streaming overhead is inherent per-packet TCG emulation — under KVM the same path is an order of magnitude cheaper. browser_test, browser_web_test, boot_test and explorer_test all green. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.72. |
| v38.71 | **Mini Browser opens real hostnames end-to-end — kernel DNS client + port-80 gateway redirect, verified offline-deterministically.** The full chain now works from a bare hostname: `sys_dns_resolve("localhost")` sends a real UDP DNS query through the slirp resolver (10.0.2.3 → host resolver), the resolved IP is connected on port 80, the kernel's Web Gateway Proxy redirect rewrites it to 10.0.2.2:8888, the gateway answers, and the reply renders in the window with the status bar. Verifying it surfaced two fixes: `html_to_text` collapsed every `\n` into a space (fine for HTML, but it shredded the gateway's line-structured plain-text replies into one blob — plain text with no tags seen now keeps its line breaks), and `scripts/gateway.py` gained a deterministic fake mode (`MECTOV_GATEWAY_FAKE_TEXT` env) that answers every request with a canned page echoing the guest's `Host`/`Path` back — so the whole chain is CI-testable with **zero internet dependency** (the new `scripts/browser_web_test.py`, registered in `check.py` + CI, resolves `localhost`, which comes from the host's `/etc/hosts`, and asserts the DNS query, the A-record resolve, the port-80 redirect, the gateway echo, the rendered rows and the status bar). Verified 3/3 stable runs; the IP-literal `browser_test` and `boot_test` stay green. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.71. |
| v38.70 | **Mini Browser fetches real HTTP pages — the guest can now open a web page served from the host.** `apps/browser.c` was rewritten around a full fetch pipeline: URL parsing (`host[:port][/path]`, `http://` stripped, IP literals like `10.0.2.2` bypass DNS entirely and connect directly — DNS stays for hostnames), an HTTP/1.0 `GET` with `Host:`/`User-Agent: MectovBrowser/3.0` (1.0 avoids chunked encoding the mini stack cannot parse), response parsing that strips headers and keeps the status code, an HTML-to-text renderer (tags stripped, `<script>`/`<style>` skipped, block tags become newlines, `&amp;`-style entities decoded, `<title>` captured), and a live status bar (`HTTP <code> - <bytes> - <title>`). Testing it end-to-end surfaced a **real kernel bug**: with a synchronous TX backend (rtl8139 + slirp) the peer's SYN-ACK can arrive via the RX IRQ *before* `tcp_start_conn()`'s `seq++` executes, so `net_handle_tcp()` compared `peer_ack` against the bare ISN, the handshake silently failed and the connection timed out every time; the corrected final ACK then exposed the second half of the same race (the interrupted `seq++` still ran afterwards, corrupting the sequence the server expected → peer RST). The fix accepts both orderings, advances `c->seq` in the race branch and guards the `seq++` on `TCP_SYN_SENT`. Also fixed while bringing this up: Ring 3 GUI apps now honor the WM's client-size event (type 5) — the browser drew its status bar at fixed window coordinates and the 20px titlebar + frame clipped it entirely off-screen. New `scripts/browser_test.py` (registered in `check.py` + CI) runs a real HTTP server on the host, boots the OS via slirp, launches the browser from the Start menu, types `10.0.2.2:<port>` (non-80 so it bypasses the Web Gateway Proxy redirect) and asserts the server saw `GET /` with the correct `Host`/`User-Agent`, the page rendered parsed (4–12 rows, not raw HTML), the status bar painted, DNS was never queried for the IP literal and no PANIC/WATCHDOG fired — verified 4/4 stable runs. Regression: boot_test + explorer_test green. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.70. |
| v38.69 | **File Explorer GUI regression + KVM→TCG auto-fallback in `run.sh`.** The Ring 3 File Explorer (v35.3, 606 lines: directory listing with icons, back stack, path bar, new-file/folder dialogs, context menu, double-click open) had never been tested since it landed, so a new `scripts/explorer_test.py` (registered in `check.py` + CI) boots the OS, launches it from the Start menu and pixel-asserts the root listing (11 rows, folder icons), navigation into `/home` (exactly one entry: `user/`), into the empty `/home/user`, and the `[<]` back button returning to root — with zero PANIC/watchdog in the serial log. Writing the test surfaced the QEMU monitor gotchas now encoded in it: `mouse_move` is *relative* (the cursor is tracked and parked off-window before every screendump so it cannot pollute assertions), and the screendump channel order is not RGB. Separately, `run.sh` now survives hosts whose KVM cannot run the `qemu32` machine (`KVM: entry failed, hardware error 0x0`): it captures QEMU stderr, detects the failure within 8 s and relaunches on TCG automatically, drops SMP 4→2 for the fallback (TCG runs one host thread per vCPU — with the spinlock-heavy SMP bring-up, 2 cores feel 2–3× smoother than 4), cleans up stale QEMU instances still holding the serial port before restarting, prints a clear message if QEMU exits unexpectedly, and honors `MECTOV_KVM=0/1` + `MECTOV_SMP=n` overrides. |
| v38.68 | **CI config matrix — boot + fork smoke-tested on 12 CPU×RAM×SMP combinations instead of one.** The full regression suite runs on a single configuration (`qemu32,+nx` / 128 MB / SMP 4); Mectov's own history shows combo-only bugs are real (the KVM fork-GPF and the `qemu32,+nx` boot stall each appeared only under one specific combination). A new parallel `config-matrix` workflow job reuses the built artifacts and smokes `boot_test.py` + `fork_test.py` on every combination of CPU model (`qemu32` plain — the NX-less path the default suite never runs — and `Nehalem`) × RAM (64 MB — allocator pressure with the 48 MB kernel reserve — and 256 MB — frames above 128 MB) × CPU count (1 / 2 / 4 — SMP bring-up, runqueue, watchdog mesh). `boot_test.py` and `fork_test.py` gained `--cpu / --mem-mb / --smp` flags (defaults unchanged). Locally verified the riskiest combos first: NX-less 64 MB SMP 1 and Nehalem 256 MB SMP 4 both boot, log in and fork cleanly.
| v38.67 | **Per-core watchdog mesh — a BSP hard-lockup is no longer a silent death.** The v38.64 watchdog hung an AP and had the BSP detect it, leaving one blind spot: if the BSP itself died with IF=0 (cli spin / lock deadlock in BSP-only code), its own detector died with it and nobody fired. The detector is now a mesh: every core's vector-32 timer IRQ (PIT on the BSP, LAPIC timer on the APs, both ~1 kHz) bumps its own heartbeat AND tick slots, then runs `watchdog_check()` — cadence-gated on that core's own tick clock — watching every PEER including the BSP. Detector state is per-core `[detector][peer]` rows, so a BSP hang is timed by the APs' clocks; detection is single-winner (`lock xchg` on `wd_fired` — exactly one core prints the marker and drives the NMI-IPI dump + reboot, the others park, no double-reset or interleaved output). `panic_finish()` already works from any core (self-snapshot + NMI all-except-self), so the detecting AP dumps the hung BSP from inside its cli spin. New `wd_self_test_bsp` boot arg + `watchdog_bsp_test.py`: the BSP cli-spins from its own main loop and an AP must declare `CPU 0 HUNG` (~3 s later), dump all four cores (BSP EIP lands in the spin, EFL shows IF=0 proof the NMI got through) and reboot. Regression: the AP-hang `watchdog_test.py` stays green, and boot + app_smoke (60 s SMP load) show 0 false positives with four concurrent detectors. Whole-system lockups (every core IF=0) remain undetectable by any software watchdog — documented as a boundary.
| v38.66 | **TLB shootdown IPI — a page-table mutation on one core is now visible to every other core instantly, closing the v38.55 SMP gap.** The audit that framed this: `schedule()` reloads CR3 on every tick, so a sibling thread running a shared address space on another core could keep a stale WRITABLE TLB entry for a page just COW-marked at fork (or unmapped/freed) for up to ~1 ms. `vmm_tlb_shootdown()` broadcasts a directed fixed IPI (vector 0x61, new stub + gate) to every other real core; each peer's handler acks, then reloads its CR3 — a full flush — but only if it is CURRENTLY running the target page directory (exact under PAE without PCID: entries for a non-current CR3 cannot exist). Senders are serialized (one shootdown in flight, cli + lock like `frame_alloc`) and the ack wait is bounded, so a peer stuck IF=0 degrades to the old flush-at-next-reload instead of deadlocking. Wired at the three mutation sites that can race a peer: `fork_common` (sibling-on-peer-core detected under task_lock, flush fired right after the lock drops — zero IPIs for the single-threaded case), `SYS_VMM_FREE` and `task_munmap` (frames already returned to the allocator must never be touched through a stale mapping). New `tlb_sd_test.py` CI self-test parks one AP and verifies the protocol deterministically with counters: a matching shootdown reloads (`recv=2 reload=1`), a non-matching one is acked but skipped. Regression: boot, app_smoke (fork/thread/signal SMP), mmapfile/iocache/syncfile/fat32 all green; 0 new compile warnings.
| v38.65 | **Sequential readahead for the ATA block layer — a cold sequential stream no longer pays one disk command per small demand.** The v38.62 block cache serves REPEATED reads from RAM, but its first (cold) pass through a stream still hit the disk once per request: ext2 reads file data one 4 KB block at a time (8-sector demands), FAT32 walks cluster chains in small slices, and a Ring 3 app streaming a big file through a small buffer (`bigread` reads 4 KB chunks) turns a 160 KB read into ~40 separate ATA commands. The ATA multi-sector wrapper (`ata.c`) now tracks each drive's access stream and, once a SMALL-request (≤16-sector) sequential pattern is confirmed by two contiguous demands, issues one wider prefetch of the next 64 sectors (32 KB) into the block cache after each miss — the rest of the stream then hits RAM. Wide readers need no help and get none: the VFS layer already batches big reads into ≤128-sector commands, and demands of that width deliberately never arm the prefetch (no prefetch storms). Correctness is unchanged because a prefetch rides the exact same anti-stale path as a demand read: `blkcache_gen_now()` snapshots the write generation before the prefetch I/O and `blkcache_commit(gen)` discards the fill if a concurrent write bumped it mid-read, so prefetched bytes can never shadow newer disk contents, and write-through invalidation is untouched. Locking: a new leaf `ra_lock` guards the per-drive stream state (u32s) AND the single prefetch buffer — two cores prefetching different drives must not share `ra_buf` — with order ra > ata (the prefetch `_io` takes `ata_lock` internally) and ra > blkcache; nothing acquires `ra_lock` while holding either, so no cycle exists. **The verification is deterministic instead of wall-clock** (TCG timing noise previously forced `min(hot)` tricks): a new `/proc/atastats` virtual file exposes `ATA_RD_CMDS` (how many real multi-sector disk-read commands have been issued) and `RA_FILLS` (prefetch batches), readable from Ring 3; `apps/bigread.mct` was rewritten from six whole-file reads into five 4 KB-chunk STREAM passes over `/bench.big` (the pattern the feature exists for, still >PCACHE_MAX_FILE) and reads the counter around each pass. The verdicts: the cold pass must issue ≤18 disk commands (measured a stable **13** across boots — ~40+ without readahead, since the 8-sector demands previously went to the disk one command at a time) and every later pass ≤2 (measured **0** — the block cache alone serves them), with the seeded byte pattern (index mod 256) verified on every pass so a wrong prefetch range or stale fill cannot pass silently. Timings still print to the log but never drive a verdict. The `/proc/atastats` node is also created on the disk-upgrade path (existing disks gain it like `/proc/dmesg` did), so no full disk rebuild is needed. Regression: boot, iocache, mmapfile, mount (ext2 write→read), fat32 (write→read coherency) and the SATA/USB drives (which get readahead too, through the same wrapper dispatch) all green. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.65. |
| v38.64 | **Multi-core hard-lockup watchdog — a core that dies with interrupts disabled no longer hangs the whole system silently.** The gap this closes is exactly the failure mode of the KVM fork-GPF and qemu32,+nx stalls this project hit earlier: a spinlock deadlock or an accidental cli+spin on one AP leaves the other cores (and CI) waiting forever with zero log lines. `src/sys/watchdog.c` (new) + `src/drivers/timer.c` build a heartbeat: every core's vector-32 timer interrupt (PIT on the BSP, local APIC timer on the APs) bumps its own slot in `wd_heartbeat[]` FIRST, before the BSP-only branch — a core stuck with IF=0 can never deliver that interrupt, so its heartbeat freezes. The BSP's timer IRQ calls `watchdog_check()` every 100 ticks and tracks, per AP, the last heartbeat value it saw and the BSP tick it last changed: frozen for 3000 ticks (~3 s) is a hard lockup (legitimate IF=0 critical sections are sub-millisecond, so the margin is enormous). A core is only watched after its heartbeat has been observed at least once, so APs still booting — or a dead LAPIC timer — can never false-positive. On a hit the BSP prints a `[WATCHDOG] CPU n HUNG` marker and falls into the v38.22 multi-core panic dump: the NMI-IPI reaches the hung core even from inside its cli spin (NMI ignores IF), snapshots exactly where it was stuck, and `panic=reboot` exits CI cleanly. Everything is lock-free (raw stores + try-writes — the detector runs in IRQ context where taking a lock held by a hung core would deadlock the dump). Self-test: the new **`wd_self_test` boot arg** makes the BSP send a directed fixed IPI on a new vector-0x60 gate (`interrupt_entry.asm` stub + `idt.c` gate + handler registered on every core through the shared IDT) to the first AP, whose handler drops into cli+spin forever; `scripts/watchdog_test.py` (registered in `check.py` + CI) boots with `panic=reboot wd_self_test` and asserts the synthetic hang fired, the watchdog detected the stalled AP, all four `[PANIC] CPU n` lines appear (the hung AP's EIP lands inside `wd_hang_ipi_handler` with EFLAGS IF=0 — the register-dump proof), `panic=reboot` reset, and QEMU exits cleanly. Bring-up exposed a **latent bug in the panic path itself (pre-v38.22, never triggered before)**: `panic_nmi_handler` read `r->useresp` unconditionally, but only a Ring 3 frame has useresp/ss on the stack — for a Ring 0 frame that field is 8 bytes ABOVE the pushed frame (registers_t offset 0x40), and when the NMI catches a freshly-switched task near its stack top (ESP within bytes of esp0) the read crosses into the unmapped guard page and faults INSIDE the dump: spurious "KERNEL STACK OVERFLOW" panics and half-written register slots (only EIP/CS filled, everything else zero). Fixed by branching on CS RPL (`(r->cs & 3) ? r->useresp : r->esp`) so a Ring 0 frame never touches useresp — this also makes the v38.22 panic dump itself more correct. Verified: watchdog_test green (hang → detect in ~3 s → clean 4-core dump → reboot, ~11 s), panic_test green (4/4 cores, full registers), boot_test green with **zero watchdog firings** (no false positives on a normal boot), and app_smoke (smpstress + sigdemo + crashme, ~4 min of 4-core load) green with zero false firings. Known bound: the detector lives on the BSP, so a BSP hang is undetectable by this design (same hard-lockup scope as Linux's per-core NMI watchdog, which this could grow into). `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.64. |
| v38.63 | **ASLR in the ELF loader — every ELF app now loads at a CSPRNG-random base.** The blocker audit found first: every `.mct` app and the legacy ELF path were compiled `-fno-pie -fno-pic`, with absolute `0x08000000` addresses baked in at link time — and the flat MCT format carries no relocation metadata, so sliding such an image would crash instantly. The ELF format does carry relocations, so that path became position-independent end-to-end: `scripts/build_elf.py` now compiles ELF apps `-fPIE` and links with `ld -pie` → ET_DYN at offset 0; i386 `-fPIE` codegen is purely PC-relative (globals are addressed through the `__x86.get_pc_thunk` runtime-PC trick) and static linking yields **zero relocations** (verified: `readelf -r` empty, no absolute references in objdump) — so the image runs at any base with no fix-ups. `build_elf_image` in `src/sys/loader.c` accepts ET_DYN alongside legacy ET_EXEC and maps its segments at `bias + p_vaddr`, where bias is a page-aligned `get_random_u32` draw in `[0x08000000, 0x08E00000)` — 3584 possible bases, bounded by real kernel constraints (user range checks treat 0x08000000 as the floor, SYS_MALLOC caps at 0x08F00000, the user library sits at 0x09000000); entry point and heap start follow the same bias. A defensive relocation pass (~30 lines) handles `.rel.dyn`/`.rel.plt` (R_386_32/GLOB_DAT/JMP_SLOT/RELATIVE — each just `+= bias` since the link base is 0) with strict bounds checks, so if a future toolchain emits PIC objects they relocate correctly; corrupt files are skipped, never crashed. Legacy ET_EXEC keeps bias 0 (identical old behavior — that code genuinely cannot move) and MCT apps stay fixed; both documented as format limits, not oversights. New `scripts/aslr_test.py` (registered in `check.py` + CI) boots twice and asserts the `[ASLR]` load-base logs differ across boots **and** across execs — observed `0x08397000`/`0x08BF3000` vs `0x08499000`/`0x08994000` — with syncdemo (semaphore + futex, globals shared across threads: the strongest relocation test) running correctly at a non-default base and the GUI elfdemo at `0x08442000`. Regression: boot, app_smoke (fork/SMP/signal) and a full compile all clean; the Makefile now makes `.elf` targets depend on `build_elf.py` so any toolchain change forces app rebuilds. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.63. |
| v38.62 | **Block cache for the raw block layer — ext2/FAT32 reads stop hitting the disk.** The audit behind this: Mectov's whole-file page cache (pcache, v38.x) only serves MECTOVFS files up to 128 KB; ext2 and FAT32 had **zero caching** — every block read (directories, inode tables, group bitmaps, indirect blocks, FAT cluster chains that re-read the same FAT sectors once per entry) and every repeated whole-file read larger than the page cache went through PIO/DMA to the disk each time. `blkcache.c` (new, v38.62) is an LRU sector cache — 512 × 512 B = 256 KB in static `.bss` — that sits at the `ata_read/write_sectors_drive` entry points, *under every filesystem*, and also covers USB/AHCI-backed drives through the same dispatch. Coherence is write-through and generation-checked, not optimistic: every ATA write invalidates the affected range **after** the data reaches the disk and bumps a write generation; a reader only inserts a freshly-read range if the generation captured at its miss still matches, so bytes racing a concurrent write are discarded, never cached (a stale fill can never shadow newer disk contents). The read path is a single compare fast-out when no sectors are cached, so boot and cold I/O pay ~nothing. Locking: `blkcache_lock` is a leaf, ordered `vfs > pcache > blkcache > ata` (documented in `docs/kernel-locking.md`); the critical sections never fault, so the #PF handler's lock-free VFS path stays safe. Bring-up at QEMU exposed two tuning bugs: a 384-sector cache evicted *itself* while absorbing the 160 KB benchmark (320 sectors) plus boot residue — raised to 512; and TCG timer preemption stretches any single big copy, so the benchmark verdict uses `min(hot reads)` instead of the mean. `apps/bigread.mct` (whose 160 KB file deliberately exceeds the page cache) now asserts the cache: read 0 is the cold disk read at 10–14 ms, later reads are served from RAM at 0–100 µs (~100×); `scripts/bigread_test.py` enforces ≥4×. Regression: iocache, mmapfile, fat32 (FAT write→read coherency), mount (ext2 write→read), usb (USB3 storage through the same cache layer), ahci, boot — all green, data verified byte-for-byte on every read so a stale-fill or wrong-offset bug cannot pass silently. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.62. |
| v38.61 | **Durability: `fsync()`/`sync()` syscalls + periodic write-back of dirty file-backed mmap pages.** The audit behind this: Mectov's regular I/O stack — MECTOVFS, ext2, FAT32 and the whole-file pcache — is already synchronous *write-through* (every `write()` reaches the disk before returning; ext2 superblock/BGD/bitmap/inode and FAT sector updates flush immediately), so the ONLY data that can be lost on a power cut is dirty **MAP_SHARED file-backed mmap** pages, which previously waited for `msync`/`munmap`/task exit. `SYS_FSYNC` (120) now flushes the file behind an fd — any task's mappings of that node, not just the caller's; `SYS_SYNC` (121) flushes every flushable dirty region system-wide; the BSP main loop runs an automatic **~5 s write-back** (a dirty mapping left untouched by its app is on disk within one interval); and `shutdown()`/`reboot()` sync before power is cut. Implementation notes: `mmap_flush_file` was parameterized by owner task and now reads each dirty page's content **through the owner's page tables** via the kernel identity map — a flush of *another* task's mapping needs no CR3 switch — run under `task_lock` (documented `task_lock > vfs_lock` order; a region whose owner is executing on another core is skipped and retried next interval instead of racing its faults). Two real bugs surfaced at bring-up: (1) after a flush cleared a page's dirty bit the page stayed writable, so the software dirty tracker (which only fires on the RO→RW fault) never saw any *later* write — a silent data-loss window — fixed by downgrading every written-back page to read-only again (`mmap_page_make_ro`, +invlpg; cross-task safe because the owner's next context switch reloads CR3 — no TLB-shootdown IPI exists, vmm.c v38.55 note); (2) the first cross-task page copy did a fixed 4096-byte memcpy into a `file_size`-sized buffer — instant heap-canary trip on a 29-byte test file, fixed with `copylen`. New demo + regression: `apps/syncfiledemo.mct` dirties a page, persists it via `fsync(fd)`, re-reads through a fresh fd to prove it is on disk while the mapping is still alive, then dirties again and **sleeps 12 s calling nothing** — the kernel's periodic write-back must flush on its own — and re-reads again; `scripts/syncfile_test.py` (registered in `check.py` + CI) asserts both `[MMAP] flushed` events and exit 0. Verified: syncfile, mmapfile (msync/munmap regression), boot, app_smoke (fork/SMP/signal) and the syscall fuzzer (which now throws random args at the two new numbers) all green. Honest bound: `fsync`/periodic sync skip a region whose owner is *currently running* elsewhere — bounded, retried next interval. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.61. |
| v38.60 | **USB HID class driver — xHCI keyboard + mouse (boot protocol), with PS/2 as the fallback.** The xHCI driver (v38.56 mass storage) now also classifies each port's device: HID boot interfaces (`03/01/01` keyboard / `03/01/02` mouse) get their interrupt IN endpoint configured (EP ctx type 7, Interval exponent in dw0 bits 16..23 from `bInterval` — QEMU asserts it nonzero), `SET_PROTOCOL(1)` boot protocol, and a single Normal-IN IOC TRB kept armed on a per-device ring (re-armed on every completion event; a NAKed transfer stays pending inside QEMU, so one TRB in flight is the right depth and bursty input queues in the device instead of being lost). Reports are translated into the exact byte stream the PS/2 path would produce: the 8-byte keyboard report (modifiers + up to 6 key usages) is diffed against the previous report and fed as set-1 make/break scancodes through the new `kbd_feed_scancode()` (same modifier tracking, feed-time mods snapshot, entropy feed), with L/R modifier bits collapsed and a usage→set-1 table covering letters, digits, punctuation, F1–F12, nav and keypad; the mouse report (buttons/dx/dy/wheel, HID axes: +y is down) lands in `mouse_hid_report()` with the same clamps/scroll semantics. Every consumer — terminal, login gate, WM, DOOM, `SYS_GET_KEY`, auto-lock — works unchanged, and PS/2 remains the fallback when no USB HID device exists (no-op fast-out in `xhci_hid_poll`, called once per main-loop and login-gate iteration; the gate blocks the main loop at boot/lock/logout so it must drain itself). A HID report landing mid-bulk-transfer is dispatched by `event_wait`'s discard path, so none is ever lost; the device pool grows to `USB_MAX_DRIVES + USB_MAX_HID` slots with a `xhci_drive_slot[]` ordinal map so a keyboard on a lower-numbered port can never displace a drive number. QEMU routing made the test genuine: `sendkey`/`mouse_move` go to the first ACTIVE handler, and the usb-kbd/usb-mouse become active on their first interrupt-IN poll — ahead of PS/2. One real bug found at bring-up: the Transfer Event length field is the *residue* (0 for a full-max-packet IN transfer), so every report was read as zero bytes until bytes-received = `mps − residue`. `scripts/usb_test.py` now attaches `usb-kbd` + `usb-mouse` behind `qemu-xhci` and asserts both enumerate, `[HID] kbd first report` (login typed through the USB keyboard), and `[HID] mouse first report` (cursor motion through xHCI — probe verified the cursor lands at the exact accumulated delta), while the USB3 storage round-trip still passes on the same controller; boot_test + app_smoke green (PS/2 fallback unaffected). Known bounds, same scope as storage: FS/LS devices and report-protocol HID (subclass 0) are logged and skipped. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.60. |
| v38.59 | **Local CI-parity test runner — `make check` — and the first release tag.** `scripts/check.py` mirrors `.github/workflows/build-boot-test.yml` suite-for-suite (same order, same `--timeout` values, same fresh `disk.img`/`ext2.img`/`fat32.img`), so a green local run is the same signal as a green CI run without the 23-minute GitHub round trip. New Makefile targets: `check` (full battery), `check-quick` (11-suite subset, ~6–8 min) and `iso` (kernel → `grub-mkrescue`); per-suite output lands in `.check/<suite>.log` with a concise pass/fail table + wall time saved to `.check/summary.txt`; `CHECK_ARGS=` passes extra flags through (`--only=<suite>`, `--keep-images`, `--kvm`). The fork/fputest **KVM regressions are opt-in** — `/dev/kvm` presence is not enough: this dev host's KVM cannot run the `qemu32,+nx` machine (the guest stalls at `[SMP] Waking up CPU APIC ID 0x1`, AP wake never completes — the same quirk as the earlier `doom --kvm` hang) while CI runners execute the same binary green, so CI stays authoritative for KVM. First full local run of the final runner: **33/33 TCG suites PASS in 23m 58s (exit 0)** — boot, login, lock, autolock, hardening, fbmap, taskbar hover, startmenu search, fork, jobcontrol, fputest, nxtest, fat32, mount, ahci, usb, socktest, poweroff, pollselect, fuzz, iocache, app_smoke, doom, mmapfile, rusthello, lseekfile, panic, perm, thread, cond, rlimit, ulimit, bigread. Also: annotated **git tag `v38.58`** now marks the CI-validated v38.58 release commit. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.59. |
| v38.58 | **Full toolchain optimization + the two latent bugs it exposed, and xHCI audit hardening (bulk-DCI bound + trace gating).** (1) **Userland builds at `-O2`** — every `.mct` app, `libc.mct` and the ELF demos had compiled at `-O0` since day one; `build_mct.py`/`build_lib.py`/`build_elf.py` now pass `-O2`. That switch instantly exposed a latent bug `-O0` had masked: the `int 0x80` wrappers in `src/include/syscall.h` carried **no `"memory"` clobber**, so GCC legally assumed the kernel never touches memory — `waitpid` exit-status reads were constant-folded to 0 (every child looked like exit 0 → `smpstress` FAIL) and the `sigaction` struct initializer was dead-store-eliminated (the kernel iret'd into a garbage handler → SIGSEGV → children silently killed). `"memory"` is now declared on `syscall()`, `syscall5()` and all 31 raw-asm wrappers, and `smpstress`'s CPU-burn accumulator went `volatile` so the loop can't be optimized away. (2) **The kernel was being compiled PIC without anyone knowing** — Ubuntu gcc's `-m32` defaults to `-fPIE` even for `-c`, so a kernel linked at a fixed 1 MB by `linker.ld` (never relocated, ever) shipped a `.got`/`.got.plt` and ~730 `__x86.get_pc_thunk` call sites; `-fno-pie -fno-pic` + pinned `-march=i686` (kernel + embedded DOOM) remove the indirection entirely — 0 `.got` sections, 0 thunks. LTO was tried and **rejected on measurement**: 17.8 fps vs 21.8 fps fitted DOOM frame rate under TCG, +560 KB binary, and two workarounds (a `__divdi3` carve-out for `doom_libc.o`, and `-Wl,--build-id=none` because the gcc driver's `.note.gnu.build-id` pushed `.text` — and the multiboot header with it — to file offset 8192, the outer edge of the bootloader's 8 KB scan window, so the kernel never booted). (3) **xHCI audit fixes** (`src/drivers/xhci.c`): `usb_enumerate` now validates `DCI=2*ep+dir` against the 32-byte input/output context arrays — a device with bulk EP ≥ 4 IN would have written 32-byte contexts past `ictx`/`dctx` into `ep0_ring` and let the xHC DMA-read past the buffer on real hardware (QEMU's usb-storage uses EP1/EP2, so it never tripped); such devices are now logged and skipped. And the per-event serial trace that printed one line per CBW/data/CSW transfer on the **data path** (73 lines for a small copy — polled serial throttling real USB I/O) is now gated to bring-up/enumeration only: 16 trace lines at boot, 0 after `[XHCI] ready`. Verified: boot/app_smoke/doom/fuzz/thread/cond/usb suites all green after every stage; in-guest `smpstress`'s 8-child wave finished in **335 ms vs ~2.8 s at `-O0` (~8×)**; wall-clock TCG suites improved −2%…−16% (emulation-dominated — the desktop apps spend most of their time in syscalls and kernel rendering, so real-hardware wins are larger than TCG wall clocks suggest). `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.58. |
| v38.57 | **Merge Ring 3 scanout takeover (v38.53) + USB 3.0 (v38.56) + docs sync.** Reconciled the divergent `main` (local `v38.54..56` vs origin `v38.53` `SYS_FB_MAP`/`PAGE_DEV`): `loader.c` now carries both `trusted_shell` (v38.54) and `pgrp`/`fg` handoff (v38.53), `vmm.c` clone/free skips `PAGE_DEV`, `README` Testing/Project Structure/Arch diagram now list `ahci/usb/fbmap`, new `docs/drivers/ahci.md` + `fbmap.md`, `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.57. Build + `usb_test` + `fbmap_test` green. |
| v38.56 | **xHCI/USB 3.0 host + BOT mass-storage — SuperSpeed sticks become drives 8+ on the existing sector API.** The kernel now speaks USB 3.0 natively: a from-scratch xHCI driver (`src/drivers/xhci.c:1181`) enumerates `PCI 0C/03/30` (`qemu-xhci`, 64-bit BAR0 in the identity-mapped `0xFE000000..0xFF000000` window) and carries bulk-storage devices all the way to the VFS. Bring-up: halt+reset (HCRST), DCBAA (`MaxSlots+1`), command ring (CRCR, 256 TRBs + Link TC), event ring + ERST (interrupter 0, **poll model** — `IMAN.IE` stays off, completions found by watching the cycle bit, exactly like AHCI; no MSI/INTx), `MaxSlotsEn`, then `RUN`. Enumeration per port: PORTSC reset (preserve PP) → **Enable Slot** (slot id from completion) → **Address Device** (input context A0/A1, slot route=root port, EP0 `512` SS / `64` HS) → `GET_DESCRIPTOR` (Setup/Data/Status TRBs, IDT) → MSC BOT interface (`08/06/50`) → bulk EPs (`DCI=2*ep+dir`, type 2/6, `1024` SS / `512` HS) → `SET_CONFIGURATION` → **Configure Endpoint** (A0 + both bulk DCIs, `Context Entries = max DCI`). Data path: `CBW(31)` on bulk-OUT → data `Normal` TRB (DIR) → `CSW(13)` on bulk-IN, each `IOC` and awaited via `Transfer Event`, CSW sig/tag/status validated; `TEST UNIT READY` (retry + `REQUEST SENSE`), `INQUIRY` (36, type 0), `READ CAPACITY(10)` (512B), `READ10`/`WRITE10` (≤128 sectors via 64K-aligned bounce + `ata_batch_limit`). Integration: `kernel.c` `xhci_init` after `ahci_init`, `ata.c` routes `drive >= USB_DRIVE_BASE (8)` to `usb_read/write_sectors`, `vfs_mount.c` allows `8..11`, `run.sh` creates `usb.img` FAT32 + QEMU `-device qemu-xhci -device usb-storage,bus=xhci0.0` (SuperSpeed, `PORTSC speed 4`). Two bring-up bugs fixed: `xhci_cap8[8]` clobbered `evt_dequeue/cycle` with `"QEMU    "` on INQUIRY (36B into 8B) → `64B`, and `bmCBWFlags` was always `0x80` (IN) so `WRITE10` stalled → `is_in` param. CI: `scripts/usb_test.py` boots `qemu-xhci` + FAT32 stick, asserts `USB3 -> drive 8` + `[XHCI] ready`, `mount /usb fat32 8` (BPB via xHCI), `cp` inside volume + host `mtools` byte-compare. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.56. |
| v38.55 | **SMP VM-consistency: atomic COW resolution, stale-TLB guard, locked fork marking + waitpid reaping, dead-task waiter cleanup.** The kernel grew 4-core SMP on a single-lock design without cross-CPU VM primitives, and the review found the serious defects clustered where shared page tables meet concurrency — all fixed now. (1) **The COW fault handler holds `vmm_lock` across the entire check-through-PTE-update**: the old code took the lock only for the sole-owner *check*, so two cores faulting the same shared page interleaved between check and rewrite — both duplicated, the second PTE store clobbered the first thread's fresh mapping (silent data loss) and leaked a frame. The destination frame is allocated *before* the lock (frame_alloc takes vmm_lock itself), the PTE is re-read under it so a peer that resolved the fault first is detected (flush + resume instead of double-copy), and the old frame is freed after unlock. (2) **Stale-TLB guard in the #PF path**: a Ring 3 WRITE fault whose PTE is already PRESENT\|RW\|USER means a peer core promoted/duplicated the page and only this core's TLB is stale — it is resolved with one `invlpg` + resume; before, it fell through into `demand_map_zero`, which **replaced a live page with a zeroed frame** (sibling thread's data destroyed). (3) **fork's COW-marking loops take `vmm_lock`** — they mutate exactly the state (source RO+COW stores, refcount bumps) the fault handler mutates on other cores. (4) **waitpid reaps under `task_lock` with revalidation** — two sibling threads could double-reap one zombie (double FREE, `num_tasks` underflow, TLS cleared on a recycled slot); losing the race now restarts the scan. (5) **Dead tasks leave sem/futex waiter lists**: `wake_one` skips ZOMBIE/FREE entries (a wake spent on a corpse used to vanish), and teardown defers the list scrub via a pending bitmap drained by the BSP main loop — the eager version deadlocked `thread_test` because signal-driven teardown runs with `task_lock` held while sync paths order `sync_lock > task_lock` (classic AB-BA, both cores spinning IF=0). A full TLB-shootdown IPI remains documented follow-up: `schedule()` reloads CR3 unconditionally every switch, bounding stale-writable-TLB exposure to ≤2 ticks. Verified: fork/cond/thread/jobcontrol/mmapfile/app_smoke all pass under TCG *and* KVM. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.55. |
| v38.54 | **Four critical security/data-integrity fixes from the full-project audit.** (1) **fd `write()` no longer destroys files >4KB** — the descriptor write path spliced into a fixed `char whole[4096]` and rewrote the file from it, so any write past 4095 bytes silently truncated the file on ALL backends (a 1-byte append to a 100KB ext2 file erased 96KB); writes now read-modify-write through a right-sized heap buffer (`VFS_FD_MAX_FILE` = 8MB cap, POSIX short-write semantics), and `lseek()+read()` on `/ext2`/`/fat32` finally honours the offset via new range readers (`ext2_read_file_range` block-map walk with sparse holes as zeros, `fat32_read_file_range` cluster-chain skip). (2) **Shell-host privilege gates were forgeable** — SYS_EXEC_CMD/SYS_KILL_TASK/SYS_KILL compared the caller's launch_arg STRING against terminal/explorer/taskmgr, but any app can exec "taskmgr.mct" with arg "taskmgr.mct" (loader stores argv verbatim) and walk through into builtins that bypass VFS permission checks (delete/mount as non-root) and kill uid-0 tasks; trust is now the kernel-computed `trusted_shell` flag (resolved image identity + parent trust, recomputed on exec, inherited on fork), plus cross-uid/kernel-target kill denial for non-root. (3) **Deleting /etc/passwd re-armed the hardcoded default password** — root dirs are deliberately 0777 and delete authorization rides the parent's write bit, so unlinking the file made login fall back to "mectov123"; delete/rename gained uid-aware variants that refuse the protected path for non-root, and `passwd_ensure_initialized()` writes the default as a real salted-SHA-256 hash at first login so the plaintext fallback is unreachable. (4) **GDB stub stack overflow + Ring-3 debugger entry** — `m addr,400` wrote 2048 hex chars into `reply[512]` (~1.5KB of kernel stack smashed inside the trap ISR, no auth on COM2); count is bounded to the buffer, int1/int3 drop DPL 3→0 (any Ring 3 program could `int3` into the debugger loop), and a `nogdb` boot flag disables the stub entirely. Regression battery green: app_smoke under TCG *and* KVM, lseekfile, fat32 (LFN verified with mtools), hardening. |
| v38.53 | **KVM boot + app-launch fix — PAE PDPTEs may carry only the P bit.** Under `-enable-kvm` the OS reset instantly at paging enable, and after boot, launching any app panicked with #GP inside `vmm_switch_page_dir`; TCG was unaffected, which is why every CI harness passed. Root cause: this host's KVM validates PDPTE reserved bits when loading them (CR0.PG 0→1 and every CR3 reload) and treats R/W and U/S — architecturally "ignored" per SDM Table 4-7 — as reserved, raising #GP(0) for any present PDPTE carrying them. Every PDPTE write now carries ONLY PAGE_PRESENT: `paging_init`'s three boot-table sites, `space_pd()`, the USER widening in `space_pt()` (PD-level widening kept), and fork's parent-flag propagation. Permissions are unaffected — the CPU enforces R/W/U/S from the PD/PTE levels down. Diagnosed by bracketing `paging_init` with serial prints (fault pinned to the exact `mov %eax,%cr0`) then bisecting the PDPTE flags; verified end-to-end under KVM `-cpu host -smp 4` including the icon-double-click terminal launch that previously froze. |
| v38.53 | **Ring 3 scanout takeover — the display-server foundation.** A Ring 3 task can now own the actual framebuffer: **`SYS_FB_MAP` (118)** maps the VBE linear framebuffer read/write into the caller's address space and returns the geometry (`fb_info_t {base,width,height,pitch,bpp}`, new type in `types.h` — the common base of the kernel `task.h` and the Ring 3 ABI). The mapping is DEVICE memory: every PTE carries the new **`PAGE_DEV`** software flag (available bit 11, beside `PAGE_COW`/`PAGE_SHARED`), so `vmm_clone_address_space` skips it entirely (a child never inherits the scanout) and both address-space free walkers skip it — which also closes a latent bug: the clone walker's refcount bump was unbounded, so a fork after any MMIO mapping above the RAM ceiling would have written past `frame_ref_count[]` into kernel BSS. Pages map eagerly with RW+USER+NX (W^X: pixel data is never executable) into the caller's own page directory (kernel tasks with `page_dir==0` are refused — device pages must never enter the shared boot tables). Authorization is logind-style, not setuid-style: uid 0 or the controlling terminal's foreground process group (the active console session); background jobs are refused. While a live task holds the scanout, **the kernel main loop stops presenting frames** — no `full_redraw`, no cursor, no WM input processing; it keeps only non-pixel duties alive (keyboard pump to the holder, GDB stub, net poll, zombie reaping, auto-lock tick) so a takeover cannot starve them. Keystrokes route to the holder through the same queue `SYS_GET_KEY` pops (the gate now accepts the scanout owner alongside the terminal's foreground app); raw mouse state was already global via `SYS_GET_MOUSE`. **`SYS_FB_RELEASE` (119)** hands the pixels back (owner-only); exit/kill drop ownership through a `task_cleanup` hook and exec discards it with the region table, each restoring the desktop on the next tick. The spawn/handoff race closed along the way: pgrp + foreground handoff moved INSIDE the loader's interrupts-disabled window (`load_mct_app_fg`) — previously an app could run on another core and hit an authorization check before `cmd_run` finished handing over the terminal. `apps/fbmap.mct` animates a gradient + bouncing box straight to the scanout, drains queued keys, then chases the mouse until any key releases; `scripts/fbmap_test.py` (wired into CI) proves scenario A (grant → interactive takeover → typed key routed through the pump → clean release → `[FBMAP] desktop restored`) AND scenario B (relaunch, Ctrl+C mid-takeover → ownership drops via cleanup → desktop restored, zero `[PANIC]`). All 11 local regressions green. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.53. |
| v38.52 | **Kernel hardening: CSPRNG, unpredictable TCP ISNs, salted-SHA-256 passwords.** The kernel now carries a real entropy pool + ChaCha8 DRBG (`src/sys/entropy.c`): `entropy_init()` seeds at boot (TSC jitter + kernel-address mixing) and `entropy_add()` feeds it continuously from the PIT timer IRQ, every PS/2 keyboard byte and every mouse byte, re-keying the DRBG every 8 samples. `/dev/random` stopped being a fixed-seed LCG (`vfs.c` now returns `get_random_bytes()`), TCP ISNs for both the client (`net.c` connect path) and the SYN-ACK (listen path) come from the CSPRNG with a `get_ticks` fallback only for the pre-seed microseconds, and a new `SYS_GETRANDOM` (117) exposes the DRBG directly to Ring 3 (POSIX `getrandom(, , 0)` shape). Passwords are no longer stored in plaintext: `passwd` writes `/etc/passwd` as `<8-byte-salt-hex>:<sha256(salt_hex‖pw) hex>` (new `src/sys/sha256.c` + `src/sys/passwd.c` rewrite); login and the `passwd` command verify through `sys_verify_password()`, which still accepts a legacy plaintext file until the next password change. `scripts/hardening_test.py` (wired into CI) boots, runs `passwd mectov123 hunter2`, proves the NEW password unlocks the lock screen while the OLD one is rejected (a plaintext file would accept both), then `apps/hardening_test.mct` proves from Ring 3 that `SYS_GETRANDOM` returns fresh non-zero bytes, two `/dev/random` reads differ, and the passwd file is 81 chars of `salt:hash` with no plaintext inside. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.52. |
| v38.51 | **Idle auto-lock (`locktimeout <seconds>`)** — the kernel now tracks the last keyboard/mouse input (`security.c`: `last_input_tick`, bumped by the main loop's mouse and keyboard hooks) and, when armed, raises the same `pending_lock` flag as the `lock` builtin once input has been quiet for the timeout (checked at 1 s granularity next to the clock tick). The `locktimeout` shell builtin sets it (0 disables, no-arg prints the current value, capped at 24 h); after unlocking, the countdown is re-anchored so the desktop does not immediately re-lock. `scripts/autolock_test.py` (wired into CI) arms 20 s, goes quiet and watches the login gate appear on its own, unlocks (session preserved, no re-lock), then disables and confirms a longer quiet window passes with no lock. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.51. |
| v38.50 | **AHCI/SATA driver — SATA disks become drives 4+ on the existing sector API.** The controller is found on PCI (class 01/06/01), BAR5 mapped inside a widened identity PCI MMIO window (`mem.c`: the two APIC-only PTs became 8 strongly-uncached PTs covering `0xFE000000..0xFF000000`); ports with a plugged ATA disk register as drives `4..7`, so ext2/FAT32 and the runtime `mount()` work on SATA volumes with zero changes to the filesystem layers. Transfers use command slot 0 + a single PRD into a 64K-aligned bounce buffer, LBA48 READ/WRITE DMA EXT (`0x25`/`0x35`), completion by polling PxCI under `ahci_lock` with IRQs off (mirrors the IDE DMA model). Two real bugs found while bringing it up: the H2D FIS fields were written one byte off the spec layout (count landed in the LBA slot, device in LBA 31:24, and the real count field stayed 0 → the device read a 64K-sector window at LBA 0x40000001 and returned zeros), and `vfs_is_file()` never accepted `FS_FAT32_FILE`, so every file op on a mounted FAT32 volume reported "source not found" (v38.42 mount gap). Also fixed: port slots now remember their physical port (non-contiguous port maps broke reads), the BAR page keeps the window's PCD\|PWT uncached flags, and ATAPI (optical) signatures are skipped. CI: `scripts/ahci_test.py` boots with `ich9-ahci` + a FAT32 SATA disk, mounts it (BPB read through AHCI), copies a file inside the volume (read + write), and byte-compares it on the host with mtools. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.50. |
| v38.49 | **PAE 3-level paging + EFER.NXE with W^X — user heap, stack and anonymous mmap pages are non-executable, and executing data kills the task.** The whole paging layer moved from 2-level (1024-entry, 32-bit PTE) to PAE (PDPT 4×512MB → PD 512×2MB → PT 512×4KB, 64-bit `pte_t` entries): `paging_init` builds static `boot_pdpt`/`boot_pds` + 256×2MB identity PTs and sets CR4.PAE before CR0.PG; the SMP trampoline sets CR4.PAE per-AP and every AP repeats the EFER.NXE MSR write (first `rdmsr`/`wrmsr` helpers in the tree, CPUID-gated with a clean "NX unavailable" fallback). All page-table walkers migrated (COW handler, heap/stack demand fill, guard pages, mmap fault/munmap, `user_page_ok`, `SYS_VMM_FREE`, SysV shm, `vmm_create/clone/free/map/unmap`), and `vmm_map_page` takes `uint64_t` flags so `PAGE_NX` survives. W^X policy: demand-zero heap/stack pages and anonymous mmap pages map with PAGE_NX; images and the signal trampoline stay executable. A page fault with the I/D bit (instruction fetch) is **never demand-mapped** — it logs `[W^X] execute fault` and delivers SIGSEGV (Ring 3) or panics (Ring 0); before this, a fetch into unmapped heap got a zero page mapped and *executed*. `apps/nxtest.mct` places a `ret` on its stack and calls it — CI (`scripts/nxtest.py`) asserts the kill. Migration also uncovered and fixed a real fork leak: the old clone re-allocated a PT for every kernel-region PDE and overwrote the copy `vmm_create_address_space` had just made (~120 frames ≈ 0.5 MB leaked per fork); the PAE clone COW-copies only the USER ptes inside kernel tables and reuses the create-time copies. All QEMU harnesses now pass `-cpu qemu32,+nx` (the default qemu32 model lacks the NX CPUID bit). 11/11 local regressions pass on the PAE kernel (boot/fork/doom/panic/fat32/thread/mmapfile/mount/poweroff/fputest/socktest) + nxtest. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.49. |
| v38.48 | **O(1) used-frame accounting + dead code removal.** `phys_get_used_pages()` used to scan all 131,072 `frame_ref_count[]` entries under `vmm_lock` with IRQs off every time `/proc/meminfo` or `get_sysinfo` was read; it is now a maintained counter (`phys_used_count`, updated at the five bitmap-mutation sites — `phys_init`, `phys_reserve_region`, `frame_alloc`, `frame_free`; direct refcount bumps from COW/shm/fork correctly leave it alone since they never move a frame between free and used) read lock-free. Verified live: `MemUsed 48244 kB` on a fresh boot = 48 MB kernel reservation + pinned zero page + runtime allocations. Also deleted `vmm_find_free_region()` — a never-rewinding bump allocator that looked like a VA-space leak but turned out to have **zero callers** (real mmap placement already uses the first-fit gap search in `mmap_region_alloc`). `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.48. |
| v38.47 | **ATA DMA bounce buffer preallocated + memcpy.** The bus-mastering DMA path kmalloc'd a bounce buffer per I/O and copied up to 64 KB **byte-by-byte** in both directions; it is now a static 64K-aligned 64 KB buffer with plain `memcpy` (both copies stay under `ata_lock` since the buffer is shared across CPUs). Being 64K-aligned also guarantees a ≤64K transfer never crosses a 64K PRDT boundary, so the descriptor table is always a single region. `bigread_test` asserts DMA engaged with zero failures; FAT32 write regression green. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.47. |
| v38.46 | **Flat desktop icon restyle — saturated flat squircles, no shadows.** The icon tiles dropped the old muted fills for a modern flat palette (near-black Terminal, blue Explorer, amber Browser, sky PCI, green Snake, violet Calc, fuchsia ELF…), the Terminal prompt is neon green, and Notepad finally gets a real page glyph with rule lines (its old white-on-light-gray glyph was nearly invisible). Labels stay plain white text with a 1px shadow. Geometry untouched — grid, hit boxes, drag/double-click and `icons.cfg` all identical, so every CI harness that double-clicks the Terminal icon at (60,64) keeps working. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.46. |
| v38.45 | **ACPI S5 poweroff from the firmware's own tables.** `acpi_init` now records the **FADT** in addition to the MADT (both the XSDT and legacy RSDT paths scan every entry — the old early-return after the MADT could miss the FADT depending on table order), parses the PM1a/b control-block addresses, and walks the DSDT AML for the `\_S5` package to get the real SLP_TYP values. `shutdown()` writes PM1a/PM1b with SLP_EN (bit 13) + SLP_TYP (bits 12:10) before falling back to the legacy hardcoded ports — under QEMU the VM now powers off through the spec path. `scripts/poweroff_test.py` (wired into CI) types `shutdown` and asserts the **QEMU process exits by itself**. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.45. |
| v38.44 | **Capacity bumps: TCP connections 8→16, global fd table 128→256, windows 16→32.** Socket-per-connection servers and window-heavy sessions were the pressure points. `MAX_TASKS` (GDT TLS coupling) and `MAX_NODES` (on-disk MECTOVFS layout) deliberately unchanged, documented in code. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.44. |
| v38.43 | **POSIX socket API, fd-integrated — `socket`/`bind`/`listen`/`accept`/`connect`/`sendto`/`recvfrom` (syscalls 108–114).** `socket()` and `accept()` return ordinary file descriptors, so `read`/`write`/`close`/`poll`/`select` are socket-aware. The listener model is now POSIX-shaped: a LISTEN slot **never mutates into a connection** — each inbound SYN spawns a fresh child slot (`listen_parent` link, free slots = the backlog) that completes the handshake on its own, and `accept()` hands out ESTABLISHED children one per call (POLLIN on a listener means "accept would return"). `connect()` returns once the SYN is out; POLLOUT means established. UDP datagram sockets use the stack's single global binding with `net_udp_peer()` feeding `recvfrom` source addresses. `apps/tcpserver.mct` exercises both directions (client echo against a host server at 10.0.2.2:9999, server accept through QEMU hostfwd with PING→PONG); `scripts/socktest.py` (wired into CI) drives it live with a Python echo server on the host. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.43. |
| v38.42 | **Runtime mount table — `SYS_MOUNT` (115) / `SYS_UMOUNT` (116) + `mount`/`umount` shell builtins.** A mount point is an empty VFS directory node retyped to the backend's `FS_*_DIR` (the existing type dispatch then routes I/O to the real filesystem); the new `src/sys/vfs_mount.c` records which drive + root key backs each mount so umount can undo it. The boot mounts (`/ext2` drive 1, `/fat32` drive 3) register through the same table in both `vfs_init` paths. `umount` drops the subtree (disk data untouched), restores a plain directory and persists the node table; `mount` with no args lists active mounts. Root-only at the syscall layer; documented limitation: the ext2/FAT32 backends keep one global superblock each, so a runtime mount targets the boot drives (or a remount). `scripts/mount_test.py` (wired into CI) does the full roundtrip: umount `/fat32` → `umount /apps` refused → remount → fat32demo passes on the remounted filesystem. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.42. |
| v38.41 | **Eager FPU/SSE context switching — DOOM and Ring 3 float code can finally be preempted against each other.** Until now the scheduler never touched the FPU: the kernel core is soft-float and DOOM was treated as the system's ONLY x87 user (it runs inside the shell's task in kernel mode), so its state survived preemption purely because nothing else emitted FPU instructions — any second FPU user would silently corrupt it. Every `task_t` now carries a 16-byte-aligned 512-byte `fxsave` image (`src/sys/fpu.c`), and `schedule()` swaps it eagerly (fxsave out / fxrstor in) with IF=0 under `task_lock` — SMP-safe with no per-CPU owner tracking. `fpu_init_cpu()` (BSP in `kernel_main`, every AP in `ap_main` — CR4 is per-CPU) sets CR0 EM=0/MP=1/TS=0 and CR4 OSFXSR\|OSXMMEXCPT behind a CPUID check with a no-op fallback, and builds the canonical `fninit` clean-state template. Lifecycle: task creation seeds the clean image, `fork()` inherits the parent's live state (flushed first — POSIX), `exec()` resets to clean. #NM is a logged safety net (the eager scheme never sets TS); #XM from Ring 3 delivers the new `SIGFPE` (8). `apps/fputest.mct` proves the exact scenario the old design could not survive: two forked processes accumulating in the live x87 register stack AND an SSE register across preemptions and across fork — both sums exact; `scripts/fputest.py` (wired into CI, also under KVM) asserts it. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.41. |
| v38.40 | **Start menu search-as-you-type — open the menu and just type to filter the 12 items.** While `start_menu_open` is set, the kernel routes printable keys, Backspace, Escape and Enter to the taskbar instead of the focused window. `taskbar.c` keeps a small query buffer and a case-insensitive substring filter over the menu labels (hoisted into a shared `sm_labels[]` so draw, hover and click all key off the ORIGINAL index through the filtered list): the query renders live in amber in the menu header ("Search: …"), only matching items are drawn (starting at the same first row, no gaps), "No results" appears for a miss, Backspace pops the query, Escape clears the query then closes the menu, and Enter launches the hovered (or first) filtered item. The filter is a real substring match — typing "lock" shows **Clock and Lock** ("Clock" contains "lock"). Hover and click hit-tests use the same filtered list, so a filtered menu is fully clickable. New `scripts/startmenu_search_test.py` (wired into CI) boots once and drives it all live: "Snake" narrows to one row and Enter launches the game (loader log grows), "zzz" shows "No results", backspace x3 restores all items, "lock" narrows to the two matching rows, and Escape clears then closes. Also fixes a hover glitch exposed by the menu work: `taskbar_draw` early-returned when the dirty rect stayed **above** the taskbar strip, so moving the mouse inside the Start menu never repainted the hover highlight — it stuck on the previous row. When a popup (Start menu/calendar/volume) is open, the early-return now also accounts for the popup's bounds, so the highlight follows the mouse (new "in-menu move" check in `scripts/taskbar_hover_test.py`). `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.40. |
| v38.39 | **Taskbar window buttons feel complete — hover feedback is wired up (it was dead code), and minimize/restore from the taskbar is verified end to end.** Clicking a focused window's taskbar button minimizes it and clicking again restores it (the click handler already existed; it is now covered by CI). The hover system was never actually running: `taskbar_track_mouse` was declared and defined but **never called** from the kernel loop (unlike `wm_track_mouse`), and even had it been, hover changes never requested a redraw — pure mouse moves only blit the back buffer, so highlights appeared only when some unrelated event (e.g. the 1 s clock tick) happened to trigger a full redraw. The kernel now calls `taskbar_track_mouse` on every move, and the function computes the desired hover state from scratch and sets `needs_redraw` only when it changed. Hovering a **non-focused** window button turns its title amber (`TB_ACTIVE`, echoing the Start-menu selection accent), the focused button stays plain, the START label highlights on hover, and the Start menu's amber selection bar now actually appears over items (the same dead system). New `scripts/taskbar_hover_test.py` (wired into CI) drives it live: minimize/restore a terminal via its button (pixel-compared region), hover the non-focused button with two windows open, verify the focused one stays plain, verify the highlight clears on move-away, and check the Start menu item bar. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.39. |
| v38.38 | **waitpid lost-wakeup fix — a parent could sleep forever even after its child became a zombie.** `task_waitpid` scanned for a zombie and then parked (set `waiting`, `TASK_STATE_BLOCKED`, `rq_remove`) without re-checking under the lock, while `terminate_task` only wakes the parent if `waiting != 0` — so a child exiting in the window between the scan and the park was turned into a reaped-able zombie with nobody woken, and the parent `hlt`-parked permanently (no timeout, unlike `task_sleep`). This latent race (in place since v36.0) intermittently hung `rlimittest`'s `fork`+`waitpid` — surfacing as flaky CI failures on `scripts/rlimit_test.py` and `scripts/ulimit_test.py` ("rlimittest did not pass after ulimit changes", `[RLIM] start` logged but no `ALL PASS`). The park now re-checks for a zombie **under `task_lock`** (the same lock the exit path holds, so the check cannot race it): if one appeared, the park is undone (state back to READY, still current on its core — the scheduler's keep-current path re-enqueues it if a tick preempts first) and the loop reaps it on the next pass. Verified: `ulimit_test` 4/4, `rlimit_test` 3/3 (both previously failing intermittently), plus `fork`/`jobcontrol`/`thread`/`app_smoke` regressions all pass. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.38. |
| v38.37 | **Full-black theme — the warm/orange tint is gone from the taskbar AND the window chrome.** The Instrument-Console taskbar used a warm charcoal face (`TB_BG`/`RETRO_FACE` 0x16130F) with an amber-brown bevel highlight (0x3C2E18) and an amber logo square on the Start button, and focused windows carried an amber hairline border with amber control glyphs — together they read as "orange/yellow" across the desktop. Taskbar surfaces are now **pure black** (bevels neutralized to subtle grays; these constants are only consumed by taskbar bevels) and the Start button is a centered off-white **"START"** label with no logo. Window chrome (ToaruOS-style) is black too: `TOARU_TITLE`/`TOARU_BORDER` pure black (no more yellow line around the focused window), title text and the close/min/max glyphs off-white. Amber survives only as selection feedback in the Start menu/calendar popups. `scripts/lock_test.py`'s taskbar detector now looks for near-black instead of the old 0x16130F charcoal. Verified pixel-wise: all four window border edges black, **zero amber pixels** in the chrome envelope, off-white title + buttons legible, Start menu black with all 12 items. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.37. |
| v38.36 | **DOOM windowed fix — the game was opening an empty window because the login gate hogged the kernel heap.** After the v38.30 redesign, `gui_login()` cached three framebuffer-sized buffers (scaled wallpaper, blurred wallpaper, transition snapshot — ~12 MB) and never released them, so a later `doom` launch failed in `Z_Init` with "Unable to allocate 5 MiB of RAM for zone" — the WM window opened but the game loop exited before rendering a frame. A new `free_login_buffers()` (login.c) returns all three to the 24 MB kernel heap the moment the gate closes (both Enter and Sign-In paths); each rebuilds lazily on the next login. Verified: `[DOOM] Entering game loop...` + tick lines after a normal login, window content pixel-checked (game visible, not a blank window), and the lock/login suites still pass. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.36. |
| v38.35 | **Lock-screen date fix — the day/date/month/year under the clock is back.** `gui_login()` cleared `date_str` on entry (every boot, logout and lock), but `refresh_clock()` only rebuilt it when the RTC day changed — and its `last_day` static survives across calls, so a second visit to the login gate (lock, logout) left the string empty and the date invisible while the clock kept ticking. The rebuild now also triggers when `date_str` is empty (same guard added for the clock string), so the full date renders on every lock — verified pixel-wise (off-white glyphs below the amber clock, readable "Saturday, 15 August 2026"). `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.35. |
| v38.34 | **Lock screen feature — lock the desktop without ending the session.** A **Lock** item in the Start menu (padlock glyph, 12-item menu), the **Ctrl+Alt+L** shortcut (consumed in the kernel so it never types an `l`), and the `lock` shell builtin all funnel into a new `pending_lock` flag (`security.c`; the old text-mode stub printed "System Locked. Please enter password." into the console and never verified anything — `is_locked` was set and never read, so it's replaced). The kernel main loop (kernel.c) notices the flag, runs the existing graphical login gate `gui_login()` **without** `wm_reset_session()` — every window, terminal and running task stays alive behind the lock — and on unlock `full_redraw()` restores the desktop. `scripts/lock_test.py` (wired into CI) boots, logs in, launches the Terminal, locks via the `lock` command, unlocks and pixel-compares the terminal window pre/post (session preserved, diff 0.1), then locks again via Ctrl+Alt+L. Start-menu geometry now comes from `START_MENU_H`/`START_MENU_ITEMS` in `taskbar.h` (shared by draw, hit-test and kernel popup-dismiss). `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.34. |
| v38.33 | **The lock screen is now completely bare — wallpaper and clock only.** The "press SPACE to sign in" hint and the "v38.xx SMP MECTOVFS 1024x768" version footer are gone (`draw_footer` and the hint in `draw_lock_screen` removed, along with the now-unused `str_advance`), leaving the full-bleed wallpaper and the bottom-left clock + date as the only elements. SPACE (or a click) still opens the password entry and the 4-second idle fallback is unchanged. `scripts/login_redesign_test.py` now asserts the two bottom-center bands (hint y and footer y) carry zero dim-gray text pixels on the lock screen. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.33. |
| v38.32 | **Animated screen transitions — the lock and password screens cross-fade into each other.** Pressing SPACE no longer swaps the screen instantly: the current frame is snapshotted into a 4 MB `prev_frame` buffer (allocated lazily, `kmalloc`) and the destination screen (blurred wallpaper + panel, or sharp wallpaper + clock/hint/footer) is rendered underneath, then a fixed-point lerp pass (`blend_transition`, t in 0..256, no floats in the kernel) blends the two — so the open animation *is* the blur animation (sharp wallpaper eases into the 5-tap blur), the caption fade-out, and the panel fade-in all at once, ~0.45 s of guest time per direction (smooth under KVM, progressively slower but fluid under TCG). The idle 4-second revert now plays the same fade in reverse (blur eases out, clock/hint fade back in, panel fades away), and the gate state (input, error, shake) resets only when the fade completes. Input stays honest during the animation: keys typed while the panel is opening are accepted normally, and any key (or click) during the *closing* fade cancels it and brings the panel back with the password intact, restarting the 4 s clock. If the 4 MB snapshot allocation fails the switch falls back to the old instant behavior. `scripts/login_redesign_test.py` now waits for the fade to settle (blur bar) before the content checks and retries through the closing fade for the revert + clock-returned assertions. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.32. |
| v38.31 | **Password entry gets full focus — blurred wallpaper, no clock, no footer.** While the login panel is open, the screen now does everything to make you type the password and nothing else: the wallpaper is run through a cached **5-tap separable box blur** (two sliding-window passes, O(n) with row-major access, so the build is instant on real hardware and still fast under TCG) and the bottom-left clock/date and the version footer are **not drawn** on this screen — they only live on the lock screen now. The 4-second idle fallback is measured in **RTC (wall-clock) seconds** — PIT ticks drift against the wall clock under QEMU TCG (a raw 4000-tick window collapsed to ~1 real second there), and the anchor is rounded up to the next whole second so the window is a genuine ≥4 s on KVM, TCG and hardware alike. The countdown only starts once the panel has actually rendered: building the blur must not count as "idle", which previously made the panel flash away before it ever showed. `scripts/login_redesign_test.py` now also asserts the panel *stays* on screen, the clock is gone, the footer is gone, and the wallpaper sharpness (mean horizontal neighbor-difference) drops by at least 45% on the password screen. The lock screen is now branding-free — the old "MECTOV OS" wordmark is gone, leaving wallpaper + clock + hint. `OS_VERSION` bumped to 38.31. |
